CONTENDED term) and the crawl's "138 ms" (parses are fully content-cached). **(INC.7) batches 1-2**
then gated **23** pure-emitter tail walkers onto the partition — a strict no-op on every full
build, so no corpus baseline moved — taking the floor **1,207 -> ~790-910 ms**. Such a gate
banks only part of its row, because the work relocates onto whoever asks next; measured twice,
that discount is **79.0% then 85.5%**, shrinking as predicted since the relocation lands on
walkers that are themselves later gated. ~350 walkers and ~470 ms remain, priced.

**AND A REFUSAL FOUND A REAL DEFECT.** Narrowing the CAPTURE queries (hover, completion,
go-to-definition, signature help) was measured at **3.7x** and **refused**: 45 of **381,666**
captured spans rendered a different type. The rows named the cause themselves — one lost a
KEYWORD type, one a mapped-type `-?` modifier, which names cannot do and a lazy member table
can — and in 5 of the 45 it was the WHOLE-PROGRAM arm rendering `any`, so both arms were draws
from an order-dependent cache. **(INC.5)** fixed it at the five capture render sites (never
inside `typeToString`, the diagnostic renderer, which would have put ~13k baselines in play),
taking 45 divergent spans to **9**. **(INC.6)** then closed the user-visible remainder: a
`Readonly<T>` member's copy Symbol now has its type written AT MINT TIME, because the only
other writer — `getTypeOfSymbol` — is gated on round 778's EMPTY instantiation context and so
refuses inside every `namespace` body, throwing a successful resolution away and printing `any`.
**9 -> 5 divergent spans, and the class where a narrowed hover renders more `any` than the full
build is now ZERO.** The 5 that remain are display-only and in 4 of them the NARROWED arm is the
better answer (an alias name vs its expanded body; a generic member's `T` the full build froze as
`any`). **THAT IS WHAT LET (INC.2b) LAND — and landing it needed a SECOND gate, because the
first covered two of the five capture channels.** `scripts/capture-channel-equivalence.sh`
sweeps members, scopes and signatures; scopes agree everywhere (0 of 8,986) and the other two
diverge in **286 rows of 21,507**, which the runner's own census resolves into FIVE display
mechanisms rather than 286 findings — a row there is a LIST, so one mechanism reaches every
caret on that receiver. 116 rows are the narrowed arm rendering the alias tsc renders against
the full arm's expanded body with `| undefined` DOUBLED; 167 are a member's type parameter
printing `<K extends any>` against `<K>`, where NEITHER arm renders the declared constraint and
both are wrong alike; **one** signature parameter renders `any` under the narrowed arm, and that
single row is the whole user-visible cost. `narrowRendersMoreAny = 168` is reported and is not
168 wrong types — 167 are that `extends any` spelling, which a substring classifier cannot tell
from a type, and a round that read the flag without the census would have refused this on a
false reading. Suite 15,648 / 0 / 3.

**SIX CLI LIBRARIES SCREENED AND THEIR 126 FALSE POSITIVES ROOT-CAUSED INTO FIVE FAMILIES
(2026-08-22).** With `@types/node` present and each library's own tsconfig, diffed against tsgo
7.0.2 per (file, line, code): **cronstrue 0/0** — the first library outside the corpus where this
checker agrees with tsgo exactly, 8,812 lines — marked 0/15, jsonrepair 1/16, fflate 2/17,
yaml 0/78. **The largest family is not a type-system defect at all: `// @ts-ignore` and
`// @ts-expect-error` suppress NOTHING**, in both directions (an unused `@ts-expect-error` also
fails to produce TS2578), and the feature *looks* implemented — both spellings are parsed as
directives and one is consulted for a narrow commonjs case. It is all 9 of fflate's TS2391 rows,
matching its 9 `@ts-ignore` comments exactly. The others: a primitive is not related to a
structural object target through its apparent type (`string` vs `interface Text`, 13 rows); a
destructuring parameter breaks arity and prints the inverted `Expected 1-0 arguments, but got 1`
(8 rows — round 921's recorded `getParameterSymbols` hazard reaching a diagnostic at last);
`isolatedDeclarations` over-reports 32 rows on a library that ships with the flag on and is clean
under tsgo; and a function expression assigned through an index signature gets no contextual
signature. ~59 rows remain untriaged and the entries say so. **cronstrue is queued as the next
backend driver** — its lowering runs to a first refusal and five named rungs separate it from a
running program — while **marked is the benchmark goal** and **fflate is structurally blocked**
by 183 typed-array uses against a runtime with none. Queued (LIB.3)/(LIB.4)/(CHK.31)-(CHK.35);
no code landed.

Compiler profile UNCHANGED (190) but the fixes GENERALIZE hugely: services 591 → 498 (−93), server 887 → 733 (−154), harness 1,118 → 989 (−129); suite 9,504 → 9,512 (+8 local across 3 test files, 0 regressions); 4 fix commits; services diffed via --listAll as strictly by-position removals. (1) TS2664 10 → 0: `resolveModuleSpecifierRelativeJsAware` — a `declare module "../compiler/types.js"` augmentation resolves `.js` → the `.ts` sibling (the base resolver deliberately avoids `.js` per the TS2459 gotcha, so the augmentation target never resolved); purely additive → only SUPPRESSES a false 'cannot be found'. (2) TS2564 17 → 0: a class property whose declared type INCLUDES `undefined` needs no definite assignment (tsc's strictPropertyInitialization `getFalsyFlags(type) & TypeFlags.Undefined`); `checkClassPropertyInit` missed the `| undefined` case, so `SourceFileObject`'s `nameTable: Map<...> | undefined` + siblings FP'd; reuses the existing `typeIncludesUndefined`. (3) TS2304 24 → 2: `buildNamespaceScope` gained a StringLiteralNode branch — a `declare module "X"` augmentation body sees the AUGMENTED module's exports by bare name (Node/NodeArray/SymbolFlags/TypeChecker/__String — compiler/types.ts exports NOT imported into services/types.ts); the 2 remaining are NodeJS `global` (env-legit, offline). INVESTIGATED & REVERTED (dashboard no-op): TS7006 array-element contextual typing — a correct M3.2 enabler (3 passing tests) but the real inferFromUsage.ts `Priority[]` case is B83.5-blocked (`interface Priority` nested in a function → unbound → `Priority[]` resolves to any); land it with nested-type resolution. (4) TS2339 44 → 0 (SourceFileLike, Blocker #3): a module-file-local `type X = A | B` alias leaks into globals and shadows the global `interface X` in OTHER files (TYPE-space analog of round 442's variable leak). importTracker.ts's non-exported `type SourceFileLike = SourceFile | AmbientModuleDeclaration` won the last-wins merge over compiler/types.ts's `interface SourceFileLike`, so a `sourceFile: SourceFileLike` param resolved to the bogus union (AmbientModuleDeclaration has no `.text`); pinned via an instrumented probe (whole-program-only — minimal repros don't reproduce). Built `conflatedTypeAliasFiles`; checkMemberAccessMissing bails a union-receiver TS2339 when the receiver displays as a conflated name in a NON-alias file (own-file still fires). **THE INCREMENTAL FLOOR IS DOWN TO 514 ms AND A NARROWED ERROR QUERY TO 542 — 68 TAIL
WALKERS NOW RUN ONLY OVER THE FILES THEY WERE ASKED ABOUT (2026-08-22, (INC.7) batches
1-3).** 376 of the ~400 program-wide tail walkers iterated `binderResults`, so they cost the
same whether the checker checks 78 files or none — 66% of the floor. Moving a walker's loop
onto `checkedResults` is a **strict no-op for every full build and therefore for the whole
corpus** (`checkedResults` IS `binderResults` when `assignedFileNames == null`), so it can
only change what a PARTITION does; and a partition reports nothing about files it was not
asked about, so a pure EMITTER skipping them loses nothing observable. Batch 3 gated **45**

_**Round 948 (2026-08-19) — (CHK.25): `using` / `await using` DECLARATIONS DID NOT PARSE AT ALL, AND THAT WAS THE LARGEST SINGLE CASCADE IN THE WHOLE PRISTINE POPULATION — 33 OURS-ONLY ROWS OVER FOUR FIXTURES._ `using x = expr;` reported TS1434 at the `using` and then TS2304 for every name the failed statement never bound. **The representation is tsc's own and needed no new node**: a `VariableDeclarationList`'s `flags` field already IS the head token, so `using` is `SyntaxKind.UsingKeyword` — no `forEachChild` arm, no `NodeKind`, no binder arm, because the binder's `isVar` test already reads any non-`var` head as block-scoped (measured: a `using` declared inside a block is invisible after it, where a `var` in the same position hoists). `await using` is two tokens collapsed onto a synthetic `SyntaxKind.AwaitUsingKeyword` the scanner never produces. **THE WHOLE RISK WAS THE CONTEXTUAL KEYWORD AND IT DID NOT MATERIALISE ANYWHERE** — the eight profiles carry **336** occurrences of `using` as an identifier / property name and **zero** declarations, and the binary grid is byte-identical on all eight; what makes the arm additive is tsc's lookahead (a binding identifier or `{` on the SAME LINE, with `[` deliberately refused so `using[x]` stays an element access) plus the for-head's `disallowOf`. **A TYPESCRIPT LIBRARY'S PUBLIC API NOW EXPORTS AS A KOTLIN METADATA KLIB (2026-08-22, owner
directive).** `exportTypeScriptProjectApi(project, entry, out.klib)` writes the artifact a Kotlin
Multiplatform `commonMain` compiles against: the library's exported declarations, typed by the
TypeScript checker's own answers. The route is generated Kotlin SOURCE through kotlinc's own
metadata compiler — Kotlin metadata is a versioned protobuf whose only writer lives in the
compiler, so the artifact is by construction what kotlinc would have written, and
`KotlinMetadataExport.source` is a readable intermediate. The surface is the ENTRY MODULE's
exports followed through `export {x} from` / `export *` / `export default`, not the union of
everything every file marks `export`. **Verification is by a CONSUMER and only by a consumer** —
each end-to-end pin compiles Kotlin against the artifact through the same metadata compiler, with
four negative controls, because a round trip that passes because the consumer compiles whatever it
is given would pass for an empty klib: a non-exported name must not resolve, the erased parameter
types must be ENFORCED, a non-re-exported module must not be reachable, and a program the checker
rejects must produce no artifact. Measured on the two real libraries this backend already compiles:
`mitt` exports `mitt(all: Any?): Any?`, `smol-toml` exports `parse(toml: String, options: Any?):
Any?` — primitives, unions, optionality, classes, enums and callbacks reach the surface typed.
**(KAPI.3) LANDED IN THE SAME SESSION and is what makes an exported library usable**: with
`runtimeKlib =` the export writes a SECOND metadata klib declaring `JsObject`/`JsArray` under
their real fully qualified names and compiles the library against it, so the same two libraries
export **`mitt(all: JsObject?): JsObject`** and **`parse(toml: String, options: JsObject?):
JsObject`** — measured on `smol-toml`'s own 1,082 lines — and a Kotlin consumer reads
`document.get("title")`. A bag needs POSITIVE evidence (the lowering's own gate: a structural
kind declared in a program file, or an anonymous object type), because a `Date` is a `JsDate` at
run time and a bag-typed one would offer members the value does not have; an intersection is a
bag only when EVERY member is one, stricter than `ErasedTypes` and forced by having no
library-type table. **(KAPI.4)** then made the erasure precise rather than merely non-`Any?`: `KirRuntimeApi.libraryType`
mirrors `KirIntrinsics.libraryClass`, so `Map`/`Set`/`RegExp`/`Date`/`Error` name the same runtime
class on an exported signature as in the compiled program — `mitt`'s parameter is **`JsMap?`** — and
it found the gate's own defect on the way: **an absent declaration is not evidence of an anonymous
shape** (a `Type.Reference`'s own symbol carries none, its TARGET's does), which had read
`Promise<string>` as a property bag. The hand-stated runtime facade is kept honest by a REFLECTION
pin over the real classes — members and constructors — with three negative controls proving it can
fail. Refusals are PER DECLARATION, not
per program: an absent declaration is a compile error at the consumer's use site, a wrongly-typed
one is silent. Suite **15,601 / 0 failures**; the KIR module 108 → 146 pins.
`docs/kir-kotlin-metadata.md`.

**THE KIR QUEUE, ALL FIVE ITEMS CLOSED (2026-08-21).** `smol-toml` on the JVM is **47.05 → 34.10 us/parse, −27.5%**, and 2.08x
slower than Node down to **1.52x**; on Kotlin/Native **163.30 → 126.55, −22.5%** and 7.26x
→ **5.70x**. `mitt` is flat on both (61.25 ns/emit, 1.35x FASTER than Node; native 354.75),
which is the control that says the lever is the one claimed. **(KIR.PERF.2)** is a matcher
for the REGULAR subset — parse, Thompson NFA, lazy DFA — answering `test` and nothing else,
REFUSING everything outside the subset, with `java.util.regex` kept live as the differential
oracle: 16.7x / 13.0x / 3.0x / 3.2x on the four patterns. It found a divergence in the OTHER
engine on the way (Java's `$` matches before a final line terminator, JavaScript's does not).
**(KIR.EMIT.1)** implements `rewriteRelativeImportExtensions`, so our ESM output loads on
Node as emitted; **(KIR.EMIT.2)** renders `undefined` as itself in a string position, decided
by the LOWERING because the runtime cannot tell §3.1's two collapsed values apart.
**(KIR.NATIVE.1)(c)** puts the native arm inside `kir-bench.sh`'s own equivalence gate.
**(KIR.PERF.1)** took both halves: censused by OPERATION the bag is 3,333 ops/parse at
~4.9 ns with **93.6% of reads on a THREE-key bag**, and the cleanest scan that population
could ask for measured NO EFFECT — a fourth design, no win — which closes the container
family and refuses the guarded slot hint with it. So the NOMINAL half was built instead,
and its first slice LANDED: an object literal whose names are statically known becomes a
generated class with one real field per property, **extending `JsObject`** so the erasure
is untouched and structural assignability never enters into it. **`mitt` −10.7%** (61.00 →
**54.50 ns/emit**, ranges disjoint, both Node arms flat, 1.35x → **1.54x FASTER** than
Node); `smol-toml` flat, because its shapes fire but `get` became virtual and its tables
are built dynamically. Suite **15,563 / 0 failures**; the KIR module 83 → 108 pins.

_**Round 935 (2026-08-18) — (CHK.3): LATE-BOUND COMPUTED KEYS, BOTH DIRECTIONS IN ONE COMMIT. THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THAT **tsc's OWN RULE IS NOT PORTABLE AS WRITTEN: A MEMBER NAME DERIVED FROM A *TYPE* IS NOT A FUNCTION OF THE PROGRAM HERE** — the first draft ported `isTypeUsableAsPropertyName` literally and made `const K = "p"; const obj = { [K]: 1 }; obj.p` emit the CORRECT TS2322 **and** `Property 'p' does not exist on type '{}'` in ONE compile, because a FILE-LEVEL un-annotated const answers the literal in the assignability pass and the widened `string` in the pass behind TS2339 (round 933's two-extraction-sites signature reached through ambient state instead of a second `when`; round 911).**_ **STEP 1 WAS tsc 7.0.2 DIRECT on ~40 scratch projects**, every row read from `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --noEmit -p .` and from our own `--listAll` on the SAME directory. _**Round 928 (2026-08-18) — (API.11): A MEMBER DECLARATION NAME RESOLVES TO ITS OWN SYMBOL — the single largest thing refusing a member rename is gone.** A member's own declaration name (an interface's, a class field's, a method's, an accessor's, a static's, a `#private`'s, a type-literal member's, an enum member's) is bound by no scope and has no receiver, so it resolved to NOTHING: `definitionsAt` answered empty, `quickInfoAt` answered `any` — or the COLLIDER's type, (BUG.4) one position over — `referencesAt` answered empty for a member never used, and `renameAt` refused whenever any OTHER interface declared the same member NAME._ **STEP 1 WAS tsc ITSELF, 22 carets over two fixtures** (`scripts/lsp_member_refs.py` plus a definition+hover driver over `tsc --lsp -stdio`), measured beside the same table on this compiler, so the round is a diff of two measured tables: merged `both` **3 refs / 2 defs** in tsc against **0/0 and 2/0** here, an overload set 4/3 against 2/0, an accessor pair 4/2 against 3/0, a never-used member 1/1 against 0/0, and **`any` on all eighteen hovers**. **THE MECHANISM IS A FOURTH RESOLUTION ROUTE, the receiver's exact dual**: a free name goes through the SCOPE CHAIN, a member use through its RECEIVER, an object-literal key through its CONTEXTUAL type, and a declaration name through its **OWNER** — the class, interface, type literal or enum it is declared in. **THE ROUND'S PRODUCT is that "its own symbol" is not a `Symbol` here**: round 884's `mergeSingleSymbol` ADOPTS, so a member declared in two merged `interface` blocks is ONE symbol carrying only the SECOND block's declaration (measured — a caret on the first answered `[decl2, decl1]`, on the second `[decl2]`), where tsc accumulates; the whole declaration list has to be reconstructed one level up, from the OWNER symbol's own declarations, each of which is a container. That matters beyond tidiness: resolving a declaration name REMOVES it from the rename completeness net, whose real quarry is exactly a merged declaration the group missed — "resolve it to itself" would have turned a loud refusal into a SILENTLY SHORT rename. So a merged declaration, an OVERLOAD set and an ACCESSOR PAIR are now one group from any of their declaration names, in every query. **HOVER came along for ~20 lines.** **ONE DELIBERATE EXCLUSION**, conservative: an object literal's own METHOD stays a loud refusal, being outside (API.10)'s key leg. **+16 pins** (`-project` 464 -> 480, core unchanged), suite **14,939 -> 14,955 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**; two existing pins CHANGED MEANING and say so in place. **NINE-ARM ABLATION**, seven distinct sets, with a per-arm POSITIVE RUN CONTROL (the first pass reported seven real results as "BUILD PROBLEM" because Gradle writes its failure summary to STDERR) and the two zero arms recorded as MEASURED-REDUNDANT guards whose reach is proved by other arms rather than claimed. `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on both modules. **AND ONE PAGE THAT DID NOT EXIST**: `docs/language-service.md` **§ 14, State of the API — the two-minute version**: what it answers with a maturity column, the one rule behind every refusal (*prove to offer*), the measured cost table, and all eleven known gaps in one list. **Successor: the incremental / re-entrant seam** — every query is still a full rebuild and a rename is two, which is now the largest thing about this API._

_**Round 927 (2026-08-18) — (API.10): ONE SPAN, TWO SYMBOLS — the LAST of round 922's five refusals. A contextually typed object-literal KEY (`{ p: v }`) and both SHORTHANDS (`{ p }`, `const { p } = o`) are now occurrences of the member the literal's CONTEXTUAL type supplies, in `referencesAt`, `documentHighlightsAt`, `definitionsAt` and `renameAt`._ **THE ROUND'S PRODUCT is that the capture filing ONE answer per span was never the obstacle** — round 926 recorded it as structural, and measured against tsc 7.0.2 (32 carets, `scripts/lsp_rename.py` + `lsp_member_refs.py`) the relation between a shorthand's two symbols is **ASYMMETRIC**: the MEMBER's group CONTAINS the token while a caret ON the token answers the LOCAL's group and nothing else. What was missing was a ROLE, not a second answer. `CapturedDefinition` now carries three declaration sets differing in which of NAVIGATION / SEED / MEMBERSHIP they hold — `locations` all three, `related` seed+membership (the heritage edge, and now an object-literal key's OWN property), the new `shorthand` navigation+membership and deliberately NOT seed, which is why the local's group and the member's never merge through the span they share. The contextual type is a SYNTACTIC walk OUT of the literal (the dual of round 926's destructuring walk) over eleven positions read out of tsc, because the checker's own is walk-scoped and `cpaCtxAt` stops at every statement edge. **THE DISCRIMINATOR** is the rename TEXT: `{ renamed: p }` from the member and `{ p: renamed }` from the local, both of which compile and are one edit at one span. **STILL REFUSED**: a second declaration of the same member name (pre-existing, and the named successor), a shorthand whose member cannot be placed, a computed key. **+19 pins** (`-project` 445 -> 464, core unchanged), suite **14,920 -> 14,939 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, ten-arm ablation with nine distinct red sets, `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on both modules. `docs/language-service.md` §§ 8, 9, 10b, 10d, 13.**_

_**Round 926 (2026-08-18) — (API.9): THE MEMBER OCCURRENCE SET. ROUND 925 MEASURED IT SHORT BY THREE KINDS AND REFUSED A MEMBER RENAME BECAUSE OF THEM; ALL THREE ARE CLOSED, AND THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THAT THE THIRD ONE'S SHAPE WAS DECIDED BY tsc RATHER THAN BY REASONING — TWICE, AGAINST TWO WRONG DESIGNS THIS ROUND HAD ALREADY WRITTEN.**_ **STEP 1 WAS tsc ITSELF**: `scripts/lsp_member_refs.py` (new) drives `textDocument/references`, `rename` and `definition` over `tsc --lsp -stdio` for 25 carets on three fixtures. What was READ rather than reasoned: an interface member is **13 spans over 2 files** including the `o["p"]` LITERAL (edit span `[77,78)` for a literal at `[76,79)` — **the quotes are excluded**), five binding-element property names, TWO implementors' declarations and their `this.p`; a plain `"p"` elsewhere is **not** a reference; a class with the same members and **no `implements`** answers **2 spans**, so **STRUCTURAL COMPATIBILITY DOES NOT RELATE**; an `override` two edges down IS in the group, so the edge is TRANSITIVE; and with `interface A {p}` + `interface B {p}` + `class C implements A, B {p}`, a caret on `A.p` answers 7 spans and **`b.p` is NOT among them**, so the edge does **not** chain between siblings. **KINDS 1 AND 2 WERE CHEAP** — a binding element's `propertyName` lacked a RECEIVER (the pattern's source is the annotation or initializer one to three levels up, `typeCaptureDestructured`, null rather than a guess for an array pattern or an un-annotated parameter), and `o["p"]` lacked a POPULATION (`SourceIndex.occurrenceNodes()` = `identifiers()` plus the string literals that name a member — `identifiers()` untouched, since `fileSemantics`' contract is documented). **KIND 3 TOOK TWO WRONG DESIGNS, BOTH CAUGHT BY MEASUREMENT**: (i) putting the base into `locations` is refuted by tsc's go-to-definition, which on an implementor's member answers THAT MEMBER — so the edge is a separate `CapturedDefinition.related` that grouping reads and `definitionsAt` does not (arm A10); (ii) carrying the edge only on a DECLARATION NAME and closing transitively fails two pins at once — a `this.p` inside the implementor stays out, and the closure merges the `A`/`B`/`C` fixture tsc keeps apart. **The rule that survives is tsc's own and is per-OCCURRENCE**: every occurrence carries its symbol PLUS the bases that symbol implements, and two occurrences are the same thing when those sets MEET (arms A4, A5). **RENAME REFUSES STRICTLY LESS** — round 925's two refusal pins are rewritten to assert the RESULTING TEXT, the discriminator being the QUOTES (a plan built from the token span writes `bracket[renamedBracketed]`, which compiles and means something else — arm A1), with an apply-and-recheck pin as the independent oracle. **STILL REFUSED**: a contextual object-literal key, the binding SHORTHAND `const { p }` and an `o["p"]` the search cannot PLACE — the first two for the same STRUCTURAL reason, one span carrying two symbols, which a capture filing one answer per span cannot express. **ONE VERIFICATION BUG FOUND IN ITS OWN WORK**: the rename's resolution check keys by the capture's key (the NODE's raw `pos`), and an element-access edit begins one character later — keying by the EDIT's start missed silently and refused every such rename as `WOULD_CHANGE_MEANING` (`PlannedEdit.nodePos`, arm A8). **PINS +20** (`-project` 425 -> 445; core UNCHANGED at 14,341), 18 of them the new `ProjectMemberOccurrenceTest`, one discriminator per kind: an `o["p"]` beside two unrelated `"p"` literals, a `{ p: local }` beside an unrelated `local`, and a `Structural` class with the same member and no `implements`. **TEN-ARM ABLATION**, anchored replacements with asserted occurrence counts, sha256-verified restore; _**Round 925 (2026-08-18) — (API.8): RENAME LANDS, AND THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THAT **THE PLAN IS VERIFIED BY APPLYING IT AND COMPILING AGAIN** — SO THE SAFETY CLAIMS ARE A COMPILER RUN RATHER THAN A READING OF THE CODE.**_ `RenamePlan(oldName, newName, files, refusal, conflicts)` / `FileRename` / `RenameEdit(start, end, newText)` / `RenameConflict`, 11 refusals and 5 conflict kinds; **`Project.renameAt(fileName, offset, newName)`**, **ZERO core changes** — (API.5)'s sweep supplies the occurrences and (API.7)'s parent ascent the edit shapes. **STEP 1 WAS tsc ITSELF**: `scripts/lsp_rename.py` drives `tsc --lsp -stdio`'s `prepareRename` + `rename` over 22 carets and prints the RESULTING TEXT, so `{ p }` -> `{ p: newName }`, `const { z }` -> `{ z: newName }` for the local and `{ newName: z }` for the property, and the lib refusal's wording, were read rather than reasoned — and it showed **two places to beat tsc**, which validates neither the new name (`const class = 1`, `const 1bad = 1`) nor collisions (it writes a second `const useZ` beside the first). **THE OCCURRENCE SET WAS MEASURED BEFORE ANY CODE AND IS NOT COMPLETE FOR MEMBERS**: on that fixture tsc's member rename edits 5 spans and ours resolves 2, missing a binding element's `propertyName`, an `o["p"]` (a string literal, outside the identifier population by construction) and an IMPLEMENTOR's member (a different symbol here). So members are **refused with the evidence** rather than planned around: a spelling scan is a SAFETY NET, never the answer, and an identifier spelling the old name that is neither in the group nor resolved elsewhere is a conflict — with a POSITION SPLIT that is load-bearing, since a member declaration name resolves to nothing and without it an `interface I { p }` anywhere would refuse renaming an unrelated local `p`. **THE VERIFICATION IS THE ROUND**: the plan is applied to a scratch `OverlayVfs` and the program rebuilt — it must re-read, add NO diagnostic (**the collision check**), and every renamed occurrence plus every identifier that ALREADY spelled the new name must resolve to exactly what it did before (**the capture check**: renaming a file-level `a` to `b` where a body holds its own `b` compiles, emits nothing anywhere, and means something else — arm A4 is the only thing that sees it). **ONE MEASURED CORRECTION**: the expectation must be each occurrence's OWN prior answer, not the seed; demanding the seed reports this API's own blind spot as a change of meaning and refused three correct member renames before it was found (arm A10). **DIVERGENCE FROM tsc, STATED**: a bare `export { p }` / `import { p }` is replaced PLAINLY where tsc expands to `newName as p` — our identity crosses the alias hop, so local and export are one symbol and the group renames together. **REFUSED with reasons**: a library declaration, an ALIASED import, an unresolved import, either half of an `as`, a reserved or malformed new name (**no build**), and a member whose set cannot be shown complete. **PINS +35** (`-project` 390 -> 425; core UNCHANGED at 14,341), 14 of them parse-only; the DISCRIMINATOR is the shorthand asserted as exact resulting TEXT, and **APPLY-AND-RECHECK** pins re-apply the plan through `updateFile` and assert the diagnostics are byte-identical — an independent oracle of the internal verification. **TWELVE-ARM ABLATION**, anchored replacements with asserted occurrence counts, sha256-verified restore. **GATES: suite 14,865 -> 14,900 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = exactly the +35**; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core and on `-project` explicitly. **MEASURED ON tsc's OWN SOURCES**: renaming `SyntaxKind` produces **9,827 edits across 49 files** in 23.9-24.5 s warm. `docs/language-service.md` § 10d._

_**Round 924 (2026-08-18) — (BUG.4): QUICK INFO ON A MEMBER NAME. THE ITEM SAID IT REPORTS `any`; MEASURED AGAINST tsc 7.0.2's OWN LANGUAGE SERVER IT IS WORSE — IT REPORTS THE TYPE OF WHATEVER UNRELATED BINDING SHARES THE MEMBER'S SPELLING.**_ `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --lsp -stdio` is an LSP server, so the ground truth for a hover is obtainable directly rather than reasoned from sources: a small Python client over a fixture whose every member is deliberately spelled like a file-level `const` of ANOTHER type gives tsc's own answer at every caret. **Of 23 wrong member positions, 16 read the COLLIDER** (`o.k` `boolean` for a `string` property, `box.value` `string` for a `number` one, `this.p` `number` for a `string` field), 6 read `any`, and one was right by coincidence — a member name is bound by no scope, so typing it as a free name does not fail loudly. **THE FIX IS A CHANNEL, AND IT IS tsc's OWN RULE**: `getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation` moves off the right-hand side of a property access ONTO THE ACCESS, so the type of the `p` in `o.p` is the type of `o.p`. A throwaway probe of exactly that was measured FIRST and was already correct for the generic instantiation, the inherited member, the union receiver, the type-parameter receiver, the static side, the enum and namespace members and the flow-NARROWED member — because `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` implements all of them. So the landed fix contains **no member walk**: the brief's `propertyTypeOnCarrier` route was the right instinct at the wrong altitude, and a member-TABLE read is what arm A2 shows failing. **THE ONE RECEIVER NEEDING (API.3d)'s CARRIER is `this`/`super`** — plain identifiers in this parser, so they type as `any` and the access inherits it; the leg reads round 923's `currentClassForThis` and is ADDITIVE, so where it cannot decide the access answers `any`: a non-answer, never a wrong name. **TWO NEIGHBOURS CASHED**: an element access `o["p"]` (the caret is on the literal, whose own `string` made the old answer right only by coincidence) and a qualified TYPE name `N.T` (through the export table). **THREE tsc DIVERGENCES NAMED rather than asserted away**: `this` in a STATIC member (`typeof C` is unmodelled, round 916), an object-literal member's literal widening, and a type rendered under a synonymous alias (proved display-layer by a second namespace type with none). **PINS +27** (`-project` 363 -> 390; core UNCHANGED at 14,341), every expected value READ OUT OF tsc rather than predicted. **EIGHT-ARM ABLATION**, sha256-verified restore: A1 channel off **21**, A2 member-table read **3** (the two generic pins + narrowing — CLAUDE.md's shared-symbol trap caught by its own pins), A3 no explicit member resolution **0** (MEASURED-REDUNDANT, recorded as that), A4 the naive free-name fallback **2**, A5 no this/super leg **4**, A6 `super` from the this-type **1**, A7 no element access **1**, A8 no qualified name **1** — **seven distinct sets**. **GATES**: suite 14,838 -> **14,865 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = exactly the +27**; Warning-clean. `docs/language-service.md` § 8. **Successor: RENAME**._

_**Round 923 (2026-08-18) — (BUG.3): THE LAYER QUESTION WAS THE ROUND, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT **THE CHECKER IS RIGHT AND THE CAPTURE WAS WRONG** — MEASURED BEFORE A LINE WAS WRITTEN, AGAINST tsc 7.0.2 ITSELF.** Round 922 found `this.` inside a nested arrow answering NO members and queued the fix without saying which layer owned it; had it been the CHECKER, `this.typo` would be silently unchecked in one of the most common shapes in the language. It is not: a 24-line fixture covering `this` in a method, in an arrow, in an arrow inside an arrow, in a `function` expression and declaration, in an object-literal method, in a getter, a setter, a constructor, a property initializer, a static member, a static-member arrow and a class expression compiles to **17 diagnostics that are byte-identical to tsc 7.0.2** — same codes, same positions, `TS2339` on `C` through both arrows and `TS2683` where `this` is rebound. So the defect was the capture's ambient reconstruction alone: `typeCaptureVisit` read `CtaFrame.classForThis`, and a cta frame is a TYPE-checking context that does not thread `this` — the frame an arrow body pushes carries null there. **The fix is `typeCaptureThisClass`, a pull-based ascent that is transparent to arrows and opaque to every other function-like**, deliberately NOT round 922's `typeCaptureEnclosingClass` (which answers the accessibility question and would offer a class's members inside a `function`) and deliberately NOT the checker's own `spineCaClassCtx` (right shape, but bug-compatible with a narrower legacy walker on the nested-`FunctionDeclaration` arm — the one place reusing it verbatim would have passed every other pin and failed). Bias PROVE TO OFFER: a static member, an object literal's method, a `function` at any depth, a class EXPRESSION and no-class-at-all each answer NOTHING. **Two side findings, both stated rather than fixed**: an EXPRESSION-bodied arrow already worked, because a cta frame is pushed at a `Block` enter and such an arrow opens none — the bug was never "arrows" but arrows that open a block; and **quick info on a member name is a separate, RECEIVER-INDEPENDENT gap** (`o.p`, `this.p` in a method and `this.p` in an arrow all report `any`, because `quickInfoAt` types the member identifier as if it were a free name), so the brief's "they share the path" is false and pinning it here would have pinned the wrong subject. **+20 pins** (`-project` 343 -> 363; core UNCHANGED). **SEVEN-ARM ablation**, five reddening a distinct set, one measured REDUNDANT and one built to show that guard's pin is not vacuous. Suite 14,818 -> **14,838 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**. `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters and the **8-profile `--listAll` grid `added=0 removed=0` on every profile against a rebuilt HEAD binary** — the change is unreachable without a capture request. `docs/language-service.md` § 9._
_**Round 922 (2026-08-18) — (API.7): THE SYNTACTIC-ROLE MECHANISM LANDS AND **THREE OF THE FIVE STANDING REFUSALS ARE CASHED**; THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THE CORRECTION TO ITS OWN RANKING — THE BACKLOG WAS PROMOTED AS ONE ITEM BECAUSE "ALL FIVE WANT THE SAME MISSING MECHANISM", AND **ONLY THREE DO**._ **THE MECHANISM** is `SyntaxRoles` (`-project`), a PULL-BASED ascent of the parent chain INV.2(a) already stamps — `referenceUse(node)` for a node's role and `grammarPositionOf(path)` / `keywordsFor(path)` for a caret's, the second expressed on the first (an identifier is in an EXPRESSION position exactly when it is a value occurrence). Pull rather than push on round 875's measurement (a maintained status is **11.1x** the work); every comparison is `===`, because AST nodes are `data class`es and a `current in parent.elements` would be round 471's deep structural compare. **IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT ALL LIVE IN ONE MODULE**: accessibility's caret-side half is the same ascent, but its other half needs symbols and heritage, so it is a sibling ascent in `Checker.kt`. **CASHED (1) MEMBER-COMPLETION ACCESSIBILITY** (round 917's refusal): `private` — `#name` fields included — only inside the declaring class, `protected` there or in a derived one, statics alike, the ascent reaching out of a nested arrow through its method to the class and the heritage walk following an IMPORT HOP; **biased PROVE-TO-HIDE**, so an unresolvable base or a missing declaring class leaves the member OFFERED — the only answer to round 917's own objection that a list which has silently lost a real candidate looks exactly like a complete one. **CASHED (2) KEYWORDS** (round 918's refusal), BOUNDED EXPLICITLY: a STATEMENT caret gets the declaration and statement starters plus the expression starters, an EXPRESSION caret the expression starters ONLY (this is what keeps `interface` out of `f(|)`), a TYPE caret the primitive type names plus `keyof`/`typeof`, and a class body / heritage clause / import clause NOTHING — with `await` gated on an enclosing async function, `yield` on a generator, `super` on a class, `return` on a function, `break` on a loop or switch, `continue` on a loop (the scan stopping at the first function-like) and the module-level starters on a file or namespace body; every CONTINUATION keyword is refused outright and said to be. **CASHED (3) READ-vs-WRITE** (round 919's refusal), `ReferenceLocation.use`, with the write set stated completely and **`UNCLASSIFIED` as a fourth state rather than a default** — the ascent is why the destructuring cases are free, an array literal inside an object literal inside an array literal being three pass-through steps and then ONE assignment test. **STILL REFUSED, REASON CORRECTED — the finding**: an element access (`o["p"]`) and a contextual object-literal key (`{ p: v }`) were **never blocked on a grammar position**; recognising either is one test on the node's parent, and what each lacks is SEMANTIC (a capture channel plus member-lookup-by-text — the receiver resolution is already here; and a CONTEXTUAL type, walk-scoped and absent outright in a ternary branch). **TWO EXISTING ANSWERS CHANGED** and the round-917/918 pins asserting the old refusals were UPDATED IN PLACE, never deleted. **A PROCESS TRAP, now in CLAUDE.md: round 855's "dry-run each arm for a real diff" is VACUOUS on a tree carrying the round's own uncommitted work** — `git diff --shortstat` printed the identical whole-round diff for every arm; what carried the run is the patch helper's anchor-count assertion, and the script now diffs against its own snapshot. **FOUND IN PASSING (BUG.3): a caret on `this.` inside a NESTED ARROW answers no members at all** (`currentClassForThis` is null there). **PINS +45** (`-project` 298 -> 343, core UNCHANGED at 14,341), 32 of them PARSE-ONLY; the discriminators are round 919's own three destructuring shapes, an EXPRESSION position asserted not to offer `interface`, a non-async function asserted not to offer `await`, and a caret inside a SUBCLASS METHOD — which every rival accessibility rule passes the easy cases and fails alone. **FOURTEEN-ARM ABLATION, one mistake at a time, restored from a sha256-verified snapshot; all fourteen compiled and ALL FOURTEEN reddened a DISTINCT set.** **GATES: suite 14,773 -> 14,818 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the +45**, re-run on the byte-restored post-ablation tree; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (750 classes, 15,981 methods) and on `-project` explicitly (35 classes, 326 methods); `spine_closure_audit.py` 46 handlers all supersets; the round-920 real-source token gate 1,327 files / 101,287,620 chars / ZERO violations. `docs/language-service.md` §§ 10a, 10b. **Successor: RENAME** — (API.5) plus an edit plan, and `ReferenceUse` is now available to it._

_**Round 921 (2026-08-18) — (API.6): SIGNATURE HELP LANDS, EVERY OVERLOAD, AND THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THAT THE RANKING'S "three-quarters built" PREMISE HELD FOR THE CALLEE AND WAS WRONG ABOUT THE ANCHOR.** `SignatureHelp(signatures, activeSignature, activeArgument)` / `SignatureInfo(label, parameters, returnTypeText, activeParameter)` / `ParameterInfo(name, typeText, optional, isRest, labelStart, labelEnd)`; **`Project.signatureHelpAt`**, null when the caret is in no argument list and an EMPTY signature list when it is in one whose callee has none. The callee half was exactly as ranked — `getCalleeType` + `getCallSignaturesOfType` answered a method through a receiver, an import, a call-callee and a decorator factory with no rule of their own; only (API.3d)'s export-table leg (a namespace member is on no TYPE — arm A5) and `getReturnTypeOfNewExpression` behind `new` had to be added. **THE ANCHOR IS THE ROUND: this is the first query in the arc whose subject is a REGION the parse carries no node for**, and three ordinary cases defeat containment — `f(a, b|)` is at the real END of `b` and still argument 1; `f(a, |)`'s second argument does not exist in the tree; and for `f(` at EOF or `f(a,` before a `}` **the call's own real end lies BEFORE the caret**. The parser recovery was READ OUT OF `Parser.kt` first: `parseArgumentListWorker` breaks on EOF and on `}` and then runs `parseExpected(CloseParen)`, so the `CallExpression` exists in every shape — so the region is BRACKET-MATCHED over the token stream (stopping at a closer that does not match the top of the stack, i.e. the `}` closing the enclosing block) and the index is A COUNT OF COMMAS, with "this list's own" decided by testing the ARGUMENTS' spans — which excludes a nested call's, an object literal's, an arrow parameter list's and a `Map<string, number>` TYPE ARGUMENT's commas by ONE test, the last being the case no bracket-depth scan could have handled (arm A8, 4 red). **ACTIVE SIGNATURE**: the first with room for the caret's argument AND accepting the arguments already FINISHED, judged by `signatureAcceptsArgs` — the same verdict `resolveCallOverload` selects with — while the argument the caret is IN is deliberately not judged (half-typed, so judging it flips the highlight under the user's hands); both halves ablated (A6 always-0 -> 2 red, A7 arity-only -> 1). **ONE COMPILER-SIDE SURPRISE**: a BINDING-PATTERN parameter is dropped from `Signature.parameters` and the survivors keep a POSITIONAL zip, so rendering from the symbols alone prints `destructured(tail: O)` — one parameter short AND wearing its neighbour's type; the DECLARATION is rendered instead (A10, 1 red uniquely its own). Every type goes through `typeToString` (hover's renderer) and NOT `signatureToString`, whose `| undefined` is a TS2345 message convention; parameter ranges index the LABEL and are recorded as it is built (A11). A generic callee renders UNINSTANTIATED. **REFUSED with reasons and pins**: tagged templates, type arguments, `super(...)` and a spread's arity; decorator factories and a call-callee are NOT refused. **PINS +56** (`-project` 242 -> 298, core UNCHANGED at 14,341) — 30 parse-only anchor pins written FIRST, 26 end-to-end; the DISCRIMINATOR is an OVERLOADED callee asserted as an EXACT list of three labels, which every shortcut answers ONE of. **ELEVEN-ARM ABLATION, one mistake at a time, each dry-run for a real diff and restored from a sha256-verified snapshot; all eleven compiled and ALL ELEVEN reddened a DISTINCT set** (A9, the region taken as the call's real end, reddens 6 — every incomplete-call pin plus the past-the-paren negative). **GATES: suite 14,717 -> 14,773 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the +56**; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** (a real gate — `Checker.kt` grew ~370 lines reachable from the hook on the hot walk); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (750 classes, 15,976 methods) and on `-project` explicitly (28 classes, 280 methods); `spine_closure_audit.py` 46 handlers all supersets; the round-920 real-source token gate 1,327 files / 101,287,620 chars / ZERO violations. `docs/language-service.md` § 10c. **Successor: the refusal backlog as ONE item** — accessibility filtering, contextual object-literal keys, element access, keywords and read-vs-write all want the same grammar-position mechanism._

_**Round 920 (2026-08-18) — (GATE.2): THE REAL-SOURCE INVARIANT GATE FOR THE POSITION APIs, AND IT FOUND FIVE MORE DEFECTS ON ITS FIRST RUN.** Round 919 fixed (BUG.2) and did not build the instrument; this is it. `TokenIndexInvariants` asserts TEN rules true of any correct implementation — tokens partition the text, gaps hold only trivia, a string never crosses a line break, **every identifier the PARSER found starts a token of exactly its length**, a descent reaches every identifier, paths nest, positions round-trip against an independent restatement of the terminator rule — with **the parse as the oracle**, which is what makes a MERGE expressible. Three corpora: an adversarial shape set, **the real `lib.*.d.ts` sources** (2.39 MB, already embedded — hermetic, no vendored tree), and `build/bench/tsc-project-*` behind a runner that **REFUSES rather than skips**. FOUND: (A) **a backtick inside a regular expression** in tsc's own `utilities.ts` opened a template literal running to the next backtick anywhere in the file — a **25,761-character token** — i.e. (BUG.2) in a second costume; (B) a **parenthesis-less arrow parameter**, an index-signature parameter and a `catch` variable were built at `[0, 0)`, so no descent could enter them: **328 sites in tsc's 78 sources**, the API's most common wrong answer; (C) `declare global`'s `global`, (D) JSX tag names and (E) a construct signature's synthetic `new` likewise had spans no descent could enter. All five fixed. The fix for (A) is the keeper: **ask the parse** — a regex node and a JsxText each carry their own raw text, so the index reproduces whatever the parser decided and the undecidable `/` question is never asked. AFTER: **1,327 files, 101,287,620 chars, 11,299,274 tokens, 3,936,158 identifiers, ZERO violations** (before: 50 of 78 files failing on the compiler profile alone). Cost **+32 ms = +9.9% of `SourceIndex.of`**, paid only by a host's position query; `cost_gate.py` +0.00%. Suite **14,708 -> 14,717 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**; core pins UNCHANGED at 14,341._

_**Round 917 (2026-08-17) — (API.4a): THE COMPLETION ANCHOR + MEMBER COMPLETIONS LAND. THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THAT THE ANCHOR — THE PART EVERY (API.\*) ROUND SO FAR CALLED THE GENUINELY NEW ONE — NEEDED **NO PARSER WORK AT ALL**, BECAUSE THIS PARSER ALREADY RECOVERS A DANGLING `.` INTO A REAL `PropertyAccessExpression`; AND THAT THE ONE THING THAT DID BITE WAS A **SPAN COLLISION** IN WHICH `o` AND `o.<nothing>` CARRY THE IDENTICAL `(pos, end)` PAIR AND FIRST-WINS ANSWERS `any`._ **THE ANCHOR** (`SourceIndex.completionAnchorAt`, `-project`, where round 910's caret already lives) is a TOKEN-level question because a completion request has no node at the caret by construction: it reports a `CompletionKind` (MEMBER / FREE_NAME / NONE), the typed PREFIX and a replacement span covering the WHOLE word rather than only the prefix. **The recovery rule for an incomplete `o.` is that there is nothing to recover** — `Parser.kt`'s `Dot ->` arm is unconditional, synthesizing a zero-width `Identifier("")` and reporting TS1003, so the receiver is a real node at end of file, before a `}` and across a newline alike; the anchor descends to the character BEFORE the dot (the dot is often the file's LAST token, so descending to the dot answers `SourceFile`) and walks back OUT to the access satisfying `realEnd(expression) <= dotStart < name.pos`, which **at most one node in a path can satisfy**. **THE DEFECT, and it is a property of the capture design rather than of completions: a raw span does not always identify a node.** With the buffer ending IMMEDIATELY after the dot the synthesized name is zero-width at EOF, so the access's `end` equals its RECEIVER's; preorder reaches the access first and `typeToString` of an empty-named access is `any`. Measured, `holder.` read EMPTY while `holder. `, `holder.;` and `holder.\n` all read `[alpha, beta]` — visible in one of five shapes. The fix states the missing invariant: **among nodes sharing a span the DEEPEST is what was asked about**, so a later visit overwrites an earlier one iff it is a DESCENDANT; two visits of the SAME node still keep the first, leaving round 911's tightest-ambient rule untouched. **THE MEMBER HALF was (API.3d) one question wider, as round 916 predicted, down to the leg order** (`this`, then the EXPORT table, then the type + `resolveStructuredTypeMembers`). What is NOT reused is the UNION RULE, deliberately: **go-to-definition COLLECTS across a union and completions INTERSECT**, because "where is `p` declared" is asked about a name already in the text while "what may I write here" must not offer something that will not compile; nullish constituents are SKIPPED rather than allowed to empty the intersection. **OFF IS STILL FREE STRUCTURALLY**: `memberSpans` is a SECOND span list unioned into `keysByFile`, so the hot-path guard is byte-identical and `fileSemantics` — which hands in every identifier in a file — never enumerates a type. **ANSWERED**: inherited members (an override ONCE), intersection, union (with the member's type rendered as the distinct constituent types joined by `|`), nullish union, merged interface, imported interface, namespace, enum, `this`, a lib primitive, and an incomplete `o.` in three buffer shapes. **REFUSED with a reason**: free names (`FREE_NAMES_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, an explicit refusal rather than a silent empty list); strings / templates / regexes / numeric literals / comments and out-of-file positions (`NO_COMPLETION_CONTEXT`, and they do NOT build); accessibility FILTERING — private and protected members are OFFERED with `accessibility` saying which they are, since hiding one correctly depends on where the caret sits relative to the declaring class. An `any` or unresolvable receiver answers empty and is NOT a refusal. **A PIN WAS WRITTEN AS A DISCRIMINATOR, MEASURED NOT TO BE ONE, AND RENAMED** (round 807 applied to my own work): the lazy-member-table rule is load-bearing but a `declare const x: T` already resolves `T`'s table, so the ONE receiver nothing else resolves is `this`. **A FIXTURE TRAP worth one line: `|` is both a caret marker and a union separator** — the first run placed a caret 46 characters early inside `{ a: number } | undefined` and measured a real anchor at the wrong offset while looking correct; the marker is U+2038 now. **PINS: +49** (`-project` 142 -> 191; core unchanged), 23 of them parse-only anchor pins written FIRST. **SIX-ARM ABLATION, one mistake at a time, each dry-run for a real diff and restored from a sha256-verified snapshot rather than `git checkout`; every arm a DISTINCT set**: A1 no `resolveStructuredTypeMembers` -> 1 red; A2 the anchor ignores the prefix -> 5 red; A3 a union COLLECTS -> 1 red uniquely its own; A4 plain first-wins -> 1 red, exactly the end-of-buffer pin; A5 no export leg -> 2 red; A6 nullish not skipped -> 1 red uniquely its own. **GATES: suite 14,613 -> 14,662 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped**, XML-summed over all six modules; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (742 classes, 15,851 methods, 0 over) and on `-project` explicitly (21 classes); `spine_closure_audit.py` 46 handlers all supersets; warning-clean. **(API.4b) — free-name completions + keywords — lands by DELETING one refusal, with no signature change; its queue entry now carries what it inherits, the per-file scope-clear that forces capture-during-the-walk, and the measured size problem (round 902: 290.94 symbols per real probe).**_

an IMPORTED interface's member (in the declaring file); an INHERITED member (the BASE's declaration); a MERGED member and a UNION/INTERSECTION receiver (**one location per contributing declaration / per constituent**, in order, deduplicated); `N.x` and the qualified TYPE `N.T` for a namespace, module alias or **enum** (whose members are on no TYPE at all — they are in `symbol.exports`, which is why that leg exists); a LIB member, by CONSISTENCY rather than taste (`definitionsAt` already documents that a free name resolving into a lib answers with a file the host may not open). **REFUSED, each with a stated reason**: `o["p"]` (the argument is a literal — only identifiers are offered a definition), `{ p: v }`'s own key (the useful target is the CONTEXTUAL type's property, a third mechanism), a member's own declaration name (it already IS the declaration), `A.B.x`'s tail, and anything unresolvable (silence, never the nearest same-named thing). **ONE EXISTING BUG ONLY REACHABLE NOW**: `typeCaptureDeclarationName` had no `PropertyAssignment` arm, so an object literal's member answered with the whole `size: 1` instead of `size`. **THREE PINS HAD THEIR MEANING CHANGED AND SAY SO IN-FILE** (an unlogged pin change is indistinguishable from hiding a regression), with a NEW negative control replacing what they guarded. **PINS +13 net +10**; _**Round 915 (2026-08-17) — (BUG.1): THE LONE-`\r` SELF-INCONSISTENCY IS CLOSED, AND THE SWEEP THE ITEM ASKED FOR FOUND **FIVE** OFFSET-TO-LINE CONVERTERS WHERE THE QUEUE NAMED TWO — FOUR OF THEM WRONG, EACH A PRIVATE COPY OF A LOOP NOBODY KNEW WAS DUPLICATED. THE ROUND'S REAL PRODUCT IS THE **SECOND** REASON THE FAMILY WAS INVISIBLE: THE STRING ENTRY POINT BEHIND THE ENTIRE GENERATED CORPUS NORMALISES `\r` AWAY BEFORE THE PARSER RUNS, SO NO CORPUS FIXTURE COULD EVER HAVE CAUGHT IT — NOT "none happens to have a lone `\r`" BUT "none CAN"._ **THE PREMISE HELD**: `Parser.computeLineStarts` broke a line at `\n`, `\r\n` and a lone `\r` (tsc's rule) while `Checker.lineStartsFor` counted `\n` only, so a SYNTAX diagnostic numbered a classic-Mac file's lines and a SEMANTIC one reported line 1. **THE SWEEP — the part that cannot be re-derived cheaply — found SIX private implementations of one conversion, in THREE conventions, none referring to any other**: the Parser's (correct); `Checker.lineStartsFor`; `Checker.posOfLineCol`, the INVERSE, so the two directions did not even round-trip; `TypeScriptCompiler.positionToLineCharacter` (~14 diagnostic call sites) and its inline TS2688 twin; the `Transformer`'s JSX dev-runtime `lineNumber`/`columnNumber`, which feeds EMITTED JavaScript rather than a diagnostic; and `CompilerOptions.computeLineAndColumn` for tsconfig positions, whose convention treated `\r` as **zero-width**. `-project`'s `LineMap` was already right and stays a reimplementation (it also carries `lineContentEnds`), pinned by a differential rather than by restatement. **WHAT LANDED**: a new `LineStarts.kt` stating the rule ONCE as `lineBreakWidthAt(text, i)` -> 0/1/2, with the two traversal shapes built on it — `computeLineStarts` (moved out of `Parser.kt`, `internal`, logic byte-identical) and `lineAndCharacterAt` (a BOUNDED scan for sites with nothing to memoize on) — and all five wrong copies delegating; `posOfLineCol` now reads the same memoized index as the forward direction, which makes the round-trip structural. **THE FINDING, bigger than the bug**: `parseMultiFileSource` opens with `.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")`, so every generated test and every `diagnose()` pin is structurally unable to put a `\r` in front of the Parser — the corpus's one genuinely lone-`\r` fixture (`templateStringMultiline3`) is normalised before parsing. Only the project/`Vfs` path preserves terminators, i.e. exactly the path the `(API.*)` arc sits on: the bug was becoming reachable as it was found. **The first core pin was therefore VACUOUS AND PASSED** — it built `\r` text, the harness made it `\n` text, and "the two halves agree" was a claim about a file with no `\r` in it; `diagnoseVerbatim` now hands the pipeline a `ParsedSource` directly. A SECOND vacuity the same way: a fixture BEGINNING with `\r` is not a leading-break test either, since blank lines before the first content line are dropped. **PINS: +10** (10 core `LineTerminatorConsistencyTest`, +1 `ProjectPositionTest` lone-`\r` differential, −1 `LineMapTest` case that pinned the divergence as permanent); the sharp four are self-consistency (TS2322 on line 3 AND TS1128 on line 5 of one lone-`\r` file, every diagnostic's `line` matched against an independent restatement), the three-shape control (`\n`/`\r\n`/`\r` must yield the SAME `(code, line)` table — what catches the obvious double-count, which would read 5 and 9 for CRLF), consecutive+trailing breaks, and the CHARACTER on the line after a `\r`; the other six pin the shared helper's edges and are labelled in-file as NON-discriminating. **ABLATION** (pre-fix `\n`-only body restored by hand, never `git checkout`): **exactly 5 pins red**, the 6 arithmetic guards and all 12 other `ProjectPositionTest` cases green. **GATES: suite 14,593 -> 14,603 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the +10** (core 14,326 -> 14,336; `-project` unchanged at 133, being +1/−1), XML-summed over all six modules; **no corpus baseline moved**, the load-bearing negative since `\n` and `\r\n` had to be untouched. `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** — a real gate here, the Checker's line index changed shape — proven live by its own 46-error / 78-file compile. `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (**739 classes, up from 738 — the gate SAW `LineStartsKt`**) and on `-project` (12); `Checker.<init>` unmoved at 5,813. Warning-clean. No wall A/B: `lineStartsFor` is memoized per source and `computeLineStarts` is lazy, so the loop is on no hot path._

_**Round 913 (2026-08-17) — (API.3b): GO-TO-DEFINITION LANDS, AND THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THAT **THE QUEUE ENTRY'S OWN PREMISE WAS WRONG** — (API.3a)'s ambient lesson does NOT transfer._ The entry said *"a symbol resolved without `withCtaFrameLocals` is the same wrong answer one indirection along"*; `withCtaFrameLocals` restores `currentLocalTypes`, a map of `String -> Type` that holds **no symbols and no declarations**, so it cannot answer a definition question in either direction. What can is the INV.2(c) lexical chain (`spineScopeLookup`), and **the spine maintains that PER NODE** (`spineScopeEnterIfOwner` runs BEFORE `spineEnterNode`) where the checking ambient is install-and-restore PER ANCHOR — so it needs no reconstruction. **WHAT DOES TRANSFER IS THE ONLY THING THAT MATTERS: both inputs are gone once the walk is over** (`spineScopeClear` nulls the chain per file), which is what still makes capture mandatory. **THE MEASUREMENT** (captured vs post-hoc, ONE `Checker`, core `DefinitionCaptureMeasurementTest`): a body local shadowing a same-named file-level `const` answers **the body declaration / the file-level one** — a DIFFERENT DECLARATION, so an editor would navigate to the wrong line and look like it worked; a parameter answers **the parameter / nothing at all**; the control, a file-level `const`, is answered **identically by both**, which is exactly what makes the body-local row dangerous rather than obviously broken. **ONE HOOK, TWO RECORDED FACTS** — no second handler and no second request type; `spineEnterMask` untouched (the hook is a `spineEnterNode` PROLOGUE line), audit run anyway. **THE SPAN QUESTION DECIDED THE OTHER WAY FROM (API.3a), ON PURPOSE**: a captured TYPE hands the RAW `(pos,end)` back for `-project`'s `SourceIndex` to snap, but a DECLARATION is usually in a file the caller never asked about and may not be able to read at all — **a `lib.*.d.ts` has no path on disk** — so the exact end is computed IN THE CORE, scanning FORWARD from the name's own `pos` (`Scanner.resetToPosition`, greatest token end strictly below `Node.end`): one or two tokens, no index, no cache, same graceful degradation `SourceIndex` documents. The span is the **NAME**, as tsc navigates. **WHAT ANSWERS NOTHING, DELIBERATELY AND PINNED AS SUCH**: a MEMBER name (`o.p`) is REFUSED rather than resolved, because a scope lookup of a member name finds whatever unrelated binding shares the spelling and a confidently wrong navigation target is worse than none — the gate is a REJECT-list over parent kinds (a missed reject is a wrong answer; a missed accept only a missing one), and the pin proves it is a refusal not a miss by asserting the TYPE at the same span IS captured. An imported name answers about the **ORIGINAL** through `resolveImportedSymbolGeneral`; a MERGED symbol answers with **EVERY** declaration. **FOUR-ARM ABLATION, ONE MISTAKE AT A TIME, each dry-run for a real diff and restored from a byte-verified copy rather than `git checkout`**: A1 drop the lexical chain -> 5 red; A2 raw `Node.end` as length -> 5 red; A3 drop the free-name gate -> **1 red, uniquely its own**; A4 drop the alias hop -> **1 red, uniquely its own**. Every arm a DISTINCT set. **GATES: suite 14,548 -> 14,567 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the 19 new pins** (11 `-project`, 8 core), XML-parsed across all six modules and re-run on the byte-restored post-ablation tree; **`cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters** (a real gate — `Checker.kt` grew ~240 lines on the hot walk — proven live by its own 46-error / 78-file compile); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (738 classes, `Checker.<init>` 5,802 -> 5,813 = the one new field) AND, per round 909's blind-spot rule, on the module explicitly (11 classes, up from 10 — the gate SAW the new code); `spine_closure_audit.py` 46 handlers all supersets; warning-clean. No wall A/B: production gains nothing but a branch INSIDE an already-null-guarded hook. **(API.3c) — batching a file's spans into ONE build — is next and unchanged; member go-to-definition is an honest gap, needing the receiver's type rather than the scope chain._

_**Round 912 (2026-08-17) — (WARM.35): THE FOUR UNPRICED CANDIDATES FROM ROUND 903's HOT-PATH AUDIT ARE **ALL REFUSED** — LARGEST **0.18%**, ALL FOUR TOGETHER **0.303% (15.9 ms)**, UNDER THE ~17 ms FLOOR FOR *ONE* LOW-RISK CHANGE — AND THE ROUND'S REAL PRODUCT IS THAT **THE QUEUE'S OWN POPULATION FOR THE LARGEST WAS A TRANSCRIBED SOURCE COMMENT**, WITH **TWO OF THE FOUR FIXES DEAD BEFORE ARITHMETIC.**_ Nothing built, no amplifier: population x a generous per-op ceiling, checked against round 896's divide-and-refuse, as round 904 refused the boxed-key family. Warm compiler profile (78 files, 46 errors), `BenchMain <proj> 6 2` / `6 3`, instrumented medians **5,065.7** / **5,170.8 ms**; denominator **5,242.6 ms** (1% = 52.4 ms). **`mappedNodeTypeKey` key build 25,987 keys of 110,780 calls = 9.36 ms = 0.179% (1.8x); `NarrowFlowMemo` default arg 31,768 = 4.77 ms = 0.091% (3.6x); `collectTypeofGuardNames` &c `LinkedHashSet` 22,798 = 1.48 ms = 0.028% (11.5x); the two ambient sandwiches 2,841 = 0.28 ms = 0.005% (60x, KILLED BY READING).** To reach 17 ms they would need **654 / 535 / 746 / 5,983 ns per operation**, against a measured **15.09 ns** for a whole `HashMap` get that recursively hashes AND `equals` a 2.76-node AST subtree. **CONTROLS: two processes agree to the last digit on all 22 counters, and `mappedNodeTypeKey calls = 110,780` reproduces `cost-counters.txt`'s `typeNode.bypassed` exactly.** **THE FINDING: the queue's "~88 k/rebuild" was an in-source comment, wrong in BOTH directions** — 110,780 calls (the comment aged 26%) and only **25,987** key builds (3.4x fewer), because **76.5%** exit at the foreign-file gate first. **WHAT DID NOT WORK, worth more than the prices:** candidate 3's `inline` **is not expressible in Kotlin** (both wrappers hand `block` to a RECURSIVE non-inline callee, so `inline` forces `noinline`, which re-materialises the lambda — dead on grounds of the LANGUAGE, and reading the CALLEE shows it), and candidate 4's shared-memo fix is a **SOUNDNESS bug** (`narrowTypeFromFlowCore` handles re-entrant walks at `narrowLiveDepth == 0` by design; 34.2% of memos outgrow 32 slots, so `clear()` is not obviously cheaper). **NEW REUSABLE CONSTANT, the allocation twin of round 904's ~1.7 M map-ops bar: a pure-allocation candidate needs > 113,000 allocations/rebuild at 150 ns, or > 340,000 at 50 ns, to clear the floor.** Recorded and still under it: `mappedNodeTypeKey` spends **110,780 parent-chain climbs + 110,780 `String`-keyed probes (~5.5 ms)** just to answer "foreign file". **No code changed** — corpus count unmoved, no suite/gate run. `docs/perf/round912-candidate-census.md`. **Successor named per the WORK ORDER NOTE: the (API.\*) arc — (API.3b) go-to-definition next, (API.3c) batching as the item that makes the API practical for an editor; the checker-side pool is now empty of UNPRICED candidates too, and the two remaining perf levers are artifact-level and both gated ((ART.1) on the owner's release decision, (ART.2) on a CRaC JDK no longer installed on this box).**_

_**Round 910 (2026-08-17) — (API.2) LANDED IN TWO HALVES, AND THE ROUND'S REAL PRODUCT IS TWO **MEASURED FACTS ABOUT OUR AST SPANS** THAT MAKE THE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATION WRONG: **`Node.end` IS THE END OF THE TOKEN *FOLLOWING* THE NODE, SO SIBLING SPANS OVERLAP AND `[pos, end)` IS NOT A CONTAINMENT TEST.**_ **WHAT LANDED:** a public `LineMap` / `TextPosition` (both 1-based, `Diagnostic`'s convention) with `Project.positionAt` / `offsetAt` — which read through the overlay and **deliberately do NOT build**, so a host converts coordinates on a dirty project for free (pinned `lists == 0`, `reads == 1`) — plus `Project.nodeInfoAt` returning a **value-typed** `NodeInfo(kind, start, end, ancestorKinds)` over an `internal nodeAt` / `SourceIndex`; **no AST, `Symbol` or `Type` is published**, so (API.3)'s surface decision stays open. **53 new pins, module 30 -> 83.** **FINDING 1, IN SOURCE AND EMPIRICALLY:** `Parser.getEnd() = scanner.getPos()` (`Parser.kt:746`) is read after the parser's ONE-TOKEN LOOKAHEAD, so in `const abc = 1;` the identifier `abc` reads **`[6,11)`** where its text is `[6,9)` — a caret on the `=` tests as inside `abc`, a caret on `let` as inside the previous statement, and `end - pos` is not a length. **FINDING 2, the mirror trap:** `Node.pos` is tsc's `getStart()`, NOT tsc's `pos` (`Scanner.scan` sets `tokenPos` AFTER `scanLeadingTrivia()`, `Scanner.kt:331-333`), so a routine ported from tsc that adds a `getStart()` skip DOUBLE-SKIPS past the node's own first token. **AND THE QUEUE'S IMPLIED FIX IS REFUTED, which is the sharpest result:** bounding by the NEXT SIBLING'S `pos` fixes the caret-on-`let` case and NOT the `=` — the initializer starts at 12 while `abc` ends at 11, so `min(11,12) = 11` and offset 10 is still inside; **the `=` is covered by no child at all, so no arithmetic over child positions can ever see it.** The sound rule is `realEnd = the greatest TOKEN end strictly below node.end`, one extra `Scanner` pass per parse, binary-searched and cached; the context-free re-scan can only SPLIT a contextual token (adding ends, preserving every real boundary), and a merge would report the PARENT — coarser, never wrong-sibling. **BOUNDARY CONVENTION** half-open (`offset == end` is OUTSIDE), matching `Diagnostic.start`/`length` and tsc's `getTokenAtPosition`; tsserver's touch preference belongs a layer above, because building it in would make two adjacent nodes both contain the boundary. **TWO PINS MEASURED VACUOUS AND SAID SO RATHER THAN SHIPPED:** a `.tsx`/`.jsx` fixture cannot pin that `computeParserFlags` was consulted (`Parser.isJsxFile` keys off the EXTENSION) and neither can a top-level `await` (our parser yields an `AwaitExpression` with the flag OFF — two tests failed proving it); the one option-derived GRAMMAR difference is `forceJsx` on a plain `.js` file, which is the pin that landed. **THE ONE CORE CHANGE IS ONE WORD:** `computeParserFlags` `internal` -> public — the implementer hit it, REFUSED to hand-roll the flags and stopped, correctly, since its KDoc calls it *the single source of truth so a crawl-time parse is provably the parse the core would produce* and a duplicate would be **drift no test in the consuming module could see** (no `-Xfriend-paths` between modules). **(API.3) DECIDED BY READING, NOT PREFERENCE** (`a966ad76`): `getTypeOfIdentifier` consults `currentLocalTypes` (*"populated during TS2322 checking walk"*), `currentParamBindingNames`, `currentCheckFileName`/`fileLocalTypeMaps`, `currentFileLocals` and the namespace chain BEFORE the node-keyed lookup, and at rest those are empty/null — so a post-hoc query skips five reads and, for a function-body local, can resolve to an unrelated same-named global. The design is **position-directed capture**. **FOUND IN PASSING, QUEUED AS (BUG.1):** `Parser.computeLineStarts` breaks a line on a lone `\r` and `Checker.lineStartsFor` counts `\n` only, so syntax and semantic diagnostics number lines differently on classic-Mac text — invisible to every corpus baseline. **GATES: suite 14,469 -> 14,522 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the 53 new pins**, XML-parsed across all six modules with core unchanged at 14,305; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** — a real control here rather than a tautology, since `internal` -> public removes JVM name mangling, and the gate proved live (46 errors / 78 files); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (732) AND on the module (9, up from 3, so the gate saw the new code); warning-clean. **Ablation: `realEndOf` returning raw `node.end` reddens EXACTLY the 7 predicted pins**, restored by hand from a scratchpad copy — never `git checkout`, which would have destroyed the round's uncommitted work._

_**Round 909 (2026-08-17) — (API.1): A NEW ARC ON OWNER DIRECTIVE — THE **PROJECT / LANGUAGESERVICE EMBEDDING API**, which is what round 908's empty checker-side pool makes room for. SLICE 1 LANDED: a new module `xemantic-typescript-compiler-project` (jvm(), `explicitApi()`, `api(project(":…-core"))`, mirroring `-cli`) with a public `Project.open` / `configPath` / `files` / `diagnostics()` / `diagnostics(file)` / `updateFile` / `deleteFile` / `close()` plus an `internal OverlayVfs`, and **30 pins**._ Owner-answered scoping: a **Kotlin embedding API first** (LSP / tsserver layered later), first slice **diagnostics + in-memory edits only**, no editor features and deliberately no stub facade. **THE ONLY PRE-EXISTING FILE TOUCHED IS `settings.gradle.kts`** (2 insertions) — zero bytes of core, which is why `cost_gate.py` was not run: on this diff it is a tautology, not a control. **THE ARCHITECTURAL FACT THE API IS SHAPED AROUND, IN ITS OWN KDoc RATHER THAN HIDDEN: a query on a dirty project is a FULL REBUILD, and that is the compiler's property, not a shortcut** — `ProjectCompiler.Result` retains **no AST, no `BinderResult`, no `Checker`** (the checker's construction IS the compilation, `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md:850`); what makes a re-query cheap is the process-global **CONTENT-keyed** `CrawlParseCache`, and that same keying is why an overlay edit **cannot be served a stale parse** (no mtime/size/stat in the decision, round 871). Every build passes `noEmit = true` — a tool that opens a project to ask questions must never scatter JavaScript from unsaved buffers through the user's tree. **THE OVERLAY IS THREE MECHANISMS, EACH PINNED SEPARATELY:** `ModuleResolver` probes `exists` before `readText`, `ProjectCompiler.walk` asks `isDirectory` per entry and descends only on yes, and the glob finds roots through `list` alone — get one wrong and a whole class of edits silently does nothing; `list` is SORTED because program order decides which file first touches a shared type node. **Ablation, one mistake at a time: dropping the overlay-children clause from `isDirectory` reddens exactly 2 pins and nothing else**, and the fix/introduce pair is airtight by construction (the backing store holds the opposite text, so neither an always-stale nor an always-empty result satisfies both). **THE ROUND'S TWO PRODUCTS BESIDES THE CODE.** (i) A **VACUOUS-FIXTURE TRAP** that cost three tests green with an *empty* diagnostic list, now in CLAUDE.md: TS2307 is suppressed both by `binderResults.size <= 1 && !isMultiFileSource` (`Checker.kt:45409,45853` — the real libs bind through their own path and DO NOT COUNT, so a two-file fixture whose second file IS the missing import reduces to ONE program file) and by the relative leg's `options.module in ES_MODULE_KINDS` (`:46098`, five resolution keys unset), so a tsconfig carrying only `target`/`strict` leaves `module` unset and **every unresolved-import assertion is vacuous** — such a pin needs a negative control or it measures its own vacuity. (ii) **(ART.1) IS STALE AS WRITTEN and the queue entry is corrected rather than worked**: `native.yml:60-72` **already builds Oracle GraalVM + PGO** via `scripts/build-native-pgo.sh`, byte-identity-verified against the JVM and uploaded as `xtsc-linux-x64`, while `bench.yml` builds the Oracle **BASE** image per push deliberately — so what remains is the **shipping decision, already tracked as (AOT.1) and explicitly the owner's**, not a perf lever, and it is unmeasurable on the dev box (**no GraalVM installed**). A comment-only `bench.yml` correction found uncommitted in the tree landed separately (`4c74eae4`) because its header contradicted its own build step. **AND AN INSTRUMENT BLIND SPOT THE SIXTH MODULE CREATED, also in CLAUDE.md: `huge_methods.py` IS `-core`-ONLY BY DEFAULT**, so its green run here was a CONTROL — the tell was `classes scanned : 732` identical to round 907's; passed `--classes <module>/build/classes/kotlin/jvm/main` it scans the new module's **3** classes, 0 over the limit (round 853's law, one module over). **GATES: suite 14,439 -> 14,469 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the 30 new pins**, counted by XML parse across all **six** modules (`*/build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` — the root-level glob matches nothing post-split); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (732) AND on the new module (3); build warning-clean; no wall A/B and nothing to A/B._

_**Round 907 (2026-08-15) — (WARM.34): THE COUNT QUESTION IS **REFUSED BY ITS OWN CENSUS**, AND THE `lexLevelHasName` FAMILY IS **CLOSED ENTIRELY**. THE QUEUE'S PREMISE WAS WRONG IN THE SAME WAY ROUND 902'S OWN LAW PREDICTS: **"THE O(depth) ASCENT" DESCRIBES THE *CHAIN* (3.69 STEPS), NOT THE *PROBES* (1.54) — A CHAIN-STEP POPULATION IS NOT A PROBE POPULATION.**_ Nothing was built. **THE CENSUS, WITH AN EXACT PARTITION CHECK:** three processes identical to the last digit, reproduced across all three of the round's builds (nine runs), per-ascent probe counts summing to **870,231 = every real probe the three families make** — **563,466 ascents**, **2,079,962 NameScope chain steps (3.69 each)**, **870,231 real map probes (1.544 per ascent)**, so **the whole probe stream at round 901's measured 36.6 ns is 31.85 ms = 0.602%**, which is the ceiling on EVERYTHING in this family and twice what round 902 projected. **THE PREMISE WAS WRONG, AND ITS REFUTATION IS ROUND 902'S LAW ONE STEP FURTHER ALONG ROUND 902'S OWN FAMILY:** the queue said the probes arise from an O(depth) ascent revisiting the big outer levels, but that describes the CHAIN — **58% of level visits are refused by the untrusted / non-head-fn rules or are hash-free EMPTY maps** (round 901's short-circuit finding), so 3.69 steps become 1.54 probes. **THE REDUNDANCY IS REAL AND DOES NOT HELP, WHICH IS THE ROUND'S SHARPEST RESULT: 80.7% of the stream re-probes a `(level, name)` pair already asked** (142,632 distinct pairs, 5.17 probes each), and all three levers are still under the floor. **(i) The ascent memo the queue named**: 36.4% of ascents repeat a `(scope, name, family)` key — a fine hit rate — but **a repeat ascent performs 1.32 real probes and a memo probe replaces them with 1**, so the net is 66,095 probes = **2.42 ms** before charging 358,586 misses and inserts; with the memo ENTIRELY FREE it is 9.92 ms = 0.187%, and at the measured probe cost it is **-10.7 ms, a regression**. **(ii) A per-level memo** — the 21.8 ms the 80.7% implies — is refused BY CONSTRUCTION: *a cache keyed by the same name at the same granularity as the map it fronts IS that map.* **(iii) A per-file proof-of-absence filter**, the only operation cheaper than a probe, is bounded by a measured superset at **<= 7.30 ms = 0.138%**. Union of (i) and (iii), both free and assumed disjoint, is 0.338%; with their own costs 0.257%. **To clear 0.31% a lever must delete more than half the stream at zero cost; the best deletes 25%.** **THE FAMILY IS CLOSED, ALL THREE LEVERS, ACROSS THREE ROUNDS** — container by round 901's filter (+0.26%) and round 902's parallel array (-0.19%), count by this round — **and the closure is now GENERAL rather than per-lever: the whole stream is 0.60%, so any one-operation oracle costing one probe recovers at most 0.21%.** Recorded in passing: **`typeParamConstraintOf` is called 0 times per rebuild**, and two of the five families average UNDER ONE real probe per ascent. **THE ABLATION'S BLIND ARM IS THE ROUND'S SECOND FINDING:** six arms, one mistake at a time, every red set unique — but **C1 was blind on the first pass**, because a pin asserting `steps > calls` **summed over five families** stayed green against a census whose ascent count (the denominator of the entire result) had been inflated to the chain-step count, one family carrying 47% of the sum. **A PIN OVER A SUM IS A PIN OVER ITS LARGEST MEMBER.** Two further pins read zero before the fixture was repaired (round 849, in both directions). **GATES — AND THE GRID IS A REAL GATE HERE**, because the production shape DID change (the five ascent functions are split into an entry and a `...From` recursion so a top-level query can be told from a chain step): **suite 14,430 -> 14,437 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = exactly the 7 new pins**, verified by XML parse across all four modules; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** (a control); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit, 732 classes**; **8-PROFILE `--listAll` GRID all eight `added=0 removed=0`**, zero exceptions, against round 905's committed captures.
