_**Round 906 (2026-08-14) — (WARM.33): THE LARGEST ESTIMATED ITEM IN THE QUEUE IS **REFUSED, AND IT IS A REGRESSION AT EVERY GEOMETRY** — AND ROUND 875 HAD THE **SIGN** WRONG, NOT THE MAGNITUDE: IT READ THE *ASCENT'S* SCATTER ONTO THE *PROBE'S* SEQUENTIAL SWEEP. **THE CEILING FOR *ANY* MEMO-LAYOUT CHANGE IS 2.65-15.99 ms, BELOW THE FLOOR EVERYWHERE — THE WHOLE DIRECTION IS CLOSED.**_ Priced with **no clock in the round at all**. **ROUND 875'S OWN QUEUED INSTRUMENT CANNOT WORK, AND SAYING SO IS THE ROUND'S FIRST PRODUCT:** it queued *a transposed-layout AMPLIFIER arm on the memo probe*, but an amplifier repeats one probe `r` times under a timestamp pair, so from the second repetition the line is **L1-hot** and it prices an L1 hit — exactly the cost the change exists to remove. (The sibling Rust compiler hit this precisely in its PG11: a memo removed 35.6% of repeat reads and moved the mechanism 16.18% -> 15.44%, *because the repeat read was already in L1*.) **A LOCALITY CHANGE CANNOT BE AMPLIFIED** — so the instrument is a census of the exact access stream plus a set-associative LRU MODEL, three layouts x five geometries, whose answer is a **miss-count delta**: a deterministic counter, not a measurement. **THE CENSUS, FALSIFIERS EXACT:** all **139** `memo[...]` access lines hooked (43 entry probes, 2 interleaved, 43 ascent probes, 51 writes) — **8,888,467 accesses per rebuild** (probe 1,960,176 / ascent 3,166,496 / write 3,761,795) over a **38.4 MiB** footprint; the 43 classifiers' probes sum to **1,909,715 = `ReachCensus.calls` to the digit**, and the gap histogram's 2,816,334 steps plus the two interleaved classifiers' 350,162 reproduce 3,166,496 exactly; two processes identical to the last digit. **ROUND 902'S LAW AGAIN, AND AGAIN IT MATTERED — the mean 2.23 is not the quantity:** **13.9% of nodes are consulted by nobody**, the 738,192 consulted average **2.655**, and the transposable population (second-and-later consultations) is **1,221,984 = 62.3%**. **THE FINDING THAT REVERSES THE CANDIDATE: THE ASCENT IS NOT SCATTERED.** **42.2% of ascent steps go to `nodeId - 1`** and **89.8% stay within 64 ids** — inside one cache line of TODAY's layout — and the spine walks in PREORDER, so each classifier's 1-byte array is swept **sequentially**: a line serves **~14.2** consultations against a 45-byte transposed row's **~3.8**, and **layout A already answers 97.0% of accesses out of L1**. **THE PRICE, zeroing separated (it is bandwidth-bound, ~4 ms, identical in every layout): box(32K/512K/16M) A 16.87 ms, transposed **+3.90**, padded row **+23.88**, ceiling on ANY layout **2.65 ms = 0.05%**; flushed(4K/64K/512K) A 30.22, +24.20, +46.21, ceiling **15.99 ms = 0.30%**; the three intermediate geometries fall between. **Shrinking the cache — the only direction in which the model's optimism could have hidden a prize — makes the candidate WORSE**, and layout C, the candidate's own best form, is the worst arm. **A CORRECTION TO THE QUEUE ENTRY ITSELF:** it promised the change *deletes 36.9 MB/rebuild of allocated+zeroed `ByteArray`*; it deletes **55 KB of array headers**, because 43 arrays of n bytes and one array of 43n bytes **are the same bytes** — a figure inherited from round 875 and restated without checking. *A queue entry is a claim, and it inherits its ancestors' errors silently.* **ONE ADJACENT DIRECTION PRICED AND CLOSED ON THE WAY PAST:** lazily allocating the 17 classifiers consulted <1,000x per rebuild is worth **~2-3 ms**, below the floor before it starts, recorded so nobody re-opens it as the ~57 ms a naive read of the model's `dram` column suggests. **GATES: suite 14,424 -> 14,430 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 6 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters (a control, not a verdict); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit; three single-mistake ablation arms, each with REACHED-NESS evidence (round 902), distinct red sets, tree restored and pins re-run green. No wall A/B and none possible — the round contains no clock.** `docs/perf/reach-memo-transposition-price.md`._

_**Round 905 (2026-08-14) — (WARM.32): THE ITERATOR-ALLOCATION FAMILY — THE ONE CANDIDATE IMPORTED FROM THE SIBLING RUST COMPILER, WHERE THE SAME MECHANISM MEASURED **-3.1%** — IS **REFUSED HERE AT 0.074% (3.90 ms), BY 4.4x**. THE MECHANISM TRANSFERS AND THE **SHAPE** DOES NOT: 215 SITES ARE **495,305 CALLS OVER 2-ELEMENT LISTS**, AND **A COUNT OF SITES IS NOT A COUNT OF CALLS.**_ Priced before the fix; the extraction landed, the fix did not. **THE CANDIDATE:** Kotlin's `Iterable.any`/`forEach` are `inline` but their bodies are `for (e in this)` on an `Iterable` receiver, so each call asks for a HEAP ITERATOR and pays `hasNext`/`next` virtual dispatch per element; `../xemantic-rust-compiler` landed exactly this conversion (its PH3) for **-3.1% wall**, recording why a sampled share did not over-promise there — *an object handed to an iterator escapes by construction*, so escape analysis was never going to fold it. **THE CENSUS REFUSED IT BEFORE THE AMPLIFIER RAN**, two processes identical to the last digit: **495,305 calls over 925,502 elements** — `forEachChild` list positions 275,477 calls / 547,102 elements (mean **1.986**, 7.0% EMPTY, **52.4% SINGLETON**), `anyIdentical` 219,828 calls / 378,400 visited (mean **1.721**, because it **hits 94.4%** of the time and a hit stops the scan). **17 ms over 495,305 calls is 34.3 ns per call, and round 904 measured a WHOLE boxed `HashMap<Long, .>` probe at 8.53 ns** — no per-call mechanism this cheap clears the floor at this population. **THE MEASUREMENT, BOTH HALVES, FITTED PER ARM** (r = 8/24, ABBA, mirrored across two processes, 16 draws, leading draw dropped; denominator 5,290 ms): `forEachChild` iterator **17.084 ns** vs indexed **5.136** (boundaries 67.9 / 61.4), `anyIdentical` iterator **5.750** vs indexed **2.996** (60.0 / 43.3) — **premiums 11.95 and 2.75 ns = 3.29 + 0.60 = 3.90 ms = 0.074%**, pooled 4.49, most-generous 4.58, all refusing. **And the premium is an UPPER bound**: both arms fold into a trivial sink, the cheapest possible body, where iterator overhead is maximally exposed, while production's body is a megamorphic `action(e)`. Falsifiers: sinks EQUAL between arms in all 16 draws, every sink an exact multiple of `r`, no arm flat. **ROUND 904'S BOUNDARY LAW IS SHARPENED BY ITS OWN SUCCESSOR — 23% BECOMES 76%, AND THE MECHANISM IS NAMED: a boundary is a property of the ARM, not of the harness.** It absorbs everything charged per CALL, and an iterator-constructing arm builds its iterator THERE, so the gap between two arms' boundaries is itself part of what is being measured; on `anyIdentical` the single-`r` form reads 4.85 ns against a true 2.75. **Round 904's "both boundaries must land near ~90 ns" free check is WITHDRAWN** — these four read 67.9 / 61.4 / 60.0 / 43.3 and were all correct; the surviving check is arithmetic, `premium + (b_A - b_B)/r` reproducing the measured single-`r` `A - B` at BOTH `r`, closing to 0.01 ns on both halves. **A COUNT OF SITES IS NOT A COUNT OF CALLS — 4-6x under the queue's own projection:** 215 sites produced 495,305 calls, because most children are DIRECT `action(x)` positions rather than list positions (IDENTIFIER alone is 44.5% of nodes and has no child list at all), and **only 219,828 of round 875's 3.32 M edge evaluations ever reach an `.any`**; with it, two ceilings corrected — the iterator is **4.4%** of `forEachChild`'s 1.40% leaf row and `.any` is **1.4%** of round 875's 44 ms edge bound. **THE SIBLING IS NOT CONTRADICTED, WHICH IS THE POINT WORTH KEEPING:** 11.95 ns is real, 1.4x round 904's whole boxed-key premium, and the mechanism is identical — what differs is the population it runs over (per-token `withIndex()` compositions allocating ~24 objects a call, against 495 k calls over 2-element lists). *A mechanism transfers between codebases; a price does not.* **WHAT LANDED, SINCE THE FIX DID NOT:** the 215 sites now route through `walkList` / `anyIdentical` in `NodeWalk.kt`, bodies the verbatim lowering of what they replace, so the family has ONE HOME and the fix would have been one line each — kept because a future agent cannot otherwise re-open it blind, and because it shrank **`forEachChild`'s three (JIT.1) partitions from 9,256 to 5,929 bytecodes (-36%)** on the compiler's traversal primitive. **The strongest gate was not a run: the substitution was proved PURELY TEXTUAL by inverting it against the parent commit** — `Checker.kt` round-trips exactly at all 145 sites, `NodeWalk.kt` at 69/70, the 70th differing only in which of two equivalent spellings the inverter chose. *Stated rather than hidden: the extraction is not priced by a warm A/B — expected effect ~0 against a +-1.0% band, so it is gated on behaviour, not wall.* **GATES: suite 14,416 -> 14,424 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = exactly the 8 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 18 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid, all eight captured, no exception and no truncation (`scripts/round905-grid.sh` enumerates by the presence of a `tsconfig.json` and REFUSES below 8 — round 895's law). That grid is a REAL gate here, not a control: the round rewrites the enumeration primitive every walk in the compiler goes through.** `docs/perf/iterator-allocation-price.md`._

_**Round 904 (2026-08-14) — (WARM.31): THE WHOLE BOXED-PRIMITIVE-KEY FAMILY IS **REFUSED** — 14 SITES, **2,698,745 OPERATIONS PER REBUILD, A 6.58 ns PREMIUM, 17.7 ms = 0.334% FOR ALL OF THEM TOGETHER** AND **0.064% FOR THE LARGEST SINGLE ONE**. AND THE 29.4 ms THAT RANKED IT WAS ONE DRAW OF A **4x-UNSTABLE** NUMBER: THE SAME LEAF FAMILY READS **72.9 ms AND 19.0 ms IN ROUND 899'S OWN TWO DUMPS, SAME BINARY.**_ Priced before a line of fix; no production behaviour change. **THE THRESHOLD WAS COMPUTED BEFORE THE CENSUS RAN AND IT IS WHAT CLOSED THE FAMILY:** at a generous 10 ns premium against the ~17 ms floor a single site needs **~1.7 M operations per rebuild**, and **the whole check spine visits 856,962 nodes** — so *every per-node memo in the compiler is refused by arithmetic before any build*, and the question reduced to whether any site is driven by something other than node count. None is; the largest of the fourteen is **519,478 ops = 30% of the single-site threshold**. **THE CENSUS, WITH BOTH CONTROLS HITTING EXACTLY:** `--boxedKeyCensus`, deterministic, two processes agreeing to the last digit — `importedSymbolGeneralCache` 519,478, `Relation.cache` 456,660, `relationComparisonStack` 444,446 (max live **27**), the enum caches 427,024, the ten spine `nodeId` memos 319,558; the filter that could have refused half the family for free refuses none of it. **TWO SITES ARE REFUSED BY *SHAPE*, NOT PRICE, WHICH IS WHAT A FUTURE "JUST MIGRATE THEM ALL" PROPOSAL NEEDS:** `Binder.lexicalScopes` is ITERATED and `IntKeyMap`/`LongKeyMap` have no iterator **by design** — which is exactly what makes the rounds-754/776/778 order hazard a compile error, the same exemption CLAUDE.md already grants `Binder.nodeToSymbol`; _**Round 897 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.24): ROUND 894'S **TOP-RANKED** CANDIDATE, SCANNER IDENTIFIER INTERNING, IS **REFUSED** — MEASURED PRIZE **17.2 ms (0.31%)** AGAINST ITS **67.7 ms** CEILING, MEASURED COST **11.1 ms PER PARSE**, AND A BLOCKER THE CENSUS'S "RISK: LOW, A HANDFUL OF LINES IN ONE FUNCTION" DID NOT SEE.**_ Priced BEFORE a line of fix (`NameCensus.kt`, a `namecensus<N>` BenchMain tier, four JVMs in two batches). **THE REGIME FACT FIRST: `CrawlParseCache` serves the program's parse from the previous request, so in the WARM regime this arc measures the Scanner does not run at all** — an intern probe is paid once per file VERSION while the map probes it makes cheaper are paid every rebuild. That also corrects the census: its 24.8 ms/rebuild of `String.hashCode` cannot be scanner strings, because a cached parse's instances persist and `String.hashCode` caches per instance, so the ceiling was 42.9 not 67.7 ms before any other deflation. **POPULATIONS: ~527 k identifier tokens per parse collapse onto ~22.4 k distinct names (94.4% intern hit rate); on the resolution path 1,063,149 `moduleOnlyGlobalNames` probes of which 623,146 HIT (58.6%)** — and the hit RATE is what matters, since `HashMap` calls `String.equals` only for a matching 32-bit hash, so a miss never walks characters and only a hit can pay what interning removes. **PRIZE, BY REPLAY (round 896(B)'s shape), 17.2 ms/rebuild = 0.31%** (four processes 14.2/15.3/20.3/19.1), ABBA per rep, falsified by ARITHMETIC — every arm's hit count an exact multiple of the reps and the two arms agreeing on all of them, which is interning's equivalence claim in miniature. **AND THE DECOMPOSITION IS THE FINDING: INTERNING IS ONLY HALF `String.equals`.** The map arm is the control — `globals` holds 185 entries and is hit 10,383 times per rep, so an equals-driven delta is at most 0.15 ms, yet the measured delta is 4.9-6.7 ms: **97% of it is the KEY-OBJECT WORKING SET collapsing from ~400 k String instances to ~22.4 k**. Splitting the set arm the same way gives **`equals` 9.1 ms + locality 8.1 ms, and 14.6 ns per equal-but-distinct comparison** — the census's § 5a mechanism measured directly for the first time. **The locality half is invisible to a leaf-frame profile**, because it is time in whatever frame dereferences the object, not in `String.equals`. **COST: 52.4 ns/token as a separate table (27.6 ms/parse) and 21.2 ns/token FOLDED into the `KEYWORDS[word]` probe `scanIdentifier` already pays (11.1 ms/parse)** — a design the census did not consider, which halves the cost and cannot go much lower (434 k intern hits per parse at 14.6 ns is 6.3 ms on its own). 11.1 is not "clearly below" 17.2. **THE BLOCKER IS THE STRONGER GROUND AND THE INSTRUMENT PROVED IT: the prize needs a PROGRAM-WIDE table (a per-FILE one captures ~none of it, since the prize is cross-file identity) and `parseForCrawl` runs inside `withContext(Dispatchers.Default)` — round 825's hazard verbatim.** The proof is the census's own numbers: its SCANNER counters disagree by up to 4.7% across four processes on one binary while its CHECKER counters are identical to the last digit in all four. **TWO MORE CORRECTIONS: (2b) is refused (a "definitely absent" bitset is worthless for a hit, so its reachable population is the 440,003 misses and its prize ~2-9 ms against a ≤42.9 ms ceiling), and (7) is CLOSED not deferred (its prerequisite now costs 11.1-27.6 ms per parse and is blocked, while its own deletable part is 6.5 ms). Three of round 894's ceilings are now measured at 4-10x their answer — round 896's finding for the third time.** **THE ABLATION TOOK THREE PASSES: 5 of 6 arms discriminate with one pin each, A2 is a RECORDED REDUNDANT GUARD; pass 1 read 4 of 6 green because A1 was a pin that did not exist (swapping the container does not change the ANSWER, so every hit-count pin stays green, correctly), A1's first repair was then blind because the arms' own ABBA rotation overwrites an even-branch fault — the observable had to become STICKY — and A5/A6 were blind because the FIXTURE seeded the snapshots and masked them.** **GATES: suite 14,350 -> 14,358 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 8 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters (the expected CONTROL for a round that lands no behaviour change, and here also the statement that the inert hooks are inert); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid vs round 896's captures on the identical recipe, ALL EIGHT `added=0 removed=0`.** `docs/perf/name-intern-price.md`._

_**Round 903 (2026-08-14) — (WARM.30): THE CANDIDATE THIS FILE AND CLAUDE.md BOTH CERTIFIED AS "THE ONE JFR ROW WORTH BELIEVING" IS **REFUSED AT 0.085%, AND ITS ROW IS OVER BY 6.3x** — BECAUSE THE PLAUSIBILITY ARGUMENT THAT ADMITTED IT APPEALED TO A MAGNITUDE NOBODY HAD MEASURED. THE RECURSION IS REAL AND IT IS **TWO NODES DEEP**.**_ Priced before a line of fix; no production behaviour change. **THE CANDIDATE AND THE STATUS IT HELD:** `state.nodeTypes` is a `HashMap<TypeNode, Type>` keyed by the AST VALUE, and every concrete `TypeNode` is a `data class` (139 in `Ast.kt`, ZERO `override fun equals`), so each probe is a recursive structural hash — round 471's hazard. JFR **57.1 ms**, the largest single map owner. Rounds 894-899 refuted eight of nine JFR-ranked candidates by dividing the row by its own population; **this one PASSED that division at 161 ns/op and CLAUDE.md recorded it as *what makes that row worth believing*** — but a recursive `hashCode` licenses ANY rate, because the check appealed to the depth of the recursion and the depth had never been measured. **THE CENSUS DECIDED IT BEFORE THE AMPLIFIER RAN:** `calls 287,062 / hits 116,999 / misses 59,283 / bypassed 110,780`, unindexed keys **0**, **probe-weighted mean key subtree 2.7567 nodes (max 337), 73.6% of probes presenting a key of at most TWO nodes.** At the measured 5.47 ns/node, 161 ns/op needs a **29.4**-node mean — exactly the 25-40 the design predicted the row would require, and **10.7x what exists**. *The row was refuted by its own arithmetic the moment its population was known.* **THE AMPLIFIER (r=8 and r=24, 16 draws per r, two mirrored batches; every sink an exact multiple of r, no arm flat): ARM A `nodeTypes[node]` (deep hash + bucket + deep equals) 15.09 ns/op; ARM B the same probe against a `(file, nodeId)` `LongKeyMap` 2.11; ARM C `isPerFileDependentRefNode` — the SECOND owner — 12.88.** `A - B` is the deep key's premium, i.e. THE PRIZE OF THE PROPOSED FIX measured directly rather than by proxy. *The map GET is amplified rather than `node.hashCode()` because a data-class hash is a pure function of an immutable object and C2 may hoist it — and the exact-multiple sink falsifier would still pass, the one failure mode that falsifier cannot see.* **THE DECISION: REFUSED BY 3.7x** — `(A - B) x 354,131 = 4.60 ms = 0.085%` of 5,429 ms against this arc's ~0.31% (~17 ms) floor, and 2.5x under it even at the most generous single-draw bound (6.77 ms = 0.125%); `A - B` is an UPPER bound (arm B's key is computed outside every pair), so the refusal is certain rather than marginal. **Round 896's sentinel-set candidate falls with it** — 118,566 of those ops at this premium is 1.54 ms, against the 3-5 ms it was refused at on a soundness ground that no longer has to be argued. **THE CORRECTION TO THE JFR ATTRIBUTION, WHICH STANDS EITHER WAY AND WHICH THE ROUND FOUND BEFORE MEASURING ANYTHING: the row has TWO owners and a leaf-frame profile cannot separate them** — `cacheable` calls `isPerFileDependentRefNode`, itself a recursive subtree walk over the SAME subtree the hash walks, on EVERY call: 12.88 ns x 287,062 = **3.70 ms (0.068%)**. Family total **5.34 + 3.70 = 9.04 ms against a 57.1 ms row = over by 6.3x**, the ninth consecutive JFR over-read and at the top of the recorded 2.1-21x band. *An instrument bracketing only the map probe would have charged the walk to the hash and reported roughly double the prize.* **ROUND 902'S LAW DOES NOT BITE HERE, AND THAT IS INFORMATION RATHER THAN AN EXCEPTION:** probe-weighted and object-weighted key sizes differ by **6.6%** (2.7567 vs 2.5856), not by 193x — the law says which weighting to CHECK, not which always wins. **WHAT THE DEEP KEY ACTUALLY BUYS, MEASURED: 130 shared probes of 176,282 (0.074%)**, read off the two arms' sink difference and exactly `130 x r` at both r in both batches; the unit fixture had PASSED an `A == B` sink equality that the real population refutes, and it is now a `<=` bound with the difference reported as a number. Not a licence to re-key — `PerFileTypeNameCacheCollisionTest` pins the case where that sharing is WRONG, so 0.074% is the size of the benefit, not of the risk. **ARM C NEARLY SHIPPED AS A ROUND-902 DEAD ARM:** `isPerFileDependentRefNode` opens with `if (multiFileModuleTypeNames.isEmpty() ...) return false`, so on a program without such names it prices a field read while reading, in every driver output, exactly like a subtree walk; a REACHED control was added BEFORE the number was quoted and reports **5**. Round 902's lesson applied one round later, prospectively rather than in the post-mortem. **GATES: suite 14,396 -> 14,409 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = exactly the 13 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 18 counters (the expected control for an instrument-only round); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit (`getTypeFromTypeNodeCore` grew and stays under, `Checker.<init>` 5,753/8,000); build warning-clean. No wall A/B for the twelfth round running, and nothing to A/B — the round lands an instrument and a refusal.** `docs/perf/type-node-key-price.md`._


_**Round 902 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.29): ROUND 901'S SUCCESSOR — THE PARALLEL-ARRAY CONTAINER IT PRICED AT 0.41-0.47% — IS **REFUSED, AND IT IS A REGRESSION**: MEASURED, IT COSTS **+13.75 ns PER PROBE = −10.1 ms = −0.19%**. THE ESTIMATE WAS NOT OPTIMISTIC, IT WAS COMPUTED OVER THE WRONG POPULATION — **A LEVEL IS SCANNED ONCE PER *PROBE*, NOT ONCE PER *EXISTENCE*, AND THE TWO MEANS DIFFER BY 193x** (1.51 own symbols against **290.94**).**_ No production behaviour change; the round lands an instrument and a refusal. **THE ROUND DID EXACTLY WHAT ROUND 901 SAID TO DO, AND THAT IS WHAT KILLED THE CANDIDATE:** 901 refused a filter at 0.26%, named the successor ("a parallel-array linear scan, map fallback above ~8, 794,251 probes at ~3-6 ns instead of 33-37 = ~22-25 ms"), and explicitly did NOT build it because the rate was ESTIMATED and *the next instrument is a third `--lexLevelAmp` arm, not a fix* — **had it trusted the estimate it would have shipped a 0.19% regression behind a container change to a binder-OUTPUT type.** **A SCOPE POPULATION IS NOT A PROBE POPULATION (round 890's law, one family over):** 901 priced the scan off `lexBoundHistogram`, which counts each `LexicalScope` ONCE — `15270 8381 3748 …`, 46.7% empty, mean **1.51**; counting the same scopes once **per real probe** gives `0 166388 101041 62112 44319 35255 28750 22145 15900 261681`, mean **290.94**, **212.12 scan steps per probe**. The unresolved-names ascent walks OUTWARDS, so it reaches the big outer levels on every walk: **35.5% of probes land on levels averaging 815 symbols**, and those alone are 213.2 M of the 214.6 M symbols a scan would traverse per rebuild. *The cost of a scan is weighted by the probes; the cost of an allocation is weighted by the scopes — 901 measured the second and priced the first.* **THE MEASUREMENT:** four arms under one timestamp pair each, cyclically rotated, `r = 4` and `r = 16`, two runs each, ABBA at the run level — warm slopes **MAP 6.00 / FILTER 0.96 / SCAN 709.2 / HYBRID 7.42 ns per rep**, and at `r = 1` where the boundary cancels between arms **the unconditional scan is +1,046 ns against the map and the hybrid +13.75 ns**. Falsifiers all hold: sink an exact 4x between the two `r`, and scan and hybrid sinks EQUAL to the map's at both (where 901's filter arm could only assert a superset), hybrid branch split 475,910/261,681 matching the histogram to the unit. **Round 901's two arms reproduce** — MAP warm slope 6.00 vs 6.4, FILTER 0.96 vs 1.17, map-minus-filter first probe 28.9 ns vs 33-37 and the JFR row's 36.6. **AND NO THRESHOLD RESCUES IT, WHICH IS THE STRONGER HALF:** the hybrid's scanned levels average **2.86** entries and it still loses, because the 35.5% that fall back pay the array load, the null test and the length test **and then the whole map probe**, and 2.86 elements is 2.86 `String` dereferences to scattered objects against one cached hash and one `Node`. **Even if the replacement were FREE the whole <=8 population is 13.8-17.6 ms = 0.25-0.32%**, straddling round 897's 0.31% refusal floor — the arithmetic is closed before an implementation is chosen. **`lexLevelHasName` is now CLOSED as a *container* question: both levers measured, both refused — the filter at +0.26%, the container at −0.19%.** **ABLATION: 6 arms, ALL discriminate, five with a failure set contained in no other arm's, three with a uniquely-their-own pin; B3 is caught but NOT separated from B1 (a strict subset), stated not dressed up. TWO ARMS READ ALL-PINS-GREEN ON THE FIRST PASS AND NEITHER WAS A BLIND PIN — both were DEAD ARMS whose edits changed nothing:** B5 guarded on `lexScopes.contains(l)`, always true because `lexLevelHasName` calls `lexScope(l)` two lines above `lexAmp`; B4 polluted the array with `existing` keys, but the SourceFile root is the only level carrying an `existing` table past the untrusted-owner rule and the fixture's root bound nothing. ***Round 855 needs the sharper form: `git diff --shortstat` proves the EDIT landed, never that it DOES anything — and in a driver's output a dead arm and a blind pin are the same line.*** **THREE PIN SPLITS IN ONE ROUND, ALL ONE LESSON:** the re-weighting pin first compared against `lexScopeBoundKeys` and failed at 80 against 586 (lib-dominated on a small fixture — round 898's A3 for the third time in four rounds); the size census began as ONE method so B5 and B6 failed the same lone assertion; and its consistency assertion, first written against the CALL count, fired under both defects. **An assertion that fires for two causes separates neither.** **GATES: suite 14,390 -> 14,396 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 6 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters (the expected control); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 714 classes; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid vs round 901's captures on the identical recipe, ALL EIGHT `added=0 removed=0` — a control, run anyway because the hooks sit on the path that decides TS2304. No wall A/B for the eleventh round running, and nothing to A/B.** `docs/perf/lex-level-scan-price.md`._


_**Round 901 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.28): ROUND 899'S LAST UNREFUTED CANDIDATE, `lexLevelHasName`, IS **REAL — TWO INDEPENDENT INSTRUMENTS AGREE ON ITS RATE TO 0.5%** — AND IS **REFUSED AT ~14 ms (0.26%)**. THE CENSUS THAT PRICED IT FOUND THE ROW'S ACTUAL CAUSE: **32,693 `HashMap`s HOLDING 47,490 KEYS BETWEEN THEM, 46.7% OF THEM EMPTY** — WORTH ~0.45%, WHICH THE FILTER WOULD HAVE FORECLOSED.**_ Priced before a line of fix; no production behaviour change. **THE POPULATION IS EXACTLY WHAT ROUND 899 DERIVED — 1,024,959 calls against a predicted ~1.0 M — AND THE DERIVATION STILL COULD NOT HAVE DECIDED IT: 271,684 of the 1,009,275 probe-path calls cost NOTHING**, because `HashMap.getNode` reads `table` BEFORE it hashes the key and a `mutableMapOf()` that was never written keeps `table == null`. *Round 898's law one level down — an operation that short-circuits before it does anything is not one of the operations you divide by*; a census counting probes alone would have manufactured 7-13 ms of prize out of free work. **THE ROW SURVIVES ITS ARITHMETIC AND THEN A SECOND INSTRUMENT: 29.8 ms over 813,571 REAL probes = 36.6 ns**, inside the 20-50 ns band — the second row in this arc (after round 900's candidate (5)) to pass, against 8 of round 894's 9 and 1 of round 899's 6 that did not. But that band was measured on `perFileScope`, whose keys are file PATHS in a populated table, while the mean queried level holds **1.5** entries, so the prior does not transfer (round 789) — `--lexLevelAmp` measures it at **MAP warm slope 6.4 ns, FILTER warm slope 1.17 ns**, with the two-arm delta extrapolating to **33-37 ns for the FIRST probe**, the one production performs. **Two independent instruments agreeing to 0.5%**; sink an exact 4x between r=4 and r=16, so neither loop was elided. *The amplifier amplifies BOTH arms in one call because at equal `r` the ~90 ns boundary cancels BETWEEN them — the only way a first-probe rate is readable at all.* **REFUSED AT ~12.6-15.8 ms (0.23-0.29%)** — 474,954 refusable probes at ~2.3% false positives, minus a filter test on all 737,591 real-probe calls and a 0.5 ms eager build — **for three reasons in ascending weight: (a)** below this arc's floor (897 refused LOW risk at 0.31% gross, 898 MEDIUM at 0.13-0.20%, 900 refused at 0.07-0.14% and built at 0.39%); **(b)** `cost_gate.py` reads **+0.00% by construction** since it removes probes and not resolutions, so its only defence would be a wall A/B at a SEVENTH of what this box settles (round 899 resolved 1.88% in SIGN alone); **(c) THE REAL ONE — a filter in front of a container is a commitment to the container**, and refusing 58% of those probes banks the smaller half while removing the justification for replacing the container. **THE FLAGGED RISK WAS NOT THE PROBLEM:** the filter would sit BELOW INV.4(c)(ii)'s three load-bearing rules and guard ONE map probe whose answer it proves, and `LexicalScope.symbols` has **exactly one writer in the repo** (`Binder.kt` `declareLexical`), so a bind-time mask cannot go stale. *The soundness argument was fine; the number was not.* **THE SUCCESSOR, PRICED FROM THIS ROUND'S CENSUS:** bound scopes by own-symbol count `15270 8381 3748 1907 1171 768 456 394 174 424` — **46.7% hold ZERO**, 93.2% hold <=4, **98.7% hold <=8** — so a parallel-array linear scan (map fallback above ~8) serves **794,251 real probes** at ~3-6 ns instead of 33-37 = **~22-25 ms = 0.41-0.47%**, clearing every floor this arc has used. NOT built: the scan's own rate is ESTIMATED and the next instrument is a third `--lexLevelAmp` arm, not a fix. **ABLATION: 6 arms, ALL discriminate, FOUR uniquely; A1 and A4 are caught but NOT separated (strict subsets), stated not dressed up. TWO WERE BLIND FIRST TIME — A2 because the FIXTURE could not express the invariant** (round 898's A3: `queried <= bound` is vacuous where the lib binding dominates the bound count; the discriminating form is the STRICT inequality against the probes), **and A4 because ONE SHARED SINK CANNOT TELL A DROPPED ARM FROM A RUNNING ONE** — splitting it per arm catches it and buys the assertion that matters most, that the filter is a SUPERSET of the map and can never sink less, which IS the proof-of-absence property and is what A6 tests. **And the driver called a blind arm a `compile error`**: Gradle prints `N tests completed, M failed` only when something FAILED, so its ABSENCE is a green run. **GATES: suite 14,379 -> 14,390 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 11 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters (the expected control); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 714 classes; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid vs round 900's captures on the identical recipe, ALL EIGHT `added=0 removed=0` — a CONTROL this round, run anyway because the hooks sit on the path that decides TS2304. No wall A/B for the tenth round running, and nothing to A/B.** `docs/perf/lex-level-probe-price.md`._


_**Round 900 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.27): ROUND 899'S CANDIDATE (5) IS THE **FIRST JFR ROW IN THIS ARC WHOSE ARITHMETIC CONFIRMS IT** — 767,521 inserts at **28.1 ns** each — AND THE COUNTER THAT CONFIRMED IT ALSO FOUND WHY THEY EXIST: **A PROBE ARGUMENT HAD BEEN MATERIALISING ROUND 801'S LAZY VIEWS ON EVERY PRODUCTION COMPILE FOR NINETY-NINE ROUNDS.** CANDIDATE (1) **REFUSED** at 84.3 ns per `Integer`-keyed probe.**_ Both populations measured before a line of fix. **CANDIDATE (5), AND THE BINARY WAS POSED OVER THE WRONG QUANTITY:** round 899 said ~0.5-1.0 M `HashSet.add`s is implausible for a set built once per file and plausible for one rebuilt per query, so one counter decides it — the counter says **767,521 names across 1,143 sets**, i.e. **21.6 ms / 767,521 = 28.1 ns per add**, exactly a `HashSet.add` with a cached `String` hash. **The row survives its own plausibility test — the only one of round 899's six and round 894's nine that has.** But the sets ARE built once each; they are simply HUGE, mean **671**. *A count of builds does not bound the work a build does — for a 100%-insert row the deciding quantity is INSERTS, and the binary named neither outcome.* **THE LEVER IS THE THIRD COUNTER: THE SUFFIXES OF ONE SCAN ARE NESTED** — 1,143 suffixes are cut from **1,220 cached scans holding 15,331 names in total**, each re-inserting the same tail, so membership is a comparison against the scan's LAST occurrence (`e in suffix(lo) <=> max{k : names[k]==e} >= lo`) and ONE lazily-built index per scan answers all of them; LAST and not first is load-bearing, since a name reassigned both before and after a closure is the shape the structure exists for. **AND THE FIRST BUILD STILL READ `materialized 1143`, WHICH IS THE ROUND'S REAL FINDING:** `FrontEnd.addClosureCensus(reassigned.size.toLong())` — the guard `if (mode != ON) return` is INSIDE the function and **Kotlin evaluates arguments strictly**, so it never got the chance to run, and asking a lazy view its size materialises it. The (FRONT.2) probe was building all 1,143 hash sets on **every production compile with the probe OFF**; round 801 created `SuffixNameSet` to stop exactly that and read its own census (`created 1143, materialized 1143`) as *every set is eventually asked*, concluding the work MOVED. **The asker was the instrument.** Post-fix: **`created 1143, materialized 0, inserted 0`**, with **192 of 1,220** scans ever questioned. *A probe that must be free when off is not free when off if its ARGUMENT does the work.* **PRICE, WITH ITS DEFLATIONS STATED: 767,521 `HashSet.add` -> 0, replaced by 11,619 `HashMap.put` = 755,902 inserts removed = ~21.2 ms = ~0.39%** of a 5,429 ms rebuild at the rate the row and the population agree on — a rate DERIVED from the JFR row, so a residual attribution bias deflates it proportionally, and no wall A/B is attempted at a fifth of what round 899's 12/12 sign test could resolve. **The claim is the deterministic population and the arithmetic on it.** **CANDIDATE (1) REFUSED ON ITS OWN ARITHMETIC:** `resolveImportedSymbolGeneral` is a genuine `containsKey`-then-`get` double probe, and the census says **259,739 calls, all top-level, 251,380 hits (96.8%), 511,119 map probes** — not the 0.7-1.5 M the 21.9 ms `containsKey` row needs, i.e. **84.3 ns per `Integer`-keyed probe** against a 15-30 ns reference: **over-read ~3x, round 898's law for the ninth time.** The removable half is **3.8-7.5 ms = 0.07-0.14%**, below round 897's 0.31% refusal and at/below round 898's 0.13-0.20%; and it is not the five lines it looks, because the value is `Symbol?` and `containsKey` is precisely what separates *absent* from *cached null*, so one probe needs a SENTINEL — a correctness-carrying construct for 0.07-0.14%. Recorded without a price: `visited = mutableSetOf()` allocates on all 259,739 calls and 251,380 never touch it, but *an allocation count is not a cost* (round 801). **CANDIDATE (2) `lexLevelHasName` NOT STARTED** — ranked below both, unrefuted, remains the top open item. **ABLATION: 5 arms, ALL discriminate, 4 with a uniquely-their-own pin; A1 (first-occurrence index) is caught but NOT separated from A2 (`> lo`) — its failures are a strict subset — stated rather than dressed up. A3 (index not shared) read a CLEAN SWEEP on the first pass because sharing changes no ANSWER, only how many times the work is done** (round 897's A1 verbatim), so every membership pin stayed green, correctly; **the repair is a COUNTER pin** (`indexesBuilt <= scansBuilt`, `indexEntries <= scanNames`, both true by construction). **GATES: suite 14,372 -> 14,379 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 7 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters (the expected control — a change deleting hash-set inserts touches no resolution); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid vs round 898's captures on the identical recipe, ALL EIGHT `added=0 removed=0` — a real gate this time, not a control; `--verifyFlowScan` 1,220 scans compared, 0 diverged.** `docs/perf/suffix-name-index.md`._


_**Round 899 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.26): THE CUMULATIVE WARM A/B OF ROUNDS 895-898 — **B FASTER IN 12/12 PAIRS, BOTH BATCHES 6/6 ON OPPOSITE ROTATIONS, SIGN-TEST p = 0.0005 — BUT THE EFFECT IS SMALLER THAN ONE ARM'S sd, SO THE *DIRECTION* IS ESTABLISHED AND THE *MAGNITUDE* IS NOT.**_ No production code changed. **THE PRIOR WAS STATED BEFORE THE RUN:** rounds 895-898 bank ~82-115 ms by counters (895's scan gating -64.3 ms, 896's `nodeToFlow` -17.9, 896's `perFileScope` 6.4-33; 897/898 are refusals plus flag-gated-OFF instruments) = **1.5-2.1% of a ~5.4 s rebuild**, against per-arm sds round 893 measured at 2.21%/3.44% — so "not resolvable, the counters remain the claim" was a pre-declared acceptable outcome. **RESULT (round 893's protocol verbatim — arms `63819970` and HEAD, one JVM per SAMPLE, WARMUP=6/ITERS=8, two batches with OPPOSITE leading arms, box quiesced and left alone): A median 5,418.4 ms (sd 2.55%), B 5,242.6 ms (sd 2.51%); B wins 12/12; pooled median paired -1.88%, mean -2.48%, per-pair range [-6.51%, -0.70%] never crossing zero, exact two-sided sign test p = 0.0005; batch 1 6/6 (-1.61%), batch 2 6/6 (-1.88%).** **AND THE MAGNITUDE IS WHERE THIS SAYS NO: the three central estimators disagree by 1.7x (-1.88% / -2.48% / -3.25% median-of-medians), the paired range spans a factor of nine, and — unlike round 893 — the effect is SMALLER than one arm sd, so CLAUDE.md's "many times the sd" override cannot be invoked at all. The defensible claim is "roughly 1.5-3%, direction certain, point estimate not resolvable on this box".** **A CROSS-ROUND ANCHOR CAME OUT UNUSUALLY WELL AND IS LUCK, NOT A LAW: arm A differs from round 893's arm B by docs and two script lines, and the two sessions measure it at 5,424 and 5,418 ms — 0.11% apart, where CLAUDE.md prices the anchor at up to 12.8% of drift; it lets the blocks be laid side by side: 5,859 (pre-887) -> 5,424/5,418 (pre-895) -> 5,243 (HEAD) = -10.5% over rounds 887-898.** **SAME ANSWERS, DIFFERENT CODE:** all 192 measured rebuilds 78 files / 46 errors; both arms' `--listAll` digest `59d930db849399aea5e03e25fedb8e4e` with a zero-line diff and no truncation; 694 vs 711 classes with `SourceScanFilter`/`MapCensus` in B only, asserted BEFORE a sample ran. **THE SIXTH LEAF PROFILE RECONCILES THREE INSTRUMENTS FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS ARC** (recipe identical to 888/893; 15,869 samples, max depth 182/170 vs the 512 cap, `checkSpine` inclusive 74.09%/74.00%, denominator 5,429 ms): per OWNER, `nodeToFlow` -42.6 gross but **+19.1 back as `LongKeyMap` frames = -23.5 net**, `perFileScope` **-36.3**, the scan gating **~-52** — **total ~-112 ms = 2.1%**, against the A/B's -1.88% and the counters' 1.5-2.1%. **A container swap must be priced NET: 45% of `nodeToFlow`'s gross came straight back.** By family the map family falls 1,300.7 -> 1,136.5 ms and String/StringBuilder 222.5 -> 147.8 — exactly the two the rounds targeted; `HashMap$TreeNode` is 0 samples in 15,869 for the second round running; by ROW the top owner is `cpaSpineLeave` at **1.81%** and nothing else clears 1.3%, round 874's law for the SIXTH take, with no C2 key split behind any large delta. **THE RANKED LIST — SIX, EACH THROUGH ROUND 898'S ARITHMETIC FILTER (ms ÷ population -> is the implied per-op cost physically possible?): (1) `resolveImportedSymbolGeneral` 24.3 ms, a PROVEN `containsKey`-then-`get` double probe on an Int-keyed BOXED cache — real only at ~0.7-1.5 M probes/rebuild, population unknown, first instrument is a counter; ~8-15 ms, LOW, same-answers. (2) `lexLevelHasName` 30.1 ms — NOT a double probe (two maps per level, O(depth) ascent); ~1.0 M probes at ~30 ns is plausible against `globals.lookups` 748,522, so NOT refuted; ~8-12 ms, MEDIUM, grid. (3) `getTypeFromTypeNodeCore` 57.1 ms, the largest map owner — 354 k ops -> **161 ns/op**, plausible ONLY because the key is a data class with a recursive `hashCode` (round 471); deletable part is the deep hash, ~10-20 ms, HIGH risk (cache SHARING, `typeNode.cacheHits`, program order, round 787). (4) REFUSED HERE ON ARITHMETIC: the two UNCENSUSED whole-map copies `spineArithFnFrame` + `spineCaCopyTop` = 42.0 ms JFR, which at round 898's measured 2.6-3.4x over-read is ~12-16 ms, below the 0.31% at which round 897 refused a LOW-risk change. (5) `SuffixNameSet.materialize` 21.6 ms, where the arithmetic makes the answer BINARY — ~0.5-1.0 M adds is implausible for a set built once per file and plausible for one rebuilt per query; one counter decides it. (6) `Integer.equals` 29.4 ms of key-side leaf work is a LOCATION for the residual boxed-Int maps, no owner named.** **NOT ON THE LIST, AND THE RULE IT SETS: `lookupPerFileForNode` is the second largest map owner (40.7 ms) and its arithmetic is textbook (1,063,149 probes -> 29 ns each) — it is absent because the LEVER is absent. A real cost with no known lever is CLOSED, not open.** **tsgo: HALF THE `LinkStore` LEAD WAS ALREADY CLOSED BY ROUND 889 AND THIS ROUND ALMOST RE-RAISED IT** — the GROUPING axis was censused and refused (our top co-access cluster is 0.49%, the bottom is one sample); the KEY axis is open and unmeasured (tsgo keys by `*ast.Node`, an 8-byte ADDRESS; we key `nodeTypes` by the AST VALUE) and IS candidate (3), nothing more. Unpriced. **GATES, STATED IN FULL BECAUSE MOST WERE NOT RUN: no production source changed (`git diff` touches only `scripts/` and `docs/`), so no suite run, no `cost_gate.py`, no `huge_methods.py` and no 8-profile grid were required or run — round 893 (K)'s posture. What WAS run: the two arms' `--listAll` same-answers control, the driver's class-dir positive controls, BenchMain's per-rebuild files/errors abort on all 192 rebuilds, and the profile's depth/thread/inclusive validity checks. Suite stands at 14,372 / 0 / 3.** `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` § 33._


_**Round 898 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.25): ROUND 894'S LAST TWO OPEN CANDIDATES, **BOTH REFUSED**, AND WITH THEM THE WHOLE RANKED LIST — **EIGHT OF ITS NINE CEILINGS ARE NOW MEASURED AND EVERY ONE IS OVER, BY 2.1-21x**. THE ROUND-891-vs-ROUND-894 CONTRADICTION RESOLVES **IN ROUND 891'S FAVOUR**: `EpochMap` copies are **11-15 ms**, not 38.1.**_ **CANDIDATE (8), AND HALF THE CONTRADICTION WAS NEVER ONE:** round 892 removed 124,709 entries from that family, so round 891's derivation **re-stated at the population 894 was looking at** (347,017 entries at 30-51 ns) reads **10.4-17.7 ms**, and the amplifier measures **11.2-14.8 ms**, inside it — *a derivation is a claim about a population as much as about a rate.* **AND 38.1 ms IS REFUTABLE WITH NO BUILD: it is 1,394 ns to copy a 12.6-entry map = 110 ns PER ENTRY**, where a `HashMap` insert with an already-cached `String` hash is tens of ns; the mechanism is CLAUDE.md's round-623 entry (a JFR leaf share is not a wall price, and `HashMap.putMapEntries` is exactly the tight allocating loop that attracts samples). **THE OBVIOUS RESCUE — "the per-entry derivation dropped a fixed per-CALL term" — IS REFUTED BY ITS OWN ARM: `EpochSet` makes 35,015 copies of mean 1.1**, the most call-dominated container of the six, **and its slope is unresolvable (0.5-2.8 ms)**. **CANDIDATE (6) REFUSED, ON A POPULATION THE CENSUS COULD NOT SEE:** `spineArgListOverlay` is **393 copies/rebuild** (365 nested-fn overlays of mean 633 entries + 28 shadow-minus of mean 753) carrying 8,152 writes, measured **12.7 ms = 0.23%** against a 41.2 ceiling; the chained-scope-map replacement trades an O(1) probe for an O(depth) walk, so this round counted the probes — **56,096 `SpineArgCtx` lookups/rebuild, 29,703 (53%) MISSES**, and a miss must walk the chain to the END — which is 1-6 ms back, leaving **~7-11 ms (0.13-0.20%)**: below the **0.31%** at which round 897 refused a LOW-risk change, where this is MEDIUM (TS2554/2555, shadowing bug-compatible with a deleted legacy walker), with **no counter in `cost-counters.txt` able to defend it**. Sharpest row in the census: **the shadow-minus copies 21,086 entries to remove 29 names — 727 per name.** **A NEW COUNTER, BECAUSE `writes/entries` ANSWERS ONLY THE UNDO-LOG QUESTION** (it cannot tell one copy written a hundred times from a hundred written once): charging a copy ONCE on its first write, **6,598 of 27,337 `EpochMap` copies (24.1%) are NEVER WRITTEN and hold 188,774 entries = 54.4% of the volume**, the never-written ones being the BIG ones (mean 28.6 vs 7.6) — so a copy-on-write `EpochMap`, which needs **no LIFO discipline at all**, is worth ~6-8 ms (0.11-0.15%); recorded, refused, since establishing it needs exactly the >=12-site pointer-swap audit round 891 declined. **THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN LESSON: A BALANCED PALINDROME IS NOT ENOUGH.** Batch 1 (three arms, two MIRRORED palindromic rotations, so a LINEAR drift cancels by construction) disagreed **2x on `em`, 4x on `al` and by SIGN on `es`** — one rotation reads 5,869/5,848/5,483/5,544/5,571/5,100, monotone downward, i.e. the warm-up is still running at rebuild 14 and what a palindrome cannot cancel is its NON-linear remainder. **The fix is FEWER ARMS and a bigger `r`, not more rotations**: batch 2 (two arms, r=64, 8 draws, two processes) gave 13.99/15.52 and 11.46/13.93, with **every one of the 16 `r=64` draws above every one of the 16 `r=0` draws**. **ABLATION: 8 arms, all discriminate after repair, 4 with a uniquely-their-own pin; A1/A2 and A3/A4 discriminated as PAIRS and not from each other, stated rather than dressed up. TWO WERE BLIND AND NEEDED DIFFERENT REPAIRS: A8 because the two copy sites in one function SHARED a census family** (the survivor kept the counter non-zero — *a census family that cannot be zero is a census family that cannot be wrong*; the split is also what produced the 727-per-name row), **and A3 because the FIXTURE could not express the invariant** — rounds 891/892 moved the fn-body locals onto `MapScopeStack`, so a single-file compile copies near-EMPTY maps (5 copies, 4 entries, none written twice) and "counted at most once" is vacuous; the pin now ASSERTS the multiplicity it needs. **GATES: suite 14,358 -> 14,372 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 14 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters (the expected CONTROL, and here the statement that hooks on `EpochMap.put` and on the arity read of every call expression are inert); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid vs round 897's captures on the identical recipe, ALL EIGHT `added=0 removed=0`.** **NO WALL A/B — the round lands an instrument and two refusals; the claim is deterministic populations and a slope with an arithmetic falsifier that held on all 60 instrumented rebuilds.** `docs/perf/copy-family-price.md`._


_**Round 896 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.23): TWO OF ROUND 894'S FIVE MAP-KEY CANDIDATES TAKEN, THREE REFUSED WITH A COST — AND THE REFUSALS ARE THE FINDING: **TWO OF THE FIVE CEILINGS ARE AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE ABOVE THE ANSWER**, WHICH THE POPULATION COUNT SETTLES IN ONE DIVISION.**_ All five were priced BEFORE a line of fix (`MapCensus.kt`, `--mapCensus` / `--perFileScopeAmp N` / `--flowMapReplay N`), each with its own shape: a lookup can be amplified and an INSERT cannot — re-putting one key measures an OVERWRITE — so the flow replay fills a FRESH container per rep over each file's real key sequence, ABBA within the file. **TAKEN (3): `nodeToFlow` -> `LongKeyMap`, 32.3 -> 14.4 ms = 17.9 ms/rebuild (0.33%), replicated at 17.6 in a second run over 262,404 keys — round 864's own entry count reached by a different instrument; the ceiling was 46.6 ms.** Its one new failure mode is the KEY and it is a CRASH: `LongKeyMap` reserves `0L` and `nodeKey(0, 0)` IS `0L`, which an error-recovery zero-width node at offset 0 produces — `flowKey` shifts both coordinates by one, still a bijection, moving the sentinel to `(-1, -1)` where `recordFlow` never writes and a synthetic node's read correctly answers null. **And the iteration-order audit is now STRUCTURAL: `LongKeyMap` has no iterator, so the rounds-754/776/778 hazard is a compile error rather than a review item.** **TAKEN (2a): `perFileScope` probed once per name lookup, 650,394 map probes/rebuild -> 6,426 (-99.01%)** — the double probe (`containsKey` then `get`, the file PATH hashed twice) halves READS to 325,236 and a one-entry reference-compared memo answers 318,810 of those (98.0%); a miss is never wrong, only slower, and the one write site clears it. Priced at 10-51 ns/probe (amplification's `cold` against the in-situ empty bracket rounds 734/735 say only BOUNDS the pair) = **6.4-33 ms**, with round 894's independent JFR figure of 53 ns at the top of the range. **REFUSED (4) `symbolTypeResolutionInProgress`: 24,232 adds, MAX LIVE 3** — the 28.2 ms ceiling would be 1,164 ns per add, ~20x a boxed `HashSet` probe on a table that never leaves its 16 initial slots; worth ~2-5 ms. **REFUSED (5) `nodeTypeResolutionInProgress`: 59,283 adds (= `typeNode.cacheable - cacheHits` exactly, an independent confirmation of the hook), 33.5% of that path's deep hashing ~ 3-5 ms — and buying it needs IDENTITY keying, which weakens the only cycle guard there.** **REFUSED (7) `AliasedCondKey`: gated on unbuilt candidate (1); without interned name ids the pack must probe a String-keyed table per key, i.e. the very hash-and-equals being removed (round 788).** **SIX single-mistake ablations: A1/A2/A3 redden 3/2/1 pins with A3's the synthetic-position pin alone; B1/B2 redden 4/7. B3 — the INV.3(d) fall-through — leaves everything GREEN and is a REDUNDANT GUARD, not a blind pin, because INV.3(d) already retired module-only names out of `globals`; the pin was RENAMED to what it does test. A2 also shows what the equivalence pins CANNOT see: the INV.2(b) side table is filled FROM the map, so a key collision makes both paths agree on the same wrong answer.** **NO WALL A/B IS CLAIMED for the eighth round running.** **GATES: suite 14,339 -> 14,350 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 11 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters both times (and for (2a) a real control — `globals.*` measure that very path); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid vs round 895's captures on the identical recipe, ALL EIGHT `added=0 removed=0`.** `docs/perf/warm-hash-owner-census.md` § 11._

_**Round 895 (2026-08-12) — (WARM.19): THE WHOLE-SOURCE `indexOf` FAMILY IS GATED — **488,469,784 CHARACTERS SCANNED PER REBUILD -> 22,894,093**, MECHANISM NANOS **86.2 ms -> 21.9 ms WARM (-64.3 ms = -1.24%)** OVER AN IDENTICAL POPULATION WITH THE SAME 14 HITS — AND A COLD CENSUS WOULD HAVE BEEN WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.**_ Round 894 § 10 found 116 ms/rebuild of whole-source `String.indexOf` in ~50 `Checker.check*` pin walkers, no owner above 0.16%, invisible to `cost_gate.py`. **POPULATION FIRST: 3,827 calls over 488,469,784 characters = 49 whole-program passes, to find 14 needles (0.37%)** — corpus-unique pins that on tsc's own sources can essentially never match. **AND THE SITE COUNT IS NOT THE POPULATION: of 218 sites, 69 are CHAR searches bounded to a node position — the largest single form and almost none of the cost — and are deliberately NOT rewritten**, since one character is below the filter's window width. **MECHANISM: a 4-character-window presence bitset per file; a needle can occur only if EVERY one of its windows occurs, so one clear bit is a PROOF of absence** — if the needle occurs at `p`, window `j` of the needle IS window `p+j` of the text, which the build set. Hash and 7-bit-fold collisions produce only false POSITIVES, and one costs a single scan because **the real `indexOf` remains the oracle**: the filter is consulted to SKIP a call, never to answer one. **Selectivity: 3,723 of 3,827 refused (97.3%), 95.3% of characters, 2.4% false positives.** The cache is length-keyed and identity-probed rather than a `HashMap<String,…>` (which would hash 10 M chars of file text per compile for nothing); a miss is never wrong, only slower. **WARM, BOTH ARMS ALTERNATING IN ONE PROCESS — scan 86.2 -> 3.0 ms, build 0 -> 18.9 ms, NET -64.3 ms; and the two regimes disagree: the JDK intrinsic warms 5.07x while the hand-written build warms 3.49x, so a COLD census OVERSTATES what gating a scan is worth — the exact opposite of round 859, where a cold table UNDERSTATED an ungated `java.util.regex` because that does not warm at all.** **Round 894's 116 ms was right and ~26% of it was never addressable** (the char searches are `String.indexOf` frames in a JFR dump too). **NO WALL A/B IS CLAIMED** — 1.24% is inside what this box settles (`overheadMs` -70/-116/-317/+261) — the claim is the mechanism's own PAIRED nanos on ONE binary with the population and hits identical in both arms. **The rewrite was decided by a parser and verified by INVERSION: 149 sites, 134 lines differ, 0 inversion failures, all 13,725 string literals identical**; the compiler caught 8 named-argument sites and one `Char` that no review would have. **WHAT DID NOT WORK: the first pass rewrote the helpers into calls to themselves (their parameter was also named `source`) — and the partition pin FAILED ON HEAD and was RIGHT, because `tooShort` calls also increment `scanned`; the partition is `refused + scanned == calls` and `tooShort` is a SUBSET.** **ABLATION: `--srcScanBogus` (record every second window) DELETES the gated TS18026 and the pin reddens, so the gate is load-bearing and the pin discriminates; `--verifySrcScan` reads `divergences == 0` sound and `> 0` bogus — a verifier reading 0 in both cases would be worthless (round 790).** **ALSO FOUND: every committed grid harness has been a ONE-profile grid** — `bench-compile-tsc.sh` names the compiler profile `tsc-project-<commit8>` and the other seven `tsc-<name>-<commit8>`, and every script globs the former. **GATES: suite 14,327 -> 14,339 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 12 new pins; 8-PROFILE `--listAll` grid, both arms of one binary, ALL EIGHT `added=0 removed=0`; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 (the EXPECTED control, round 876 — which is precisely why the grid and the corpus are the real gates here); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit.** `docs/perf/whole-source-scan-census.md`._

_**Round 893 (2026-08-12) — THE CUMULATIVE WARM A/B OF ROUNDS 887-892: **-8.18% MEDIAN PAIRED, B FASTER IN 12/12 PAIRS, BOTH BATCHES 6/6 ON OPPOSITE ROTATIONS.** THE ARC'S **FIRST DEMONSTRATED WARM SPEED-UP** — SIX ROUNDS EACH CORRECTLY DECLINED TO CLAIM ONE, AND THE AGGREGATE CLEARS THE FLOOR BY A WIDE MARGIN.**_ Each of rounds 887-892 landed real removed work at ~0.5-1%, below what this box settles (rounds 840(c)/858/886 each recorded a disagreeing second batch at that size), so the arc's wall effect was UNMEASURED and each round's claim was an accounting one. **THE PROTOCOL IS THE ROUND:** two BUILDS (`6a4e3612` = parent of 887, `abf184ee` = HEAD), **one JVM per SAMPLE** (round 867), `WARMUP=6` (the 2026-08-10 calibration — two identical arms sit 3.3% apart at warm-up 2 and 0.8% at 6), sample = that process's median, **two batches of six pairs with OPPOSITE leading arms** so each arm leads exactly 6 of 12; box quiesced before measuring (round 851) and left alone throughout (round 774). **RESULT: A median 5,859 ms, B 5,424 ms; B wins 12/12; median paired delta -8.18% (-479 ms); per-pair range [-12.03%, -2.81%], never crossing zero; batch 1 6/6 median -7.32%, batch 2 6/6 median -9.51%.** **BOTH ARM sds ARE ABOVE THE ~1% QUIET-BOX THRESHOLD (2.21% / 3.44%) AND THIS SAYS SO RATHER THAN HIDING IT** — the effect is ~2.4x the larger sd, which is NOT the "tens of times" case CLAUDE.md's override is written for, so what carries the verdict is the SIGN: 12/12 is 1-in-4,096 under the null, the per-pair range lies wholly below zero, and the two batches replicate with the rotation reversed. **-8.18% is not a point estimate to quote precisely; the defensible claim is a real speed-up of roughly 5-10%.** **CONTROLS: both arms answer the same program on every one of the 192 measured rebuilds (78 files / 46 errors, `--listAll` digest `59d930db849399aea5e03e25fedb8e4e` for both, zero-line diff, no truncation) from structurally distinct binaries (694 vs 688 classes; `MapScopeStack`/`SetScopeStack`/`SpineMask` only in B, asserted as positive controls before a sample ran — round 853). Same answers, different code.** **THE EXCESS OVER THE SUMMED PRICES (~3-5%) IS THE ROUND'S OTHER PRODUCT.** **GC IS REFUTED ON BUDGET, NOT ON SIGN:** one `-Xlog:gc` process per arm reads A 68 pauses / 1,375 ms, B **86 pauses / 1,287 ms** — B is faster while doing MORE GC — and the whole pause budget is **~92-98 ms per rebuild = ~1.7%**, an order of magnitude below the effect, so eliminating GC entirely could not produce 8%. **THE FIFTH WARM LEAF PROFILE FINDS THE MECHANISM: `HashMap$TreeNode` as a leaf was 5.91% = 348.8 ms/rebuild at round 888 and is 0.00% — ZERO samples in 16,055 — at round 893.** Rounds 889/890 did not make lookups cheaper by a little; they removed an entire mechanism (of that 348.8 ms, ~89 came back as ordinary bucket probing and **~260 ms is gone**) — and **round 890 priced its own change at ~0.5%, i.e. under-read ~9x**, because an amplifier counts map OPERATIONS while treeified-bucket cost is superlinear in depth. By owner family in ms/rebuild: `cta*` **216.0 -> 84.3** (rounds 891/892, ~1.6x under-read), `flow-graph build` **207.2 -> 134.0** and `module/import resolution` **192.3 -> 133.9** (round 889's `nodeKey` finalizer); no single OWNER row exceeds ~1%, so round 874's "the ROW is the wrong unit" holds for the fifth take running. **WHAT IS NOT CLAIMED: the profile spans 889-892 while the A/B spans 887-892, it is a CROSS-ROUND absolute comparison (up to 12.8% of drift on identical code), and a JFR leaf share is still not a wall price — so the table RANKS mechanisms and only the paired A/B MEASURES the arc. Non-additivity under a shifting denominator (round 870) stays open: the excess is largely ATTRIBUTED, not yet EXPLAINED.** **GATES: no production code changed — no suite run, `cost_gate.py` or `huge_methods.py` was required; suite stands at 14,324 / 0 / 3.** `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` § 32.

_**Round 892 (2026-08-11) — (WARM.18b): THE FAMILY ROUND 891 REFUSED IS **CONVERTED** — `CtaFrame`'s localTypes+declNodes+shadowed AND the narrowing frame's `EpochMap`, **1,214,236 ENTRIES COPIED PER REBUILD -> 28,695 UNDO RECORDS OVER AN IDENTICAL POPULATION** — AND ALL THREE REFUSAL REASONS DISSOLVED UNDER THE INSTRUMENT ROUND 891 QUEUED BUT DID NOT BUILD.**_ Round 891 named the instrument this family needed — *"a counting facade over `CtaFrame.localTypes`, run for one census, BEFORE any code"* — and this round built exactly that, first. **THE CENSUS** (throwaway, over EVERY write path, reverted before a line of the fix; a hook anywhere else is round 891's own 2-of-3-paths hook, which read 6.3x optimistic): **28,695 writes needing an undo record against 1,089,527 entries copied = 2.6%**, plus 567 free root writes, **zero** `putAll` outside the seed and **zero** `clear()` calls. **THE THREE REFUSALS, AND TWO WERE ARTEFACTS OF NOT HAVING MEASURED:** (i) the ratio now exists; (ii) **`ambiguousNames` RESETS rather than shadows — true, and IRRELEVANT: it is not in the family.** A fn frame gives it a FRESH EMPTY set, O(1); it was never COPIED, so there is nothing to replace — **the refusal treated a sibling FIELD as a sibling COST**; (iii) the narrowing frame's `EpochMap` was the one real reason, and its answer is to convert it TOO, onto the SAME stack — not optional, since a function declared in a then-branch takes its base from `ctaFrames.last()`, and worth another 124,709 entries. **CONDITIONS re-audited over all 423 references:** strictly LIFO with a per-file `reset`; **no `.keys`/`.values`/`.entries`/`.forEach`/`.iterator`/`.sorted`/`.toMutableMap` reader and no `for (x in map)` anywhere**; 0 `clear()` measured; and the retention question resolves — 37 local POINTER SWAPS (object identity stable) plus exactly two FIELD retentions, both capturing the PRE-SPINE resting map, never a cta frame's. One stated divergence: the `onMutate` epoch bump is a SUPERSET of what the replaced maps did, which is the safe direction for a probe-only fence. **12-ARM ABLATION, every non-zero red set DISTINCT, five arms with a uniquely-their-own pin — AND ITS REAL PRODUCT IS THAT IT FOUND A MISSING PIN: A9, the arm for the invariant that DECIDED the round, reddened NOTHING** (no fixture had an `if`); the first replacement pin then FAILED ON HEAD because this compiler emits nothing for `const s: string = <string | undefined>`, so the working shape (`typeof` over `string | number`) was verified against an un-narrowed control in the CLI loop before either assertion was written. **A10/A11 still redden nothing for STRUCTURAL reasons** (no fixture drives declNodes/shadowedNames across a fn boundary; `diagnose()` is single-file so a cross-FILE leak is inexpressible), and **12 of 32 pins are reddened by no arm and are recorded as INVARIANT GUARDS, not counted as coverage**. **NO WALL-TIME A/B IS CLAIMED** — ~0.8-1.1%, inside what this box settles. **GATES: suite 14,308 -> 14,324 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 16 new pins (7 more with the ablation's repair); compiler-profile `--listAll` digest `59d930db849399aea5e03e25fedb8e4e` over 46 errors; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 694 classes.** `docs/perf/cta-frame-copy-families.md` §§ 6-11._

_**Round 891 (2026-08-11) — (WARM.18): THE `CtaFrame.varTypes` PER-SCOPE COPY BECOMES AN UNDO LOG — **1,145,523 ENTRIES COPIED PER REBUILD -> 16,182 UNDO RECORDS OVER AN IDENTICAL POPULATION** — AND THE OTHER THREE FAMILIES ARE REFUSED WITH A PRICED CONDITION TABLE, INCLUDING THE ONE THAT IS THE BIGGER PRIZE.**_ Round 869 took two of the six per-scope copy families and stopped, because the rest are where a wrong scope does not crash — it silently resolves a name to an OUTER binding. This round ran the item in CLAUDE.md's order: **conditions first, price second, code third.** **CONDITIONS:** `CtaFrame.varTypes` is ONE strictly-LIFO stack (`ctaFrames`, one frame per owner node, `reset` per file); of 217 references the only mutations of a frame map are two `[k] = v` writes and one `putAll`, there is **no removal** and **no `.keys`/`.values`/`.entries`/`.forEach`/`.iterator`/`.sorted` reader anywhere**, and all ~15 legacy call sites read it synchronously inside one spine dispatch. **PRICE, on the binary that still had it** (three new per-family amplifier arms; no timestamp pair anywhere): **~33 ms = 0.59%** of a warm rebuild. **AND THE MEASUREMENT LESSON: two batches of the SAME family on the SAME binary with OPPOSITE rotations read 53.6 and 14.1 ms/rep — a 4x disagreement**, because at two draws per arm the leading draw's ~15% lands wholly on whichever arm ran first; pooling the 12 draws gives 33.9 and discarding each batch's leading draw gives 32.6 with the sub-intervals agreeing to 2.5%. The amplifier is the change's own falsifier — re-run on the new binary it reads **`ampSink 0`**, i.e. nothing left to amplify. **THE BIGGER PRIZE IS REFUSED AND THIS IS NOT A RANKING BY SIZE:** `CtaFrame.localTypes` prices at 0.8-1.0% and is refused because its produced-vs-consumed ratio is round 849's **un-instrumented** zero, its sibling `ambiguousNames` is a **RESET not a shadow** (O(size) for an undo log), and its narrowing-frame copy lands in the `EpochMap` family — which is refused outright for **not being one stack at all** (three spine stacks plus >=12 ad-hoc `currentLocalTypes = EpochMap(currentLocalTypes)` sites whose restore is a POINTER SWAP).
