_**Round 849 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.3) CLOSED AS A MEASURED NEGATIVE AT ~0.014-0.08%, AND THE CENSUS THAT MEASURED IT FIRST REPORTED A CONVINCING ZERO FOR THE WRONG REASON.** A prize measurement, taken before any design, exactly as the queue item demanded; nothing optimized. **THE VERDICT: the entire per-request lib-TYPE re-derivation is 71 outermost mint spans costing 1-6 ms against a 7,139-7,316 ms warm rebuild = 0.01-0.08%** — an order of magnitude below even (WARM.2)'s 0.13% and two orders below the ~1% close threshold. **Instrument:** `LibTypeCensus` hooked at the two boundaries the checker ALREADY guards — `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol`'s `declaredTypes` hit/miss and `resolveStructuredTypeMembers`' `properties != null` early return — so round 801's produced-vs-consumed ratio comes FREE rather than as a second instrument; only the OUTERMOST mint is timed, through a depth counter SHARED by both hooks (they recurse into each other, so per-hook depths would double-count), which makes a span INCLUSIVE of everything it caused, i.e. exactly what a served entry would delete. **THE NUMBERS, bit-identical across all four warm draws AND the cold run: 119 lib mints (63 declaredTypes + 56 member tables) against 14,766 non-lib — the lib surface is 0.80% of all mints — producing 15,932 consumptions, a ratio of 133.9 : 1.** So the derivation is already fully amortized WITHIN one request and sharing across requests could only ever delete the production side. A lib mint is genuinely expensive (**93,464 ns/span warm vs 4,606 for a non-lib one, 20x**); there are just 71 of them — round 716's law by COUNT, not by unit cost. **THE STRONGER NEGATIVE, FREE FROM THE SAME RUN, RETIRES THE WHOLE DIRECTION AND NOT ONLY THE LIB SLICE: the entire mint boundary, lib plus user code, is 13,988 spans costing 38-66 ms warm = 0.5-0.9% of the artifact**, so NO cache placed at those two functions can clear the +-1.0% warm A/B band whatever it is keyed on, even in the impossible limit where user types were shareable. Type derivation is simply not where a warm rebuild's time is; round 847 already located that (`checkSpine` 66.0%, top four handlers 33.4%). **The hazard analysis the queue entry demanded is MOOT at this price and must not be re-opened**: round 778's first-touch instantiation context (failure mode byte-identical output — round 607's 51 corpus failures with `--listAll` unchanged), thread-local `Type` id sequences crossing the request boundary (the round-825 `--workers` mechanism), and round 844's 175 merge collisions. **THE ROUND'S BEST FINDING IS THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN DEFECT, CAUGHT BY ITS OWN PIN:** the consumed-side hook first went into `resolveStructuredTypeMembersCore`, whose `properties != null` guard is DUPLICATED one frame up in the wrapper — so the wrapper absorbed every hit, the hooked branch was dead, and the census read `memHitLib = 0`, which would have been written up as *'nothing ever reads a derived lib type back'*: the exact OPPOSITE of the truth (133.9 : 1), and a conclusion that happens to agree with the correct verdict for an entirely wrong reason. **A produced-vs-consumed census keyed on a boundary the CALLER already short-circuits measures nothing at all and reports it as a zero** — the counter-form of round 786's 'open on the WRAPPER' law; CLAUDE.md entry added. **ALSO LANDED, UNRUN: (WARM.4)(b)'s harness.** `BenchMain` now takes `cta`/`cpa`/`arg` tiers plus a `*coarse` twin each, so the three intra-handler section probes — all of them COLD one-shots on record, covering 48.7% of the warm artifact — can be re-taken warm with the boundary priced DIFFERENTIALLY inside one process (round 734). A next round starts at `bash scripts/round849-warm-sections.sh 2`; phase 1 validated that script's whole build -> cost-gate -> JIT-gate -> daemon-stop -> measure path. Calibration it must carry: this round's in-situ empty pair read **96-209 ns warm against 501 ns cold**, independently reproducing round 847's 'a probe boundary is ~1.85x more expensive cold'. **GATES: suite 13,984 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (13,979 baseline + the 5 new pins), counted per module with `xml.etree` over a fully wiped results dir; `cost_gate.py` **all 18 counters +0.00%**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **exit 0**, census still zero. `docs/perf/lib-type-rederivation.md`._

_**Round 848 (2026-08-08) — (SERVE.1) CLOSED: THE CLI ARGUMENT LOOP NO LONGER LEAKS DEBUG MODES ACROSS `--serve` REQUESTS, AND THE QUEUE ENTRY'S "SEVERAL OF THESE CHANGE WHAT THE COMPILER DECIDES" IS NOW MEASURED — WITH A DIFFERENT ANSWER.** `runCli` wraps its whole body in `try { … } finally { modes.restore() }` over a `ModeLedger`: `modes.set(Obj::field, v)` records the value the field HELD, so the restore is exact by construction (round 619: never assign a default back — `ArgNarrowGate.mode` defaults to ON, not to its zero value) and there is no second list to keep in step. **The queue named six unrestored flags; the audit found FIFTEEN**, including two nobody had listed — `SpineDispatch.mode == GATED` (only `PROBE` was ever cleared) and `CallSections.preGateProbe`, which forces the call prologue to run for every call. The argument loop is extracted as `parseCliArgs` and `printUsage` as `usageText`, which is the flag registry the mechanism pin sweeps; the ad-hoc round-843 restore block and ten per-report `mode = OFF` clears are DELETED, subsumed. **THE MEASUREMENT: all 14 arms are byte-identical on the compiler profile — 46 lines, md5 `4090b73e`, added=0 removed=0 — INCLUDING `--flowScanBogus`, which exists to corrupt the fast scanner**, so the profile cannot classify these at all (round 753's law). Escalated to the corpus: `FlowScan.bogus` defaulted TRUE gives **13,902 tests → exactly 1 failure, its own `FlowScanEquivalenceTest`** — every one of the ~13,900 baselines green with a corrupted scanner. So the honest split is three-way: ONE leak with a measured, always-visible effect (`PartitionCheck.reportLines`, re-printed by every later request) plus round 843's instrumentation COST leak; ELEVEN decision-altering by CONSTRUCTION with no discriminating shape known to either instrument (they were built as equivalence baselines, so agreeing is what they were designed for — and round 792's law says a green corpus bounds a hazard's frequency, never its existence); and THREE resource/threading (`--workers`, `--partitionCheck`, `--flowEagerSet`) the corpus cannot see by construction. Pins: `ModeLedgerTest` (4), `CliModeRestoreTest` (2 — JVM reflection over every declared non-final field of all 16 mode objects, non-vacuous three ways), `CompileServerModeLeakTest` (2 — end to end through `CompileServer.respondTo`, reading the flag OBJECTS). Five one-mistake-at-a-time ablations, each attributed to the pin it reddens. Gates: **jvmTest 13,979 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (13,971 + 8 new pins), `huge_methods --fail-over 0` census **0** (`parseCliArgs` 4,306 bytecodes), `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 18 counters. **[CORRECTION, round 853, 2026-08-08: the md5 `4090b73e` above was captured through a stale pre-module-split classpath AND under a fourth digest recipe. Round 853 re-ran all 14 arms on a guard-verified binary — every one 46 lines, added=0 removed=0, md5 `84bbe7f0…` — so the finding STANDS; and `4090b73e` reproduces exactly on that verified capture under `s#.*/src/#src/#`, so the number was never wrong either, only unlabelled.]**_

_**Round 847 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.4): THE FIRST WARM PER-KIND / PER-HANDLER ATTRIBUTION OF `checkSpine`, WHICH IS 66% OF THE WARM ARTIFACT AND HAD NEVER BEEN OPENED WARM. THE KIND SHAPE IS REGIME-INVARIANT; THE HANDLER ORDER IS NOT; AND CLAUDE.md's STANDING SIX-HANDLER LIST IS STALE BY 53% ON ITS LARGEST ROW.** An instrument round — nothing optimized, no lever landed. New tier: `BenchMain`'s 4th argument accepts `dispatch`, which is NOT a `PassTiming` tier but runs round 732's `SpineDispatch.PROBE` for one rebuild, so `spine,dispatch,spine,dispatch` takes two draws of each inside ONE warm process. **Nothing under `commonMain` changed** (the whole change is `commonTest/kotlin/BenchMain.kt` + two scripts), which is what let the round be spent on measurement. One binary, the 78-file compiler profile, daemons stopped inside the measuring script, box unwatched; all 11 instrumented rebuilds answered 78 files / 46 errors. Warm probe-free medians **8,176.7 / 8,013.7 ms** (mean 8,095.2, a THIS-ROUND absolute). **SUB-ROWS (warm n=4 / cold n=1): enter 3,102 (58.5%), leave 1,586 (29.9%), ures 490, forEachChild 86, scope 42; SUM 5,307 ms; enter:leave 1.96** (round 846's independent `rows`-tier reading was 2.01 — reproduced). cold/warm: enter 3.62x, leave 3.28x, ures 2.76x, and the two near-pure-traversal rows barely warm at all — **forEachChild 1.43x, scope 1.31x** (128 ms combined = 1.6%, a shape fact and not a lever). Partition: sub-rows 5,307 vs the tier's own `checkSpine` row 5,990 = **88.6%**, the gap being the tier's boundaries at 797 ns/node — and COLD the same boundary costs **1,475 ns/node**, i.e. **a probe boundary is ~1.85x more expensive cold**, which is the mechanism behind round 846's ratio-compression correction. **PER-KIND: `CALL_EXPRESSION` 1,040 ms (23.8% of the spine, 19.8 us/node over 52,509 nodes), `BLOCK` 654, `VARIABLE_STATEMENT` 515 (35.0 us/node, the most expensive kind), `IDENTIFIER` 459 (over 381,670 nodes at 1.2 us), `EXPRESSION_STATEMENT` 386, `RETURN_STATEMENT` 366. The five statement anchors are 9.9% of the nodes and 39.9% of the warm spine against 40.0% cold on the same binary** — CLAUDE.md's cold record reproduces to 0.1 points, so it is a property of the compiler, not of the cold JVM (and it remains a LOCATION, not a lever). **Warm-up is NOT ordered by cost, confirmed on a second instrument**: the most expensive kind per node warms 4.02x, the second most expensive (`BLOCK`) 2.67x — the worst in the table — the cheapest 3.60x, the best 4.15x; scaling a cold per-kind cost by one factor is wrong by up to +-25%. **PER-HANDLER — THE DELIVERABLE: `ccetSpineLeave` 876 ms = 18.2%, `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` 853 = 17.7%, `cpaSpineLeave` 617 = 12.8%, `ctaSpineEnter` 359 = 7.5%; top four = 2,705 ms = 56.2% of the probed spine and 33.4% of the whole warm artifact**, top six 63.0%. Partition check, the strongest available: the handler nets sum to **4,812 ms against the `spine` tier's independently measured enter+leave of 4,688 = 102.6%** — two probes sharing no code, 2.6% apart. **THE ORDER SWAPS BETWEEN REGIMES:** cold #1 is `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` (19.1%), warm #1 is `ccetSpineLeave`, because the latter warms **2.75x** against the spine's 3.38x; `ctaSpineEnter` rises 5.4% -> 7.5% on the same mechanism (2.28x). **A handler's warm share is its cold share x (spine warm-up / its own), and those differ by up to 1.5x — so a cold handler table cannot be read as a warm one.** **CLAUDE.md's six-handler list is CORRECTED: `cpaSpineLeave` is 2,060 ms cold, down 53% from the recorded 4,366** (rounds 788-795 landed levers in it, as the round-830 entry predicts) **and is THIRD, not first; the top six hold 60.9% cold / 63.0% warm, not 71%**; `ccetSpineEnter` dropped out and `spineArithEnterNode` entered. **WHERE THE COST SITS (handler x kind): `ccetSpineLeave` is 92.5% `CALL_EXPRESSION` — 839 ms net = 10.4% of the warm artifact, the single largest object in it**; `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` is 93.0% in three kinds (VARIABLE_STATEMENT 39.2%, RETURN_STATEMENT 29.7%, EXPRESSION_STATEMENT 24.1%) = 10.1%; `ctaSpineEnter` is 62.7% `BLOCK`; `cpaSpineLeave` alone is genuinely spread (largest kind 20.0%). **What that does NOT license:** round 733 already opened both `...SpineLeave` handlers cold and found 88.4% of their time is the cpa/ccet passes' own checking work, so ~776 of `ccetSpineLeave`'s 876 warm ms is type-system work and only ~100 ms scaffolding. **(DISPATCH.1) RE-PRICED WARM AND STAYS CLOSED: 340-362 ms upper bound warm at 10-11 ns per skipped consultation vs 1,000-1,019 ms cold at 31 ns**, over the same 32,006,965 skipped consultations = 6.6% of the warm spine vs 5.6% cold; **a skipped consultation warms 2.95x, essentially the spine's own 3.38x, so the table's relative value is REGIME-INVARIANT**, and round 732's own 11-34% realisation discount puts it at ~40-120 ms = 0.5-1.5%, straddling the +-1.0% warm band. **NOT MEASURED, named rather than implied: (WARM.3) IS NOT SIZED** — lib type re-derivation lives inside these handlers and no row separates it; the cheapest honest instrument is a counter+timer around `resolveInterfaceMembers` keyed on lib-file declarations, read on the SECOND of two consecutive warm rebuilds, which is a `commonMain` change and a full gate cycle and was not started. Also unmeasured: any A/B, the other seven profiles, emit mode, and a cold `spine` draw beyond n=1. **GATES: suite 13,971 / 0 / 3, unchanged** (wiped per-module results dirs, `xml.etree`); `cost_gate.py` and `huge_methods.py` **not run and not required** — nothing under `commonMain` changed and `BenchMain` carries no `@Test`. **No pin was added, stated rather than omitted:** the change is a measuring harness whose claim is a measurement and whose repeatability is the committed `scripts/round847-warm-spine.sh` + `scripts/round847_analyze.py`. `docs/perf/warm-spine-attribution.md`._

_**Round 846 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.1)(c) CLOSED: `--passTiming` NOW HAS THREE TIERS, THE `rows` TIER IS FREE (+0.25% cold, 0.0% warm), AND THE PROBE'S LANDING SITE IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN BRACKETED.** An instrument round — nothing optimized; the deliverable is a warm attribution a lever can be sized against. None of the probe's ~2.6 s is the ~417 `pass()` boundaries: it is the per-CALL bookkeeping (574,620 `getTypeOfExpression` hooks each doing a distinct-keyed `HashSet` add + a by-pass `HashMap` + a shadow-memo probe + a timestamp pair; the `InstrumentedSymbolTable` on the hottest map in the program; every fenced-setter no-op; two BOXED `HashMap<Int,Long>` entries per node over 856,962 nodes). Tiers: `rows` (pass rows + checker-init, PRODUCTION spine walk) / `spine` (+ SPINE sub-rows) / `full` (unchanged; both flags default TRUE, so `--passTiming` and `cost_gate.py` are bit-for-bit unchanged). `BenchMain`'s 4th argument takes a tier LIST, so `rows,spine,full,rows,spine,full` is the differential inside ONE warm process. **CALIBRATION** — `overheadMs` vs each process's own probe-free median (n=4): rows **−113 ms**, spine +336, full +2,657; cold, 6 interleaved one-shots: probe-free 26,843 / rows 26,910 (**+0.25%**) / full 29,461 (+9.75%). **WHERE IT LANDS (measured both regimes): 99.5–99.8% in checker-init, 101–109% in `checkSpine`** — so § 3.1's bracket (A) is right and (B) refuted. **THE CORRECTED WARM TABLE (rows tier, n=4): wall 8,083 ms; checker-init 7,184.9 = 88.89%; `checkSpine` 5,335.9 = 74.27% of checker-init and 66.0% of wall (enter 3,121 = 38.6%, leave 1,553 = 19.2%, enter:leave 2.01); the other 416 passes 1,849.0 = 22.9%; front end 898.0 = 11.1%.** Cold (n=2): 26,910.5 / 23,246.6 = 86.38% / 18,459.5 = **79.41%** / 4,787.1 / 3,663.9. **So warm `checkSpine`'s share FALLS (79.4 → 74.3%)** where the raw table reads flat and round 843's raw cross-round reading said "rises". Cross-check: bracket (A) applied to this round's own full-tier numbers gives 74.80% against the directly measured 74.27% — two instruments 0.5 points apart. **TWO CORRECTIONS a future round must carry: § 4's warm speed-up column is INVERTED for its two biggest rows** (`checkSpine` warms **3.46×** and the tail **2.59×**, not 2.24× vs 2.38× — a constant additive probe compresses the ratio of exactly the row it lands on), **and the probe's OWN price warms up** (first instrumented rebuild in a process 3,457 ms, second 1,856 ms, sign-consistent in both processes), so round 843's n=1-per-process 3,450–3,945 ms is a FIRST draw. Partition check: the `spine` tier's sub-rows sum to 5,299 ms against the `rows` tier's 5,335.9 ms `checkSpine` row = **99.3%**; the tier's own boundaries are 513 ms = **599 ns/node**. **GATES: suite 13,971 / 0 / 3** (`xml.etree`, wiped dirs; core 13,896 + api 27 + client 18 + daemon 30 — **+7 exactly the new `PassTimingTierTest` pins**), `cost_gate.py` **every counter +0.00%**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**; all 6 `full`-tier runs across three JVMs report identical deterministic counters and every run answered 78 files / 46 errors. `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md` § 10._











_**Round 845 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.1)(b) CONFIRMED: THE (JIT.1) ARC BOUGHT 1.5x ON THE WARM ARTIFACT WHILE ITS OWN COLD INSTRUMENT READ IT AS NOISE.** Round 843 left this an explicit hypothesis; one isolated warm A/B settled it, and the answer changes how a standing gate is read. **THE INTERVAL WAS ISOLATED, which is what makes this an ATTRIBUTION and not a "since round 802" figure:** arm A = `93ff3195`, the immediate PARENT of the first split `d194baca` (census landed, zero splits applied); arm B = `d8ff69b5`, the arc's close. Every `perf(...)` commit in between is a `(JIT.1)` split (the rest are docs, split pins, bench chores), `git diff --name-only` across the interval shows ZERO build/gradle/toml/properties changes, and both arms predate the MOD module split. **The arms were verified in the BYTECODE before being measured: A = exactly 19 methods over 8,000 (matching round 802's census), B = 0.** **THE NUMBER: 4 interleaved pairs, 3 warm-up + 8 measured, per-process medians — A 10,088.0 ms (sd 0.58%) -> B 6,702.9 ms (sd 1.31%), -3,385.1 ms = -33.56%, B wins 4/4, 1.505x**, all 64 measured iterations in both arms at `files=78, errors=46` and both series flat 1->8. Against which the arc's own recorded verdict was round 803's falsifier: **`+0.08%, B wins 3/5, NOISE-DOMINATED`.** **The ~1% quiet-box sd rule was OVERRIDDEN EXPLICITLY, not ignored** — arm B is 1.31%, but the effect is **38x the larger arm sd**, every pair clears it by ~35x, and dropping B's one low outlier gives sd 0.38% and -33.46%; that rule is calibrated for the +-1% regime and this is not it. **THE RESIDUAL keeps round 843 honest:** batch 2, r821 vs HEAD, 3 pairs — 6,481.7 -> 6,620.9 ms, +2.15%, HEAD wins 0/3, i.e. at 1.9x the arm sd **zero warm compute movement between round 821 and today**. So 843's -39.5% decomposes as **~10% r771->r802 (NOT attributable to code — it spans the 4-core -> 8-core box change), -33.6% r802->r821 ((JIT.1)), ~0% r821->HEAD**; the same r821 binary read 6,702.9 in batch 1 and 6,481.7 in batch 2, a cross-batch drift 3x the within-batch band, which is why only within-batch paired deltas are quoted. **WHAT IT LICENSES, a standing change: `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` is a WARM-PATH gate** — it protects the daemon/`--serve`/AOT artifact far more than the CLI, because a method over 8,000 is never JIT-compiled and so its cost cannot improve with warm-up, which is exactly why the COLD protocol is structurally blind to the class. **Any change that grows a hot method past 8,000 must be A/B'd with `ab-warm.sh`, never `ab-interleaved.sh`** (CLAUDE.md entry added). Incidental, n=1, NOT quotable: cold smoke samples read A 24.7 s vs B 21.7 s, so there is likely a real but much smaller cold component too. **The reusable artifact that made a 40-round-apart warm A/B expressible at all is `WarmBench.java`** — a reflection clone of `BenchMain` linking against ANY arm's `ProjectCompiler` regardless of its `build(...)` arity; `BenchMain` is compiled against one arm's signature and cannot do this, and THAT, not the build, was the real obstacle to ever running this comparison. **GATES: none run and none required** — nothing under `src/` changed; two builds in a throwaway worktree and a measurement. `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md` § 2.2._

_**Round 844 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.2): SHARING THE BOUND LIB STATE ACROSS DAEMON REQUESTS IS WORTH 8.65 ms = 0.13% OF A WARM REBUILD. MEASURED, NOT ESTIMATED; NOTHING BUILT; STOP.** A warm-ONLY candidate, invisible to every attribution table this repo has (all cold): the daemon rebuilds a fresh program per request and `RealLibSnapshots.parseCache` reuses the PARSED libs process-wide, but BINDING them is per-program — free cold, paid on every request warm. Round 772's "retained program state is worthless" verdict is about the USER's files (`export *` barrels ⇒ 77-of-78 closures) and says nothing about the libs. **Priced before anything was designed**, per the round-716 rule that killed three caches in one round. **THE PRIZE:** whole real-lib region **8.65 / 9.86 ms** against a **6,846 / 6,663 ms** rebuild = **0.126% / 0.148%** — `RealLibResolver.resolve` 2.0 (a pure function of `(libNames, target)`, run TWICE per program), `bindLibFiles` **4.9**, identity stamping 1.5, `mergeSymbolTable` 0.09; 45 lib files, 379 top-level statements, 185 merged symbols. Cross-checked by an independent microbench of `bindLibFiles` alone (median **4.53 ms**, 10 runs). Lib-derived setup passes add ≤4.7 ms, so the **ceiling on everything lib-shaped is ~13.4 ms = 0.20%**. **WHY SO SMALL: the prize was already harvested** — `parseCache` shares the *parse*, the expensive half; what is left is a top-level bind over ~400 KB of body-less `.d.ts`. **IT DOES NOT MERELY MOVE** (round 788 does not apply: the type caches are `CheckerState` fields, i.e. per-`Checker`), which is what makes the number quotable — it is genuinely deletable and genuinely tiny. **HAZARDS MEASURED, NOT THEORIZED, and one is worse than predicted: 175 merge collisions per compile, 20 of them MUTATING a lib symbol** (`Symbol`, `ImportAttributes`) — so request 2 would inherit request 1's `flags |=` and accumulated `declarations`, unbounded — and that is a LOWER bound (nested `exports`/`members` writes and `mergeModuleAugmentations`' `globals[…] =` are uncounted). The id hazard is **ORDER, not collision** (one daemon thread ⇒ no interleaving), but skipping the bind shifts every later program's symbol ids by ~185, and round 607's sibling failure was **51 corpus failures whose `--listAll` output stays IDENTICAL**. A safe version is barely expressible — the merge target set is discovered dynamically, so safety means copy-on-write over every lib symbol, re-doing the allocation the bind was doing; only `resolve` + stamping are safely shareable, **3.5 ms**. **VERDICT: not worth a round** — 0.13-0.20% is an order of magnitude inside BOTH A/B bands (cold ±2.0%, warm ±1.0%), i.e. unmeasurable by the instruments that would confirm it, while carrying a hazard class that produces byte-identical wrong output. `RealLibSnapshots`' "binding is deliberately per-consumer" KDoc is right and now has a price: **4.9 ms warm**. **RULED IN BY ELIMINATION, queued as (WARM.3):** the compile is 91.8% checker warm, so the only lib-shaped candidate left is per-request re-derivation of lib **TYPES** — unmeasured, inside `checkSpine`, and strictly MORE hazardous (`Type` ids, interning, instantiation contexts). It gets a prize measurement, not a design. **GATES: none run, none required** — nothing under `src/` changed, probe reverted, tree verified clean; the probe was 3 timestamp pairs per PROGRAM (~0.3 µs against 6.8 s) and its walls sat at or below round 843's probe-free medians._

_**Round 842 (2026-08-07) — (AOT.5)(f): THE AOT TRAINING DRAW IS WORTH UP TO 2.4%, AND EVERY AOT RESULT ON RECORD WAS MEASURED WITH ONE TRAINED CACHE PER ARM.** Five caches trained by an IDENTICAL command (`scripts/xtsc-aot train`, nothing changed between runs), **168 whole-project compiles in seven rotated batches**. Paired best-vs-worst draw: **+322 ms (+2.4%), 10/12**, and the ordering **REPLICATES** — batches D and E, run separately with different rotations hours apart, put the same three draws in the same first three places — so this is a persistent property of a cache FILE, not run noise. **The methodological result: "two batches" is the standing rule for a MEASUREMENT and does NOT cover a TRAINING claim**, because a second batch re-uses the same cache files and so replicates the measurement draw while leaving the training draw a between-arm confound. **New rule: an A/B on a training configuration needs >=2 independently trained caches per arm.** **It overturns no DECISION and costs one NUMBER its precision:** round 840(c)'s SHIPPED emit-training win is -1,132 ms (-5.4%) against a draw term of ~1.2-1.6% on that same workload (>3x, safe), while round 840(d)'s rejected `--workers 4` arm was a *null* on the path it was meant to help plus a cost on the default — a draw term cannot turn a null into a win — but its **+3.5%** is only ~1.5x a term it modelled as zero and must stop being quoted to three digits. **STRUCK OUTRIGHT: 840(d) § 10.3's reading of that cache's "+82 KB" as evidence of a different recorded profile** — five identical-command draws span **212,992 B**, 2.6x that, and size does NOT order speed (the largest of the five is second-slowest, the smallest is slowest). **Batch A was a textbook false positive for the third round running** (medians spread 483 ms = 3.5%, bigger than the effect that survived; batch B put a different draw first). The emit half adds the finer point: the EXTREMES carry across workloads (draw 1 fast, draw 5 slow on both) while the fine ordering does not. **Nothing ships — that is arithmetic, not caution:** "train twice, keep the faster" needs ~10 paired compiles to resolve a 2.4% difference worth ~0.3 s per run, paying back after ~800 compiles, and it makes the installed cache non-reproducible by the documented command. **(AOT.5)(a) closed in passing:** the shipped `~/.cache/xtsc` cache, un-retrained since round 840's fingerprint change, was retrained (54,079,488 B, verifies `USE`) and **pruned 1 stale cache** — § 2's "delete on upgrade" observed on a real fingerprint change. **(AOT.5)(b) ALSO CLOSED — `--serve`/`--daemon` UNDER a cache, through the launcher: 32 requests, all 46 errors / one md5 / ZERO in-process fallbacks; paired over three rotated server pairs, **request 1 is 1.66× (3/3), request 2 still 1.32× (3/3), requests 3-4 a wash** — round 839's "halves the first request, worth nothing warm" reproduced through the shipped launcher and REFINED (the cache shortens the C2 warm-up ramp by about one request, which first/warm-only sampling could not see). **Its first run was an A/A that looked exactly like an A/B:** `build/libs/*.jar` is **NOT byte-reproducible** (`./gradlew jvmJar --rerun` with no source change yields a different sha256 AND size, 5,639,210 -> 5,639,200 B), so this session's own suite run invalidated the cache trained 8 minutes earlier and both arms ran uncached at a plausible ladder — caught ONLY by printing the launcher's decision (`XTSC_AOT_VERBOSE=1` -> `aot SKIP no-cache-file`). **Train AFTER the last build; never measure a cached arm without printing the decision.** **Correctness:** all 168 compiles at **46 errors and one diagnostics md5** (`59d930db…`), no empty capture, no `more error(s)` tell; the 36 emitting runs additionally share **78 files and ONE whole-tree digest** (`0b59764c…`). **GATES: suite 13,909 / 0 / 3, unchanged — and NO PIN WAS ADDED, stated rather than omitted:** nothing under `src/` changed, the claim is a measurement, and its repeatability is the committed harness `scripts/aot-draw-variance.sh`; `cost_gate.py` and `huge_methods.py` not run and not applicable. `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 12._

_**Round 840(c) (2026-08-06) — (AOT.4)(c): THE CACHE IS NOW TRAINED *WITH EMIT*, WORTH −5.4% ON AN EMITTING COMPILE FOR NOTHING ON THE CHECK-ONLY PATH — AND THE ROUND'S MORE VALUABLE RESULT IS A FALSE POSITIVE IT CAUGHT IN ITS OWN FIRST BATCH.** `xtsc-aot train` ran `--noEmit`, so the shipped cache carried no C2 profile for the Transformer or Emitter at all; round 839 priced training with emit at ~800 ms and left it unclaimed. **54 whole-project compiles of the 78-file profile, paired within-rep, rotated, daemons stopped, box unwatched: emit 14/15 pairs, median −1,132 ms = −5.4%; `--noEmit` 10/15, −150 ms = −0.8%, a wash. There is no trade** — the check-only path pays nothing. Round 839's MECHANISM is confirmed by differencing each rep's emit run against its own `--noEmit` run: the emit tail goes **3,148 → 2,216 ms (−932 ms)**, i.e. back to the 2,139 ms an UNCACHED run pays, which is exactly "the cache now carries an emitter profile" and not "longer training also warms the checker" (that would have moved the check-only arms, and it did not). Predicted ~800 ms, measured 932. **THE FALSE POSITIVE, which is the part to remember: batch 1 read the CHECK-ONLY path as 5/5 sign-consistent wins, median −1,161 ms (−6.6%), with the winner in the drift-disadvantaged position 4 of 5 times** — every internal check a single batch can offer said "real". Batch 2 (3/6, +192 ms) and a verification batch (2/4) refuted it. **Rotation and pairing control DRIFT; only REPLICATION controls the DRAW** — now a CLAUDE.md entry, and a caution over every 5/5 and 6/6 win rate this arc has quoted. **Packaging: acceptable only via a new `--outDir`.** `train` runs against the user's own project, so emitting into its `outDir` would clobber a real `dist/` or die on a read-only tree; so `ProjectCompiler.build(outDir = …)` is exposed as `--outDir` (CWD-relative like tsc's, inert under `--noEmit`, deliberately NOT threaded through `--incremental`/`--watch`) and `train` emits into a `mktemp -d` removed on every exit path — verified end to end: no `dist/` in the profile, no leftover temp, cache self-verifies `USE`, 4/4 paired emit wins. The fail-safe contract is deliberately untouched: **the training WORKLOAD is not in the fingerprint and must not be** — the manifest binds a cache to the code it was trained from, not to how that code was exercised — so existing `--noEmit`-trained caches stay VALID, merely ~5% slower on emit until retrained. Cost: +2.9 MB cache (+5.6%), +1.5 s training. **Correctness:** all 54 runs one sorted-diagnostics md5 (`59d930db…`, 46 errors, the digest rounds 828/832/839 recorded) and all 30 emitting runs one whole-tree sha256 (`a3ccd863…`, 78 files) with `dist/` deleted and re-hashed each time. **GATES: suite 13,903 → 13,908 / 0 / 3** (+4 `ProjectOutDirTest`, +1 script-contract pin), `cost_gate.py` every counter **+0.00%**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit** — both required and run, because this one touched `commonMain`. **NOT measured:** a cache trained under `--workers 4`; `--serve`/`--daemon` under a cache through the launcher; `--watch`/`--incremental` under a cache; the other seven profiles; a project whose emit share exceeds this profile's ~13% (the direction should hold and the share should grow — an argument, not a measurement). Commit `e355a990`; `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 9._

_**Round 840(d) (2026-08-07) — (AOT.4)(c)'s SECOND HALF CLOSES AS A MEASURED NEGATIVE, AND IT BOUNDS THE PRINCIPLE 840(c) JUST ESTABLISHED.** Training the AOT cache under **`--workers 4`** buys the parallel path **0.9%** — inside the band — and **costs the sequential default 3.5%**, so `xtsc-aot train` STAYS SEQUENTIAL, now pinned (`AotCacheGuardTest.the trainer trains sequentially`, ablation-verified after committing: adding `--workers 4` to the trainer fails exactly that pin and no other). 60 compiles, two caches differing ONLY in training worker count, **two independent 5-rep rotated batches that AGREED at every level** (seq +501/+679, w4 −110/−114, w8 −854/−610 ms). Paired: seq **+545 ms (+3.5%), 2/10**; w4 −112 ms (−0.9%), 8/10; w8 −732 ms (−4.7%), 8/10. **THE TRANSFERABLE RESULT: 840(c)'s "train under the mode you actually run" is a claim about ONE DIMENSION AT A TIME, not a law.** Emit was free because the Transformer/Emitter profile was simply ABSENT, so adding it took nothing away; worker count is the SAME checking code under a different thread structure, so the shared JEP 515 profile is a genuine trade. Commit `654ac969`; `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 10._

_Round 838 (2026-08-05) — **(NARROW.2)(a)+(b): TWO CORE NARROWING DEFECTS, AND BOTH OF ROUND 837'S DIAGNOSES ARE RETRACTED AND REPLACED BY MEASURED ONES.** (a) was handed down as "there is no `instanceof` arm in `extractNullNarrowing`". There does not need to be: a user-declared `class C` narrows correctly through the flow walker at every consumer probed (assignment, argument, property access), and the whole defect was `resolveInstanceOfRhsType`'s `SymbolFlags.Class` gate one function away — **every ambient constructor in the lib is written `interface Error { … }` + `declare var Error: ErrorConstructor`, so `Error`/`Date`/`RegExp`/`Map`/`Set`/`Promise` narrowed NOTHING in either branch while a user-written class narrowed fine.** Fixed with tsc's `getInstanceType` (the `prototype` property, then a lone construct signature's return), deliberately bounded: no `[Symbol.hasInstance]` leg, no `mapType` over a union-typed RHS, no signature erasure, and where tsc falls back to `emptyObjectType` we return null — a `{}` narrow would be a new and wrong type at every consumer. (b) was handed down as "a guard's narrow LEAKS out of its `if` into the next sibling `if`". **It is not a scoping leak — the `if` scoping is correct and a UNION subject behaves perfectly.** It is one relation call: both single-type NEGATIVE branches (a type-predicate guard's and `narrowByInstanceOf`'s) decide "the subject IS the target, so the false branch is impossible" with the ASSIGNABLE relation, and `any` is assignable to everything — so the else branch of a guard on an `any` subject was `never`, the flow join of (`Foo` from then, `never` from else) produced `Foo`, and it ACCUMULATED across sibling guards (`Error | Function` after two). **Nothing in the output ever names `never`, which is exactly why it presented as a leak.** tsc survives it by construction (its assumeFalse filter goes through `isTypeSubsetOf`, plain identity for a non-union candidate). The POSITIVE branch is untouched — that is (c). **PINS, BOTH DIRECTIONS ON REAL BINARIES, 14 across two classes.** (a): against the rebuilt pre-change binary exactly the **4 positives fail and the 3 controls stay green**; against a single-mistake ablation returning the constructor type where the resolution has no answer, exactly `a value that is not a constructor does not narrow` fails and the other 6 stay green. (b): against the rebuilt pre-change binary exactly **3 positives fail**; against a both-branches ablation, `the positive branch on an any subject still narrows` fails. **A fourth pin written as a positive was MEASURED not to discriminate and is RENAMED to say what it actually tests** — narrowing the leaked `Function` by `is Error` happened to answer `Error` anyway. **No control exists for "this subject still collapses to `never`": a `never` narrow is unobservable in this compiler** — assignable to every probe target, silent at a property access, and an argument position reads the declared type. **GATES:** suite **13,900 / 0 / 3** (13,886 + 14 pins, **0 regressions**, `xml.etree`); **the 8-profile grid was run TWICE against a rebuilt before-arm — after (a) and again after (b) — 46x7 + harness 94, every sorted capture md5-IDENTICAL on all eight, both times**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, 0 over the limit; `cost_gate.py` PASS on both commits, `output.errors` 46 unchanged, largest movement -0.01% (`narrow.walks` -2, `typeNode.bypassed` -9, `globals.lookups` -27 after (b) — the profile sites where a negative branch no longer washes a subject to `never`), rebaselined with `--update` in each commit. **NO CONFORMANCE MOVEMENT, and that was predicted:** all three `types/any` narrowing cases still emit nothing, gated entirely on **(c)** — `checkMemberAccessMissing` reading a narrowed `any` receiver — which stays UNCHECKED and carries the round's FP warning._

_Round 837 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER DIRECTION: CONFORMANCE BREADTH. THE HANDED-DOWN TARGET WAS RE-COSTED AND OVERRULED ON EVIDENCE, AND THE ROUND'S SECOND DELIVERABLE IS THAT CONFORMANCE BREADTH IS NOW MEASURED TO BE OUT OF CHEAP WORK.** Round 836 priced `types/any`'s three NARROWING cases as "ONE mechanism: narrowing FROM `any`, exempt `Function`/`Object`". Re-created the survey harness from its own description (~110 lines, **40 s per measurement against ~14 min for a suite arm**; it reproduced round 836's row exactly) and probed the compiler with deliberate mis-assignments — **it is THREE gaps**: a type-predicate guard ALREADY narrows an `any` subject (only tsc's `Function`/`Object` exemption is missing, two lines); `instanceof` narrowing reaches NOTHING (no arm in `extractNullNarrowing` at all — even an `Error | string` body-local is un-narrowed in the then-branch); and **the narrow never reaches the PROPERTY-ACCESS walker when the declared type is `any`**, which is what actually blocks all three cases and means widening `checkMemberAccessMissing` over the shape tsc's own sources use constantly. A fourth defect found and not fixed: a guard's narrow **LEAKS out of its `if` block into the next sibling `if`**, precisely the five-sequential-`if` shape the fixture is built from. **TOOK THE THREE BOUNDED CASES IN THE SAME CATEGORY INSTEAD — `types/any` 6 -> 3 failing, all three closed on errors AND emit:** TS2347 through `new` (tsc reaches `resolveUntypedCall` from `resolveNewExpression` too, so `new x<any>(x)` is as much an error as `x<any>(x)` — same narrow gate as the call leg, not `calleeType === anyType`); `void` and `typeof undefined` assumed-initialized for TS2454 (tsc's guard is `type.flags & (AnyOrUnknown | Void)` OR facts carrying `IsUndefined`; deliberately NOT folded into the shared `typeIncludesUndefined`, whose other callers are TS2564 sites where a `void` property DOES want the diagnostic, and deliberately SYNTACTIC because resolving inside a candidate COLLECTOR makes the set a function of resolution order); and TS2631 in an assignment target. **THE CORPUS CAUGHT THE THIRD BEING WRONG AND IT IS THE ROUND'S GOTCHA:** keying on `SymbolFlags.Module` came back 13,885 / **5** — `Module` is the UNION of `ValueModule` and `NamespaceModule`, and tsc gives the two DIFFERENT diagnostics at the same syntax (instantiated ⇒ the write is the error, TS2631; non-instantiated ⇒ no value meaning, so the value-position TS2708 fires first). **PINS, BOTH DIRECTIONS ON REAL BINARIES:** 16 new + 2 corrected; against the rebuilt pre-change binary **exactly the 8 positives fail and all 8 controls stay green**, and the `ValueModule` ablation is the round's own first attempt, whose control fixture is byte-identical to `assignToModule.ts`. Four TS2347/TS2454 controls are recorded as ARM pins, not claimed as coverage. **GATES:** suite **13,886 / 0 / 3** (13,870 + 16, 0 regressions, `xml.etree`); `cost_gate.py` PASS, 46 errors unchanged, every counter +0.00% except `globals.lookups` +198 (+0.03%) from the new `new`-site resolution, rebaselined in the same commit; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, largest 5,149; **8-profile grid against a REBUILT before-arm, md5-identical on all eight**, the before-arm reproducing round 836's digests exactly. **RECOMMENDATION: leave conformance breadth as a target.** ~9 bounded subtests remain across nine categories and **none completes a category**; rounds 833-837 landed five features and seven cases and adopted zero categories. The concrete alternative is **(NARROW.2)** — an `instanceof` arm, block-scoping the guard narrow, and letting `checkMemberAccessMissing` see a narrowed `any` receiver — whose first two steps are core correctness gaps, not fixture-chasing._

_Round 836 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER DIRECTION: CONFORMANCE BREADTH. THE ROUND IS A SURVEY AND A FIX: the nine held-back conformance categories are now priced PER CASE *including emit* (`docs/conformance-worklist.md`), and tsc's OPTIONAL-CHAIN leg of `checkReferenceExpression` — five codes that existed NOWHERE — lands.** Suite **13,870 / 0 / 3** (13,855 + 15 pins, 0 regressions); all 8 profiles md5-for-md5 identical against a REBUILT before-arm._

_**THE SURVEY, AND WHY IT IS NOT A SUITE RUN.** Three rounds in a row adopted a category on its failure COUNT and had to re-cost it upward, because a category's name describes its FIXTURES, not its gaps. So: `git archive` the categories out of the blobless clone, replicate the generator's own subtest algebra in a throwaway `main` (`parseDirectives` / `computeVariations` / `tsgoSkippedTests` / `usesUnsupportedOption` / `tsconfigInTestUsesRemovedFeature`, verbatim), and diff both the `.errors.txt` SUMMARY and each `//// [name.js]` section. **~40 s for all 124 cases against ~12 minutes for a suite arm**, and it yields the per-case diff a suite arm does not. **HEADLINE: 124 cases, 79 error subtests, 65 with a diagnostic diff, 12 with a JS-emit diff — and the emit column is what decides adoptability**, since `conformanceDeferredErrorBaselines` defers only `.errors.txt`. Four categories (`asOperator`, `optionalChaining`, `conditional`, `labeledStatements`) carry an emit-red case and are vetoed outright no matter what the checker learns. By MECHANISM rather than category: **~20 subtests M3.1/M3.2 inference-and-relation, ~7 parser/ASI/error-recovery, 12 JS emit, 4 EMBEDDED-LIB gaps (`Record`/`Partial` — not checker work at all), ~9 flow narrowing, ~12 bounded single-purpose checks** — only the last bucket buys whole cases in a round. **`types/any` is the best-shaped remaining target** (six small cases, no emit veto, three of them ONE mechanism: narrowing FROM `any`), `types/typeAliases` the flattest (nine cases of 2-7 diffs, no emit veto, six unrelated mechanisms). **THE SURVEY'S FIRST PASS WAS WRONG AND SAID SO — 13 of 125 cases came back "skipped":** `usesUnsupportedOption` is a per-CONFIG skip, not a per-FILE one, so `@target: es5, es2015` loses its BARE subtests and KEEPS the `(target=es2015)` variation; now a CLAUDE.md entry._

_**WHAT LANDED.** We implemented the first leg of `checkReferenceExpression` (TS2357 / TS2364) and NONE of the second, so `obj?.a = 1`, `obj?.a++`, `for (obj?.a in {})`, `for (obj?.a of [])` and every destructuring-assignment element compiled in complete silence. One predicate at five sites: **TS2777** (`++`/`--` operand), **TS2778** (object REST target), **TS2779** (assignment LHS plain and compound, array/object destructuring element, AND an array rest target — tsc reserves TS2778 for the object one), **TS2780** (`for..in` LHS), **TS2781** (`for..of` LHS). We carry no `NodeFlags.OptionalChain` bit, so the predicate is a descent through the chain's own links: a `?.` anywhere BELOW makes the whole target a chain (`a?.b.c` is one), a PARENTHESIS terminates it (`(a?.b).c` is not), outer wrappers skipped first as `skipOuterExpressions(expr, Assertions | Parentheses)` does. `checkDestructuringPrivateIds` already enumerated exactly tsc's target positions, so it carries the new leg as `checkDestructuringAssignmentTargets`. **`propertyAccessChain.3` and `elementAccessChain.3` (22 diagnostics each) now match their baselines exactly, ORDER included — and BOTH CASES STAY RED on their own `?.`-downlevel EMIT subtest, which is the survey's whole point: the errors half of a case can go green while the case does not.** Exposure is bounded by construction: every one of these codes reports code tsc itself rejects, so tsc's sources cannot contain one — and the grid measured **zero sightings on all 8 profiles**._

_**PINS, BOTH DIRECTIONS, ON REAL BINARIES.** 15 pins. Against the **rebuilt pre-change binary** exactly the **10 positives fail**, 5 stay green. ONE MISTAKE AT A TIME, each its own build: skipping PARENTHESES inside the chain descent fails exactly `a parenthesis terminates the chain`; answering "the subtree contains a `?.`" fails exactly `an optional chain inside the index is not a chained target`. **The other 3 pins are NOT ablated and are recorded as ARM pins, not claimed as coverage.** **GATES:** suite **13,870 / 0 / 3**; `cost_gate.py` **PASS, all 20 counters +0.00%**, `output.errors` 46, no rebaseline; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, largest 5,149; **8-profile grid vs a REBUILT before-arm — 46x7 + harness 94 on both arms, no truncation tell, sorted-capture md5 IDENTICAL on all eight** (compiler `59d930db849399aea5e03e25fedb8e4e`)._

_Round 835 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER DIRECTION: CONFORMANCE BREADTH. THE `object` KEYWORD'S *APPARENT* TYPE: tsc has NO `object`-specific elaboration — `structuredTypeRelatedTo` compares `getApparentType(source)`, which for `TypeFlags.NonPrimitive` is `emptyObjectType`, so a failing `object -> { foo: string }` is decided by `propertiesRelatedTo` against an EMPTY member table and reported as a MISSING PROPERTY whose source displays as `{}` — never as `object`. _**WHICH GAP, RE-VERIFIED RATHER THAN INHERITED.** Round 834 handed this down as "1 diff, message-shape only, cheapest by a wide margin"; I re-measured it and the four other residual cases by extracting each fixture's source from its `.js` BASELINE (`//// [name.ts]` carries the original verbatim — the `tests/cases/conformance` tree is not in the sparse checkout) and running the project CLI against the `.errors.txt` summary. **The costing holds exactly**: 6 of 7 diagnostics already matched; the diff was line 5, `y = a`. The coarse TS2322 below still says `object` — that is also tsc, which reports that one from the original type. **The relation VERDICT is untouched in both directions**, so the change can neither add nor remove a diagnostic anywhere. **DELIBERATELY NOT `getApparentType`**: a NonPrimitive arm there is the obvious "proper" fix and would reach ~40 consumers at once — round 834 measured exactly what widening a shared predicate costs. The var-decl twin WAS included, because a rule applied at one of two sibling emitters is an inconsistency the next agent trips on._

_**PIN DISCRIMINATION, VERIFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS ON REAL BINARIES** — 8 pins, `NonPrimitiveApparentTypeTest`. Against the **rebuilt pre-change binary** (the grid's own before-arm, so no extra build was spent) exactly the **4 positive pins fail** and the 4 controls stay green. Against a **naive ablation** — the predicate returning the apparent type for EVERY source — exactly the **2 controls written for it fail** (`a primitive source keeps its own coarse message`, i.e. keying on `Type.Intrinsic` rather than the flag; `a named interface source keeps its name`) and the 4 positives stay green. The other 2 controls are **not ablated** and are recorded as arm pins, not claimed as coverage. **WHAT COST TWO BUILD CYCLES:** `Diagnostic.character` is **1-based** while `line` is not directive-stripped, so a related-info position pin must assert the column verbatim against the baseline (tsc's `2:10` is `character == 10`) and the LINE relatively. **RE-COSTING — the cheap end of `nonPrimitive` is now exhausted; none of the 6 residual cases is a message shape.** Cheapest-first: `nonPrimitiveAccessProperty` (2, and its `var { destructuring } = a` half wants the SAME apparent-type substitution), `nonPrimitiveUnionIntersection` (4, intersection REDUCTION — moves identity and display), `nonPrimitiveNarrow` (3) + `nonPrimitiveStrictNull` (12, flow narrowing on `object`), `nonPrimitiveConstraintOfIndexAccessType` (10), `nonPrimitiveAndTypeVariables` (1, `TypeParam -> TypeParam` lenience PLUS a union display-ORDER divergence, so a correct relation fix alone still leaves it red)._

_Round 825 (2026-08-04) — **(PERF.HW.a) IS DIAGNOSED AND FIXED, FOR REAL THIS TIME: THE `--workers` RACE IS A SINGLETON ID-SPACE COLLISION, AND THE VERIFICATION IS 48 RUNS AGAINST ROUND 754's ONE.** **PROVED, not inferred:** `runInDeepStackWorkers` gave every worker the **SAME** `WORKER_ID_BASE`, which is sound only if nothing SHARED is ever minted from a worker's own sequence — and **nothing in the sequential prefix of a parallel compile reads an intrinsic type**, so `TypeKt`'s static initializer first ran INSIDE whichever worker reached `anyType` first, AFTER that worker had rebased. Commits `9c69eaff`, `42d8f48c`._

THE PERFORMANCE CASE FOR (M2) UNPARKS, THE CORRECTNESS GATE DOES NOT.** **The headline is correctness and it outranks the scaling table:** at a FIXED worker count the diagnostic count varies run to run — **seq 46x8; w2 46x5/47x5; w4 46x3/47x3; w8 46x1/56x6**. Divergences are strictly ADDITIVE (`added=N, removed=0` everywhere — the mode never loses a true diagnostic, it invents false ones) and each OUTCOME is byte-reproducible (w8's 56-set matches by md5sum across captures), but **which outcome you get is not**; two DISTINCT FP families (w2/w4 = `debug.ts:601` TS2345; w8 = 10 other lines NOT including it). **Round 754's byte-identity was ONE DRAW OF A 50/50 COIN — this round reproduced the false green itself.** On the old box ~0.85 free cores effectively SERIALIZED the workers, so **the upgrade did not introduce the race, it made it OBSERVABLE**; `--partitionCheck` cannot see it (a static partition model cannot produce two answers for one worker count), and the detector that can is a COUNT DISTRIBUTION over >=5 runs. **THE JVM CORE TAX IS NOT A CONSTANT — IT SCALES WITH `nproc`, WHICH REWRITES THE GATE ITSELF: 4.17 of 8 cores vs 3.15 of 4**, because `CICompilerCountPerCPU` defaults true (JIT CPU ~21.7 -> ~43.8 s; `-XX:CICompilerCount=2` drops the run 4.20 -> 2.55 cores with self time FLAT, reproducing round 618/740). So free cores went 0.85 -> **3.83, not 4.85** — and **3.83 SUFFICED: the answer arrived at 8, not the stated >=12.** **LADDER (self ms, n=5, rotated interleave, paired):** seq **26,145** (3.6%) | w2 **21,324** (**-19.05%**, 5/5) | **w4 19,472** (**-23.84%**, 5/5) | w8 **21,605** (**-17.02%**, 5/5) — every level sign-consistent, none straddling zero as round 740's w4 did; box verified clean (8 real cores, no SMT, no cgroup cap, steal 0.000/113 samples). **The SHAPE changed, not just the magnitude: w4 is now the BEST level and w8 — a +19.4% REGRESSION on 4 cores — is a 17% WIN**, with nothing saturating (6.44 of 8 cores). **THE 1.25x CAP IS DEAD, refuted by counterexample: w4 measured 1.343x**; re-fits give 1.584x (seq/w2) and 1.516x (seq/w4), agreeing within 8% where on 4 cores they disagreed 3.5x. **VERDICT: two of round 740's three reasons to not attempt (M2) have failed; the third is worse than believed — the mode is a RACE, not a fixed bug — so (M2) stays blocked on (PERF.HW.a), re-opened**, and step (b) is now worth ~1.5x rather than ~1.25x. **What did not work:** an UNSORTED flicker diff manufactured 3 phantom TS2591 extras that were pure line ordering, and **stage C's 8 extra runs are what turned "w8 is deterministically 56" into "w8 flickers" — one capture per level would have shipped the wrong classification, the SAME under-sampling error round 754 made.** No `src/` change, so `jvmTest` not applicable; 13,803 / 0 / 3 untouched. `docs/perf/worker-scaling-round824.md`, commit `c9258486`._

Level 2, three spans per CLOSURE: `collectReassignedNamesInRange` **275–444 ms over 2,014 closures**, and **~700 ms is the flow walk itself at 3.0 µs per flow node**. **LEVER A — remove 367,189 `String` allocations** (the census sized it: 382,520 identifier occurrences classified, 15,331 recorded, **24×**) **and the boxed `Char?` of every `getOrNull`: both arms twice on ONE binary, identical boundary counts, 314/362 vs 317/320 — ZERO.** *An allocation count is not a cost.* **LEVER B — defer the suffix set**, whose one consumer is a narrowing-walk membership test and whose walks fell 75% since 758: the row collapsed **53.5 → 0.9 ms**, and then its own census answered round 788's law AGAINST it — **created 1143, materialized 1143**, so the work **MOVED into the checker, not deleted. NEUTRAL, not quoted as a win.** **`bind` joins `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` (797) and the spine-leave handlers (733/799) as measured, bounded and CLOSED.** **What did NOT work, beyond both levers**: a memo over the returned suffix was abandoned unbuilt (the key is distinct per call by construction), and the deferral pin's first draft asserted a STRICT inequality and FAILED — that red test is how round 788's law surfaced at all. **The positive control is honestly DEAD where the prize was measured**: `--flowScanBogus` reports 0 divergences on the compiler profile (no `%=` in a scanned range) and fires only in the fixture — round 793's rule honoured rather than rediscovered. Grid vs the real pre-change path in the same binary **46/94/46/46/46/46/46/46, 0 added and 0 removed BOTH directions**; `--partitionCheck 2` **EQUIVALENT — 46**; cost gate **all 20 counters +0.00%**. **No wall A/B, deliberately.** Full derivation: `docs/perf/bind-attribution.md`._

_Round 800 (2026-08-02) — **(IANY.1) THE 249 ms CALL-ARGUMENT ARM IS GATED: 93% OF ITS CALLEE RESOLUTIONS BOUGHT NOTHING.** Round 799 handed forward the arm at 249 ms over 31,575 edges (7.9 us each, 32% of `spineIanyEnterNode`) with its obligation stated exactly: the state it computes is a `kind = 0` one with no type in it, so the arm is pure cost whenever the argument's subtree holds no reader — and `rhsCanConsumeFnCtx`, the obvious reuse, is UNSOUND for it. `spineIanyArgSubtreeMayRead` is the predicate built to that obligation: reader set = ARROW / FUNCTION_EXPRESSION / OBJECT_LITERAL (every other reader sits strictly inside one of those three), descending the arms that INHERIT the state and every no-arm parent, STOPPING at a nested CALL/NEW (whose own arm shadows its whole subtree — and an UNREACHED call makes everything below unreached, reach being monotone). **It defaults to `true`, so it can only ever refuse to skip**, and a 32-step cap (0 hits) bounds it; measured cost **1.87 steps per edge**. Both arms twice on ONE binary (`--ianyArgGateOff`), identical boundary counts, the predicate priced INSIDE both: callee resolutions **20,812 -> 1,439 (-93.1%)**, the arm row 196/190 -> 93/96 ms, and — quoting the ENCLOSING total per round 798's own correction — the **HANDLER TOTAL 724/707 -> 602/617 = 106 ms, 6.2x the within-arm spread, ~0.36% of a check-only compile**; a `--passTiming` pair agrees (`checkSpine` 21,047.6 -> 20,949.4). **No wall-clock A/B**: 0.36% is a third of the +-1.0% warm band. **ROUND 788's LAW IS ANSWERED RATHER THAN ASSUMED** — `typeOfExpr.distinct` falls **6.18%** against `calls` **4.21%**, and distinct falling FASTER means **14,813 expression nodes are now typed NOWHERE in the compile**, i.e. the arm was their only consumer and the work is deleted, not moved (`narrow.walks` +0.00%, `typeNode.bypassed` +5 of 110,776). **TWO CORRECTIONS.** The cheap gate does not exist: `typed = false` occurs **15 times in 20,827** because at a reached argument edge the state is ALWAYS the call's own kind=1 frame. And **round 799's own second counter-shape is VACUOUS** — the ablation that drops the array-literal AND `as` descents fails the array pin and the equivalence pin but NOT the `as` pin, because `spineIanyEdge`, the REACH classifier, has no `AsExpression` arm: nothing below an `as` is walked, so there is no reader there to lose. **What did NOT work**: the first probe build crashed on CLAUDE.md's own declaration-order trap, and the first timing batch read every row ~270x too large because the build's Kotlin daemon was still resident — BUILD.1's trap one round after round 799 recorded it; the deterministic census was immune and carried the round. Suite 13,454 -> **13,461 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (+7 pins, 2 discriminating under ablation). Grid vs `--ianyArgGateOff` **46/46/46/46/46/46/46/94, 0 added and 0 removed BOTH directions**; `--partitionCheck 2` **EQUIVALENT — 46**; cost gate rebased. **`spineIanyEnterNode` IS NOW FLAT** — 798+799+800 removed 481 ms of 1,031 and no concentration remains. Full derivation: `docs/perf/implicit-any-attribution.md` sections 13-18._

The prior mistook the PARAMETER for the ARGUMENT — a parameter that is not simple-checkable sounds like a callback and is usually an INTERFACE, whose arguments are ordinary names; round 759's own law is what it violated. **THE ITEM'S LITERAL QUESTION, ANSWERED**: `getTypeOfExpression` by kind is **PropertyAccess 11.2 us x 7,774 + Call 23.0 us x 2,612 = 73% of the row over 26.6% of the iterations**, against Identifier at **0.8 us** for 46.5% of the iterations — here the RARE kinds are the expensive ones, which is why a partition and not a count was required. **THE HARNESS ADDS NO BOUNDARY** (the classification is taken inside the already-open row) and **five partition checks print EXACT**. **THE NUMBER THAT CHANGES THE MAP**: two new sub-measures close the row to a named residue and price round 796's own gate directly for the first time — **131-137 ms over 9,823 calls = 13.3 us each**, not the "~70 ms" that round got by subtraction; still net-positive (it removes ~390 ms of walking) and un-cheapenable, since `checkTypeRelatedToCore` already short-circuits on `source === target` and only 416 of 8,905 refusals bail early at `getReferencePath`. **THE RESIDUE IS THE PROBE, QUANTITATIVELY**: 7 nested timestamp reads x the harness's own 423 ns in-situ figure = **2.96 us against a measured 3.3 us**. Two asides the cross-tab gave free: **66% of the arguments that reach the assignability relation are LITERALS** (which is why that relation call is 5-7 ms), and **22% of all iterations type an identifier to `any` and discard it** (8,574, 99 ms — a modelling signal, not a perf one). **NO LEVER, AS A BOUND**: the row is ~600 ms = 2.2% of a check-only compile and splits into ~200 ms irreducible type resolution, ~135 ms of the narrowing tail round 736 already closed the memo behind, ~131 ms of gate that buys 390, ~24 ms of literal preservation, and the probe — half a noise band is ~275 ms. _Round 791 (2026-08-01) — **(ENGINE.2d)(b): THE SUPPRESSION APPARATUS AT THE TOP OF `checkMemberAccessMissing` NOW RUNS 57 TIMES PER COMPILE INSTEAD OF 67,067 — ONLY FOR A CALL WHOSE BODY ACTUALLY EMITTED. The `R_FLOW` row **1,132 -> 49 ms**, the plain narrowing walk **794 ms / 22,270 calls -> 4 ms / 47**, level R total **2,686 -> 1,702 ms** — a 984 ms removal against the 781 ms the queue item priced, because the deferral also takes the `getTypeOfExpression` and the round-489 pre-gate that sat in front of the walks. `narrow.walks` **52,401 -> 27,249 (-48.0%)** and NO counter rose.** **ROUND 790's VETO RESTED ON A FALSE PREMISE, AND THE CORRECTION IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX**: "(b)'s correctness is a 20-way case analysis over emission sites, and no counter can falsify it" assumes the deferral must decide PER SITE whether the suppression applies. It does not — the three blocks only ever `return`, so the deferral has to undo the BODY, and the body's whole effect is enumerable in one grep: **42 `diagnostics.add` in 2,035 lines and ZERO other references to `diagnostics`, zero `.put(`/`.remove*`, zero `current*` ambient assignments, zero stack pushes** — same for all seven `tryEmit*`/`emit*` helpers. "Run the body, then remove what it appended" is therefore equivalent BY CONSTRUCTION, no site enumerated, and a 43rd emission site cannot break it. That is strictly better than the lazy-thunk shape round 790 proposed (a thunk still requires every reader to force it — the enumeration survives, renamed), and round 788's rejection of a thunk does not transfer because the deferred call happens INSIDE the very invocation that deferred it, so no ambient state can have escaped. **The one subtlety that decides correctness is the retraction FLOOR**: the intersection-reduction `never` TS2339 is emitted ABOVE the blocks' old position and was never theirs to suppress, so the floor is that position, never `diagnostics.size` at function entry. **The residual hazard — cache-mutation ORDER (round 754) — was MEASURED**: `--verifyDeferSuppression` evaluates the predicate at both positions, honours the EAGER verdict (so the run reproduces the pre-change binary by construction) and compares at `Type`-INSTANCE granularity — **compiler 67,067 / services 92,174 / harness 109,622, 0 type-diffs and 0 verdict-diffs across all 268,863** — with `--verifyDeferSuppressionBogus` as the shipped positive control (type-diff 19,864, VERDICT-DIFF 1,164). Warm A/B **B wins 3/3** (-6.05% / -3.21% / -2.04%, median **-381 ms / -3.21%**), but **both arms' sd exceed the 1% quietness criterion (A 3.30%, B 1.30%) and the per-pair spread is 509 ms against a 381 ms delta**, so the SIGN is confirmed and the MAGNITUDE is the partition's 984 ms; in percent the two agree (3.5% cold vs 3.21% warm). _Round 790 (2026-08-01) — **(ENGINE.2d)(a): THE ROUND-425 LOOP-ENTRY RETRY IS A PURE REPEAT OF THE WALK BEFORE IT 88.7% OF THE TIME. SKIPPING IT REMOVES 18,976 FLOW WALKS — 26.6% OF EVERY NARROWING WALK THE COMPILER PERFORMS — WITH ZERO CHANGE TO ANY DIAGNOSTIC ON ANY OF THE EIGHT PROFILES, AND `narrow.walks` FALLS BY EXACTLY THAT NUMBER WHILE NOTHING RISES.** The retry row **528 ms / 21,384 walks -> 90 ms / 2,408**; its containing R_FLOW row **1,548 -> 1,111 ms**; level R total 3,094 -> 2,618, level Q 3,488 -> 3,026 — the saving is visible at every level of the partition, which is the shape a real removal has and an absorbed one does not. **The equivalence is provable AND was falsified empirically before it was claimed**: `narrowTypeFromFlow` and `narrowTypeFromFlowFollowLoopEntry` are line-by-line mirrors differing in the `FlowLoopLabel` arm ALONE, so a plain walk that arrived at no loop label makes exactly the traversal the mirror would — and since every resolution the mirror would drive was already driven and CACHED moments earlier, the second walk is a pure repeat, which is also what disposes of the round-754 cache-mutation-ORDER hazard that kept round 789 from landing it. Two MONOTONE, never-reset counters (`narrowWalkLaunches` inside the walk lambda; `narrowRetryRelevantObs` at both walkers' loop arms and every truncation exit) make the bracket re-entrancy-safe, and unknown => run the retry. `--verifyLoopRetry` keeps the pre-gate behaviour and compares at `Type`-INSTANCE granularity: **compiler 18,976 / services 24,290 / harness 24,681 skippable calls, 0 type-diffs and 0 verdict-diffs across all 67,947**; `--verifyLoopRetryAll` is the shipped positive CONTROL over the complement population and does diverge, so the zero is not a dead instrument. **Round 788's law (an aggregate that is skippable is not thereby recoverable) was CHECKED, not assumed, and does not bite**: `narrow.walks` 71,377 -> 52,401 = -18,976 exactly, `globals.lookups` -4.28%, `globals.misses` -4.36%, nothing rose. Warm A/B **B wins 3/3** with every delta outside the +-1.0% band, but **arm A's sd is 2.47% > the 1% quietness criterion**, so the SIGN is confirmed and the MAGNITUDE is the partition's 438 ms rather than the wall's 696 — reported as the driver's own rule requires. Suite 13,380 -> **13,389 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (+9 pins, **7 discriminating** against an ablated binary; the 2 holders are the two deliberate controls, and no two faults cancelled). 8-profile grid captured at HEAD FIRST and after, diffed set-for-set BOTH directions: **46/46/46/46/46/46/46/94, 0 added and 0 removed on all eight**; `--partitionCheck 2` **EQUIVALENT — 46**; cost gate rebaselined in the same commit with the mechanism and population named. **(ENGINE.2d)(b) — the remaining 781 ms — is deliberately NOT attempted and is re-scoped in the queue with why (a) does not calibrate it.** Full derivation: `docs/perf/property-access-attribution.md` sections 17-22._

_Round 779 (2026-07-31) — **(REL.4)(b) CLOSED — AND ITS OWN LABEL IS FALSIFIED: THE FAMILY IS TWO DEFECTS AND ONLY ONE IS GENERIC INFERENCE.** Scaffolded price **compiler 52 -> 48, services 56 -> 49**; `diff` says exactly those eleven lines and no new one. **(b1), 3 of the 4 compiler lines** (`checker.ts:12576`, `esDecorators.ts:1093`, `esnext.ts:605`) is not inference at all: `Debug.assertIsDefined<T>(value: T)` has a BARE type-parameter parameter and T was inferred correctly as `Declaration` — the FP came from `tryEmitOptionalMemberArgVsRequiredNamedTs2345`, a walker whose whole premise (*"the parameter independently REQUIRES this named type, so the optional member's unmodelled `| undefined` is the sole failure"*) is **self-referential** when the named type is one WE inferred from the very argument being judged; a bare type parameter requires nothing. _Round 774 (2026-07-31) — **(AOT.2) THE WARM A/B DRIVER LANDS (`scripts/ab-warm.sh`), AND ITS OWN A/A VALIDATION FOUND THAT ROUND 771's ±1.0% WARM BAND IS A PROPERTY OF A BOX NOBODY IS TOUCHING: THE SAME BINARY AGAINST ITSELF READ **−6.70%** WHILE I POLLED THE RUN'S LOG AND **−0.36% / −0.54% / +1.02%** WHEN I LEFT IT ALONE** (process-median sd **278 ms** vs **48 ms**; round 771's own was 187 ms). The driver is the warm counterpart of `ab-interleaved.sh`: one fresh JVM per SAMPLE running `BenchMain`, the sample being the MEDIAN of that process's iterations (never the iterations themselves — they share JIT state and heap, so pooling them would fake the sample count), interleaved A,B,A,B so both arms meet the same drift. **The statistical stance is copied from the cold driver verbatim; only the band changes** (1.0% vs 2.0%), which is why an A/A correctly reads NOISE-DOMINATED in BOTH runs — so the discriminator between a good run and a poisoned one is the per-arm sd the driver now prints, not the verdict, and the rule recorded is to discard any warm run whose arm sd exceeds ~1%. **Self-falsification is wired and was TESTED BY FAKING IT**: the driver parses every iteration's `files`/`errors` and aborts (exit 3) on any disagreement or on a process that yields no iterations; both paths were exercised with a stub `java` emitting a deliberate 78/46 → 78/47 drift and emitting nothing. All 96 real iterations reported 78/46. **The classpath contract is the non-obvious part**: `BenchMain` lives in `commonTest`, so the run needs the test classes while the A/B must swap only MAIN classes — `<arm main dir> : build/classes/kotlin/jvm/test : <deps>` — which means the shared test dir comes from the CURRENT build and a change altering the API `BenchMain` calls cannot be A/B'd warm at all; and there is no B-side extra-args slot, since `BenchMain` takes no compiler flags (a flag comparison stays a cold job). The dep tail is cached in `build/bench/cp-warm.txt` and used only while newer than build.gradle.kts — the committed `build/bench/cp.txt` is STALE (kotlinx-io 0.9.0 / kotlin-stdlib 2.4.0 where the build resolves 0.9.1 / 2.4.10), which is exactly the trap that guard exists for. **The prediction I got wrong is the round's output**: I read the first run's spread as "this box is worse than round 771's" and was one commit from writing that down; the cause was my own ~100 polling shell commands, which is round 740's "a single xtsc run already takes ~3.15 of 4 cores" arriving from the other side. **No arc item was re-opened** — doc § 4's caution stands (measurable is not worth landing; round 736's identity pre-test was rejected as UNSOUND, not small). Script + docs only, no `src/` change, so no suite gate; suite unchanged at **13,223**. Artifacts: `scripts/ab-warm.sh`, `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md` § 4a, `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 6._

_Round 770 (2026-07-29; bookkeeping completed 2026-07-30) — **(REL.4)(a) CAUSE 5, THE POST-SWITCH FALL-THROUGH — AND THE DEFECT WAS IN THE BINDER, NOT THE CHECKER.** Leaving a `default`-less switch, `bindSwitchStatement` joined the post-switch flow to a bare FlowCondition chain asserting "every case EXPRESSION is falsy" — the truth for the `switch (true) { case <cond>: }` idiom ONLY, since a discriminant switch's case expression is a VALUE (`SyntaxKind.A`), so every link narrowed nothing and `Debug.assertNever(x)` after an exhaustive `default`-less switch saw the WHOLE declared type. Fixed as tsc encodes it: a switch-clause flow with an **EMPTY clause range** (`createFlowSwitchClause(…, 0, 0)`, read as `clauseStart === clauseEnd` ⇒ treat as `default:`), layered **ON TOP** of the existing chain rather than replacing it, so every existing `switch (true)` answer is untouched; three checker readers now treat an empty range as "no case matched", i.e. the same position as a `default:` clause and hence the same subtraction. Round 768's label for this cause — "does not narrow at all … NOT enum-specific, flow-graph work" — is confirmed exactly: the pins cover enums, discriminated unions and `typeof` tags alike. **A SECOND DEFECT FELL OUT, found by a pin breaking rather than by inspection**: `narrowBySwitchOnTrue` keys purely on `clauseStart` and never consults `clauseEnd`, so the new empty range read as "clause 0 matched" and narrowed POSITIVELY on the way OUT, collapsing `switch (true) { case c1: … }` to `never` (`narrowByClauseExpressionInSwitchTrue3`); guarded, since the edge's own claim is already carried by the chain beneath it. The general invariant — four readers consult this node, and one keying on `clauseStart` alone is wrong the moment an empty range reaches it — is now in CLAUDE.md. **THE ROUND WAS SENT AT CAUSE 6 AND PIVOTED**: cause 6's machinery is `narrowByCallPredicate`'s over an INTERSECTION subject, which has no member list to peel, so it stays its own round — the pivot confirms round 769's sizing of it. Gates, all **re-run from scratch on a restarted host rather than taken from the round's own commit message**: suite **13,216 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (13,207 + 9 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` **46** and programFiles **78** unchanged, cost gate exit 0 with **16 of 20 counters bit-identical** and four moved (`narrow.walks` 71,326 -> **71,323**, `narrow.memoServed` 45,489 -> **45,488**, `globals.lookups`/`globals.misses` **+0.16% / +0.17%** from the new readers resolving case tags that used to bail) — inside tolerance, rebaselined. No corpus baseline moved, so no `LogicalParityDivergence`. **Every pin run against pre-fix `619641b2^`: the 5 TARGETS FAIL and the 3 NEGATIVE CONTROLS PASS**; the ninth passes on both sides by design — it guards the second defect, which the fix itself introduced and then fixed. **NOT MEASURED, and it is the round's open end: the scaffolded (REL.4) flip price.** Cause 5's two lines (`checker.ts:11536`, `37648`) are both in `src/compiler`, so the expectation is **compiler 56 -> 54 / services 60 -> 58** — a PREDICTION, and the unscaffolded grid at 46 cannot see it, because those lines exist only while the imported-namespace pair is scaffolded on. **PROCESS: three consecutive server-side API failures (500, 529, 529) killed the authoring agent after the work was complete and before it could gate**; the orchestrator inspected the diff for completeness and probe residue and landed `619641b2`, then the host restarted before protocol step 5, leaving the note, this bump, the queue update and the rebaseline undone — all four are the follow-up commit._

_Round 748 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1)(c) STEP 4: a function-body-scoped `enum` is RESOLVABLE IN TYPE POSITION, and B583's blocker moves from RESOLUTION to DISPLAY.** Compiler profile `--listAll` **BYTE-IDENTICAL at 46** — the whole output, diffed sorted line-by-line against a build of pristine `7ab9b215`, composition unchanged (TS2591x43 / TS2304x2 / TS2584x1, no TS2322). _Round 745 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1)(c) STEP 1: the relation judges an enum by VALUE, and `checkEnumLiteralAssignments` (B203) is RETIRED in the same commit.** Corpus **12,971 -> 12,982 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (+11 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` **at 46, diffed line-by-line** (composition unchanged: TS2591x43 / TS2304x2 / TS2584x1, no TS2322), cost gate **+0.00% on all 20 counters** — no rebaseline. **THE ABLATION IS THE EVIDENCE, BOTH WAYS.** Before: disabling B203 through PassLab left `enumAssignmentCompat5` emitting **nothing** — the relation could not answer. After: the relation alone emits all three baseline lines **byte-identically** (message, position and length), and with B203 still enabled the run shows **6 diagnostics, each of the three duplicated** — exactly the co-emission round 740 predicted, which is why the rule and the retirement are ONE commit. **THE RULE IS tsc's, VERBATIM:** the wide `number` stays assignable to any numeric enum or member (the bit-flag compatibility rule, so `a = n` is still legal), but a numeric LITERAL is assignable only to a member whose value it EQUALS, or to any member of an enum whose domain is not fully known. That last clause is `enumValueDomainIsComplete`, and it is where tsc's *computed* enum lives: **a member with no initializer in an AMBIENT non-const enum has NO value in tsc**, so `declare enum D { X, Y }; let d: D = 7` is legal — we auto-number those because the Transformer needs a value to emit, and B203, which had no ambient notion at all, reported it. Split into `ts2322KeepsSourceLiteral` (display) and `enumNumericLiteralSource` (source, restricted to a `Type.NumberLiteral` by construction) it is 46 again. An enum-MEMBER target also prints QUALIFIED now (`E.A`, not `A`) — `formatTypeForDisplay` reduces a `QualifiedName` to its last name, which is right for a namespaced interface and wrong for an enum member. **STILL LOAD-BEARING, unchanged:** `checkEnumToEnumAssignments` (all 12 of `enumAssignmentCompat3`) and `checkNamespaceEnumUnionAssignments` (2 of 5 of `enumLiteralAssignableToEnumInsideUnion`) — both are enum-to-enum VALUE identity, a different rule from this one._
