_Round 743 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1) PART 1 DONE: the overload picker was doing TWO things wrong, and neither is about enums.** Corpus **12,949 -> 12,956 / 0 failures / 8 skipped** (+7 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` **BYTE-IDENTICAL at 46** across both commits. **(1) The B136 concrete-overload SWAP re-picked an overload `resolveCallOverload` had ALREADY REJECTED.** When the chosen signature's return still carries an un-inferred type parameter, the swap looks for a non-generic overload with a concrete return — and took the FIRST such overload **without ever asking whether it accepts the arguments**, which is how `factory.createToken(SyntaxKind.ReadonlyKeyword)` selected the correct generic `<TKind extends ModifierSyntaxKind>` overload and was then swapped back to `createToken(token: SyntaxKind.SuperKeyword): SuperExpression` (checker.ts:7997). The omission was invisible for the Array predicate pair the rule was written for, where the non-generic overload accepts whatever the generic one does. The type-based verdict is now extracted as `signatureAcceptsArgs` so any site adopting a signature asks the SAME question. **THE REDUCTION NEEDED THE GENERIC OVERLOAD** — two same-arity NON-generic overloads distinguished only by an enum-member parameter already selected correctly via the round-459 AST gate, which is why round 742's two earlier reductions came back clean and blamed the barrel and the enum size. **(2) Overload selection could not see an ASSERT narrow.** `getTypeOfIdentifier` answers from `currentLocalTypes` and the declaration tables: an `if (isFoo(x))` narrow is recorded there, an `asserts` narrow lives ONLY in the flow graph behind `getNarrowedTypeForReference`, which every emission site opts into individually and selection never did — so `Debug.assert(isKeywordOrPunctuation(kind)); tokenToString(kind)` picked the `string | undefined` overload (parser.ts:2494). Fixed as a SECOND CHANCE on the rejecting path only, so it can only turn a rejection into an acceptance. **Both reductions are ENUM-FREE**, which is what makes the pins bite on main. Cost gate: +0.16% on commit 1; on commit 2 `narrow.memoServed` +9.93% and `typeNode.bypassed` +23.26%, **justified and rebaselined** — 87% of the extra narrowing requests are served by the round-664 memo (`narrow.walks` only +0.83%) and the wall is flat interleaved (26,098/26,593 vs 26,312/26,394 ms). **PART 2, (REL.1)(b): 4 profile FPs -> 2, still parked, now on `wip/round743-rel1b`** (all five `@Ignore`s flip ON there). Remaining: `utilities.ts:4175` (intersection-over-union distribution) and `declarations.ts:846` (`if (!input) return` failing to strip `undefined` from a `T | undefined` where T is a constrained type parameter). **Neither reduced — a hand-built model of each shape passes**, which per round 742's rule means a missing ingredient, not an absent bug. (c) the scaffolding deletions stay open._

_Round 742 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1)(b0) DONE: an enum-member EXPRESSION is a member type, not `any` — and that was the blocker under (b), not the relation.** Step (a) minted the member type but only ANNOTATIONS could reach it: an enum's own `Type.Object` carries no member table, so `getPropertyOfType` missed `E.A` and `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` fell through to its `anyType` tail. That is why round 741 could flip three of the four `EnumMemberRelationTest` expectations from the relation but not `const e: E.X = E.Y` — the relation was being handed `any`, however sharp its rule was — and why round 740's `take(Ext.Dts)` control was vacuous. **Corpus 12,940 -> 12,949 / 0 failures / 8 skipped (+9 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` back to 46 (byte-identical output), cost gate max +0.35% on the first commit (rebaselined) and +0.00% on the second.** **THE AUDIT, NOT SITE-BY-SITE FIREFIGHTING.** Round 741's gotcha says every classifier whose rule is "an enum value is a number/string at runtime, never an object" must accept `Enum or EnumMember`; six more sites moved to `isEnumFlavoredObjectType` and the two arithmetic classifiers now answer PER MEMBER from the member's own constant. **Two were WRONG in a direction a mechanical widening would have missed:** `comparabilityCategory`'s blanket `EnumLiteral -> number` would have called a STRING member a number and FP'd a cross-category TS2365, and the discriminant filter's "an object can never strictly-equal a primitive literal" rule silently stopped excluding enums the moment a discriminant was a member type. **THREE PRE-EXISTING BUGS THE `any` HAD BEEN MASKING — fixed at the root, because they ARE the content.** (i) `isComparableType` never resolved a TYPE PARAMETER's constraint, so `token >= SyntaxKind.FirstToken` with `TKind extends SyntaxKind` FP'd 6 TS2365 (the sibling rule had always been in `isValidArithmeticOperand`; only the relational path lacked it). (ii) The arithmetic pass's first-wins local recording refused a nested body's own `const state = exception.state` because an ENCLOSING body's `let state: Identifier` was still in the INHERITED locals map — `spineArithInheritedName` decides inheritance by instance identity against the innermost saved map, so first-wins still holds within a scope. (iii) `checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore` has its OWN inline literal-widener, separate from `widenType`, and it is what the TS2322 assignment check reads as the target's declared type — so `let flags = TransformFlags.None; flags = TransformFlags.ContainsESNext`, the way tsc starts every flags accumulator, became an error; the same widening must also DISTRIBUTE over a union (`let variance = cond ? VarianceFlags.Invariant : ...`). Those two widening paths alone were **23 of the 28** FPs that (b)-on-top-of-(b0) showed. Member types also now print QUALIFIED (`SyntaxKind.Identifier`), as tsc does — the bare member name is also an unrelated interface's name. **(b) IS PARKED AGAIN, on `wip/round742-rel1b`, where ALL FIVE `@Ignore`s flip ON** (including the fourth, which (b0) unblocked, and the fifth, which the deletion of step (a)'s string TARGET leg unblocks — tsc rejects `string -> Ext.Dts`). **What blocks it is EXACTLY round 741's four FPs** (checker.ts:7997 TS2769, parser.ts:2494 TS2345, declarations.ts:846 TS2322, utilities.ts:4175 TS2322): that the count returns to FOUR after a change putting member types into every enum-member expression in the program is itself evidence for round 741's read that these are relation/narrowing gaps passing vacuously, not enum work. **Diagnosed, not fixed: checker.ts:7997 is an OVERLOAD-PICK bug with the enum as witness** — `factory.createToken(SyntaxKind.ReadonlyKeyword)` picks the FIRST overload (`token: SyntaxKind.SuperKeyword`) and returns `SuperExpression`; reduced to a standalone repro, two same-arity overloads whose parameters are DIFFERENT enum members pick the first, while the same pair distinguished by ARITY picks correctly. (c) the scaffolding deletions stay open._

_Round 741 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1) STEP (a) DONE: the enum-MEMBER type exists, and it changed no answer.** `SK.A` used to resolve to **`anyType`** — `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker` had no `SymbolFlags.EnumMember` branch — so `kind: SK.A` and `kind: SK.B` were literally the SAME `Type` instance and the relation was never asked a question it could get wrong. It now mints a distinct `Type.Object(Object or EnumLiteral)` per member, **interned on `"<canonicalEnumSymbol.id>#<memberName>"`** (a per-SYMBOL mint would hand two non-equal types to one member — the exact catastrophe `canonicalEnumSymbol`'s own doc records — because the same enum arrives as the merged global, a file-local and a barrel-resolved alias), and the enum's own type is flagged `Enum`, so **`TypeFlags.EnumLiteral` has a writer for the first time and the widening rule written for it at Checker.kt:143100 finally executes.** **Behaviour-preserving by construction and by measurement: corpus 12,935 -> 12,940 / 0 failures / 8 skipped (+5 pins, +1 `@Ignore`), compiler profile `--listAll` BYTE-IDENTICAL at 46 errors (whole output), cost gate max +0.40% (rebaselined, justified: unions that used to collapse to one `anyType` member now carry their real constituents).** The entire measured gap was **one rule family, enum member <-> its base primitive, in both directions** — and the mechanism is one line: `isNumericEnumObjectType`/`isStringEnumObjectType` classify by `SymbolFlags.Enum` on the type's symbol, which a MEMBER symbol does not carry, so neither pre-existing rule could fire. An ablation build (legs deleted) fails all four leg pins. The legs are deliberately **value-blind**: `let a: E.A = 2` where `A === 0` is still `checkEnumLiteralAssignments`' error, and the relation co-emitting it is the 4 spurious TS2322 round 740's probe measured. **The one knock-on was not union collapse but a CLASSIFIER: `typeof x === "object"` asks `symbol.flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Enum)`, false for a member, so `ModuleKind.ESNext` survived a narrow it must not survive (program.ts:1341 TS2339). Closed with `isEnumFlavoredObjectType` at both sites of that family — recorded as a CLAUDE.md gotcha, because every "an enum value is a number/string, never an object" classifier now has to accept `Enum or EnumMember`.** Both round-740 latent hazards closed too (`SK.A` was looked up under the bare `"A"` in `currentTypeParamScope`/`currentTypeAliasArgs` and in the round-444 `globals[name]` recovery). Also found: one of round 740's four positive controls (`take(Ext.Dts)`) is VACUOUS — an enum-member EXPRESSION types as the enum, never as a member — which is why the new pins annotate through `declare const d: Ext.Dts`. **(b) WAS attempted and is PARKED on `wip/round741-rel1b-disjointness`: the one-line rule flips 3 of the 4 `@Ignore`s ON but costs 4 profile FPs (46 -> 50) in one family — an enum-member union that used to collapse to a single `anyType` member now has real constituents, so a relation/narrowing path that passed VACUOUSLY stops passing (overload pick, assertion-narrow, intersection-over-union). The 4th pin cannot flip from the relation at all: an enum-member ACCESS EXPRESSION types as the ENUM, so only ANNOTATIONS reach the new type. And the obvious hypothesis for the FPs — one member splitting into several `Type` instances because `canonicalEnumSymbol` cannot canonicalize a module-scoped enum post-INV.3(d) — is measured FALSE: tsc's structural `isEnumTypeRelatedTo` verdict leaves the profile at the same 4.** (c) the scaffolding deletions stay open._

_Round 740 (2026-07-28) — **(PERF.HW) DONE: the cores are REAL and the item's premise is still wrong — a SEQUENTIAL xtsc run already consumes 3.15 of the 4 cores, so 79% of the machine is spoken for before the first worker exists.** The item asked "does this VPS have real cores, so M2 is worth reviving", with unpark condition ">= 8 real cores". **The box: `nproc` 4, AMD EPYC-Rome @ 2445 MHz, 4 distinct `core id`s with ONE thread sibling each (no SMT), NO cgroup `cpu.max`, and steal time 0.0 mean / 0 max over 72 vmstat samples.** Steal alone does not settle it (a hard quota need not be accounted as steal), so the cores were tested directly with a tiny-working-set pure-CPU loop: **1.00x / 1.56x / 3.45x / 3.61x at 1/2/4/8-way — four real, independent, unthrottled cores.** **THE SCALING TABLE** (compiler profile, `--noEmit`, -Xmx4g, 3 reps, ROUND-ROBIN interleaved across levels rather than round 666's blocks): **w1 27,126 ms | w2 24,452 (per-rep median -11.67%, wins 3/3) | w4 25,976 (-4.24%, wins 2/3, per-rep deltas STRADDLE ZERO = undecided) | w8 32,212 (+19.37%, wins 0/3)**, drift band re-derived from w1's own reps at **+-2.87%**. That reproduces round 666 (seq 27,873 / w2 24,669 / w4 flat) and adds the w8 point. **THE EXPLANATION, which no round had measured: 85.6 s of USER CPU for a 27.1 s wall = 3.15 cores in a run everybody calls single-threaded.** Attributed by starvation: `-XX:CICompilerCount=2` drops it to 2.34 cores / 62.8 s user (**JIT ~21.7 s of CPU**), `-XX:ParallelGCThreads=1 -XX:ConcGCThreads=1` to 3.06 (**GC only ~2.7 s**) — C2 compiling a ~110k-line `Checker.kt` never finishes inside a 27 s run. **Self time is FLAT across all four configurations (26.6-27.8 s), so the JIT is not stealing from the compile thread** (round 618's "flag hunting is dead" holds) — it is consuming the cores a WORKER would need, leaving ~0.85 free. Every level then saturates at the same **~3.6-core ceiling** (3.49/3.57/3.65) and what actually changes with worker count is TOTAL WORK: **user CPU w1 85.6 s -> w2 85.3 (+0%) -> w4 92.6 (+8%) -> w8 117.7 (+37%)**, because each worker re-binds every file and runs all ~318 collectors. w2 divides without adding and banks -11.7%; w4/w8 add CPU to a machine with none left, and it converts straight into wall. **NOT a JIT artifact — tested and negative: freeing ~0.34 cores at w4 (`CICompilerCount=2`, 3.62 -> 3.28 cores) left the wall unmoved (25,870 -> 25,574).** Four INDEPENDENT concurrent compiles took 3.85x each (aggregate throughput 1.03x) — which is **82% parallel efficiency** once the 3.15 figure is applied (4 x 85.6 core-seconds on 4 cores floors at 86 s; measured 105 s). **The box parallelises fine; our compile does not, because one copy already occupies 79% of it.** Amdahl: the w1/w2 fit gives P = 5,348 ms divisible (**19.7%**), R = 21,778 ms — infinite-worker floor **-19.7% (1.25x) ever**; the w1/w4 fit gives P = 5.7%, disagreeing 3.5x, so per the rule fixed before the run the model is contention-broken beyond w2 (and 19.7% vs round 666's 23% is NOT resolvable — P is twice a delta whose per-rep spread is 1,554 ms). **ALSO FOUND, a correctness gap: `--workers N` IS NOT BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING — 62 diagnostics vs sequential 46.** The 16 extras are one family in one file (`utilities.ts:11349..11410`, TS2322 `EvaluatorResult<T>` not assignable to `EvaluatorResult`), **identical at w2/w4/w8** and deterministic across reps — the round-609 signature (a program-wide collector iterating the INV.6 partition view instead of `binderResults`), not a race. **VERDICT: M2 stays parked, and the unpark condition is REWRITTEN** — ">= 8 real cores" is necessary but insufficient; the measured requirement is >= 8 cores *net of the ~3.2 the JVM's own JIT/GC consume*, i.e. realistically **>= 12**, and that tax is FIXED per JVM (it does not grow with workers), so a larger host simply out-sizes it. Revival order: close the divergence, shrink R, only then re-probe. Predictions **5 of 6** (P5 falsified: w8 peak RSS 2,240 MB fits -Xmx4g easily, GC 1.1 s of the 5.4 s regression — **no level was skipped for want of RAM**; memory was never the constraint). No `src/` change -> no suite run (12,927 stands). Artifact: `docs/perf/worker-scaling-round740.md`. **PART 2, (REL.1) DECOMPOSED AND SIZED (fix deliberately NOT attempted): the blast radius everybody feared is ONE corpus baseline, and the root cause is not "the relation is lenient" — it is `anyType`.** The item said "every enum-typed comparison in the corpus goes through this, TS2322/TS2367/TS2345 baselines included". Measured: **12,927 tests, 1 failure.** **ROOT CAUSE LOCATED:** `getTypeFromTypeReference` reduces `SK.A` to the BARE member name `"A"`, resolves it to a `SymbolFlags.EnumMember` symbol, and `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker` **has no branch for that flag** — it falls to `else -> anyType`, so `SK.A` and `SK.B` are *the same `Type` instance* and the relation was never asked a question it could get wrong. **Corollary: `TypeFlags.EnumLiteral` is SET NOWHERE — all ~11 read sites are dead, including a widening rule at Checker.kt:143100 that is already written and waiting for a flag that never arrives.** **MEASURED with a throwaway 3-edit probe, then REVERTED** (proof: `git diff src/commonMain` empty + profile `--listAll` byte-identical at 46 errors + cost gate all 20 counters +0.00%): corpus **1 failure**, compiler profile **46 -> 52 (+6)**, **no perf cost** (26,192 ms in the 26.5-27.1 s band). **AND THE ONE FAILURE IS THE MISSING LEG, NOT THE ADDED ONE** — 4 spurious `Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'A'`: a numeric enum member must stay assignable FROM `number` and from a literal equal to its value. The profile's +6 is the same family (`Extension.Dts`, a STRING member, not assignable to `string`). **The entire measured gap is ONE rule family: enum member <-> its base primitive — so this is a SESSION, not an arc.** Decomposition written into the queue item: (a) mint the type + the base-primitive legs, behaviour-preserving by construction; (b) add disjointness (acceptance = the four `@Ignore`s flip on); (c) delete the scaffolding one walker per commit. **Deletable: `discriminantPropAnnotation`, `kindDomainProvesNotSubtype`, `kindDomainKeysExceed` and THREE whole AST-only passes** (`checkEnumLiteralAssignments`, `checkNamespaceEnumUnionAssignments`, `checkEnumToEnumAssignments`), plus twelve AST key-space helpers; **but `canonicalEnumSymbol` SURVIVES** — its duplicate-`Symbol`-instance problem is independent of the relation and a naive per-symbol mint reproduces the catastrophe its doc records. Two latent hazards recorded (`SK.A` is looked up under the bare name `"A"` in `currentTypeParamScope`/`currentTypeAliasArgs` and falls back to `globals["A"]` — invisible only because everything collapses to `any`). Suite **12,927 -> 12,935 / 0 / 7** (+8 `EnumMemberRelationTest`, 4 `@Ignore`d pins naming the item + 4 NOT-ignored positive controls that are the FP firewall for step (a))._

_Round 736 (2026-07-27) — **(CALL.3) DONE, and the M5 attribution arc has its FIRST LANDED WIN: `-4.53%` median wall on the compiler profile, B wins 6/6 interleaved pairs, outside the +-2% drift band by 2.3x.** Round 735 handed forward 394 narrowing walks costing 1,485 ms and forbade designing before two numbers existed; both were measured with a new opt-in probe (`NarrowSections`/`NarrowProbe`, `--narrowSections{,Coarse}`, behaviour-free when off). **(i) ARRIVALS vs DISTINCT: a `>= 1 ms` walk arrives at 1,900 flow nodes but only 214 DISTINCT ones — revisit factor 8.85 against 1.48 for a typical walk. The tail was never a bigger graph; it is the same small graph walked nine times. (ii) THE PER-ARRIVAL SPLIT: 51% of the entire narrowing population is `applyConditionNarrowing` (1,412 ms / 759,784 calls / 1,858 ns), and the tail's arrivals are 6.3x costlier purely because of their MIX — `FlowCondition` is 41% of tail arrivals against 18% overall, `FlowBranchLabel` 22% against 9%, while cheap `FlowCall` pass-throughs fall 57% -> 19%.** **THE CAUSE WAS ONE CONDITION.** The intra-walk memo `NarrowFlowMemo.served(id, depth)` answered only when `depth <= storedDepth`, so a flow node reached again by a LONGER path recomputed its whole antecedent subtree — **631,585 arrivals compile-wide, 426,753 of them at `FlowCondition` nodes, against 290,011 serves.** That condition guards exactly one thing (a deeper entry has less budget before `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH`=2000), and that is DECIDABLE, not approximable: `depth` influences the result through no other channel, and only non-truncated results are ever stored, so an entry now carries `hi` = the max depth its own subtree reached and serves a deeper probe iff `depth + (hi - storedDepth) < maxDepth`. The one non-obvious part: a served hit must fold `depth + servedHeight` into the CALLER's height or the disjunct goes unsound under nesting. Mirrored into `narrowTypeFromFlowFollowLoopEntry`. **EFFECT: invocations 1,455,915 -> 659,592 (-55%); arrivals 4,759,476 -> 3,500,214 (-26%) with DISTINCT nodes UNCHANGED at 3,212,764; `applyConditionNarrowing` calls 759,784 -> 333,031 (-56%); the `>= 1 ms` tail 429 -> 230 walks and its arrivals 815,259 -> 34,490 (-96%).** Distinct unchanged to the node is the signature of a correct memoisation change. Context that made the argument checkable: maxDepth reached is 249 of 2000 and depth/budget/cycle truncations are 0/0/0. **WHAT DID NOT WORK, both priced: (1) a "does this condition mention the name" pre-test — 95.6% of `applyConditionNarrowing` calls return their INPUT unchanged, but they are the CHEAP tail at 949 ns against 21,708 ns for the calls that narrow, so the whole population is ~410 ms net = 1.3%, INSIDE the band before the pre-test's own cost; (2) memoising the fast-forward chain's pass-throughs — `FlowCall` is 57% of arrivals and the biggest miss population in the table, and also the cheapest at ~131 ns each, so its entire revisit share is <=120 ms.** Corpus 12,899 -> **12,910 / 0 / 3** (+4 `IntKeyMapTest` height pins, +7 `NarrowMemoDepthTest`); profile `--listAll` **byte-identical A vs B** (46 errors); cost gate `output.errors`/`spine.nodes`/`narrow.walks`/`typeOfExpr.distinct`/`mapped.*` all +0.00% and **four counters FELL and were rebaselined in the same commit** (`typeNode.cacheable` -10.6%, `typeNode.cacheHits` -14.6%, `globals.lookups` -8.4%, `globals.misses` -8.5%, all downstream of the 56% cut). Full derivation: `docs/perf/narrow-walk-attribution.md`. Follow-on queued as **(CALL.4)**._

_Round 728 (2026-07-27) — **THREE causes cracked in one session, and the three that remain are now ISOLATED IN LIB-FREE REPROS instead of described. _Round 727 (2026-07-27) — **TWO families cracked in one session, both by triaging all eight remaining sites by CAUSE before touching anything. Real-lib false positives 14 → 7 (arm C 27 → 23 → 20, MEASURED at every step; TS2339 ×4 → 0 and TS2345 ×4 → ×1, not one other code moved); corpus 12,795 → 12,804 / 0 / 3 (+9 pins); cost gate PASSES with the largest counter −0.68% (an improvement); embedded-lib compiler profile still 46.** THE DEFECT: `narrowByCallPredicate`'s SINGLE-TYPE positive branch compared the reference against the guard target AS A WHOLE (`candidate <: t ? candidate : t <: candidate ? t : candidate`), so when the target is a UNION and neither whole-union relation holds it handed back the ENTIRE candidate union. tsc's `getNarrowedType` never compares against the whole candidate — it `mapType`s over it and keeps, per constituent, whichever of the two is the subtype, dropping constituents unrelated in both directions. The live shape is esDecorators.ts `visitAssignmentElement`'s `isAssignmentExpression(node, true) && isNamedEvaluation(node, …)`, where `NamedEvaluation` is a 9-member union of unrelated `X & { … }` intersections; a union's property set is the INTERSECTION of its members', so every later `node.left`/`node.right`/`node.operatorToken` FP'd. The fix filters the constituents on the previously-`else` path only (both relating branches stay byte-identical) and keeps the whole-union fallback when nothing survives. **STATED PLAINLY because it is invisible in the numbers:** in this shape exactly ONE constituent survives, and our TS2339 walker does not fire on an intersection (or plain-interface) receiver at all — so part of the suppression comes from a diagnostic class that does not exist. The narrowing itself is independently correct, dumped via revealing TS2322s, and where two constituents survive the result is a union on which TS2339 does fire; that is what the second pin exercises. **PROBE DISCIPLINE, first attempt useless:** two negative controls came back silent WITH the fix and read as "it deleted a diagnostic class" — they had landed on that same intersection gate and would have been silent on HEAD too. The discriminating control keeps TWO constituents alive, so a dropped constituent's property becomes legal while a property on only one survivor STAYS an error; both targets were then verified FAILING on unmodified HEAD. **THE SECOND FAMILY, same session, same method:** the TS2345 ×3 are `(x ||= new Set()).add(path)` — the REAL lib's `SetConstructor { new <T = any>(…): Set<T> }` let the CONSTRUCT SIGNATURE's own type parameter escape, so the receiver was `Set<Path> | Set<T>` and the B516 union-of-callables rule CORRECTLY intersected the two `add` parameters into `Path & T`; the combining rule was never the bug, the leaked TP was. Fixed by substituting an uninferred signature type parameter's DECLARED DEFAULT — TypeScript's own rule — only where one exists and only at the return reference's top level. The corpus cannot see it (the EMBEDDED lib's `SetConstructor` is non-generic `new(): Set<any>`), which is what made it safe; `new Map()` does not reproduce even under real libs, because `MapConstructor` has a non-generic `new()` overload that wins. Its pin's discriminating control runs the OPPOSITE way — with `new <T = number>()` the substitution makes a diagnostic APPEAR, which is far easier to prove than one that removes a class of them. **OPERATIONAL:** two builds lost to OOM under 1 GB available, one a full `jvmTest` that ran 7m31s and wrote ZERO result XMLs — read an empty results directory as "the build died", not as "everything failed"._

_Round 726 (2026-07-27) — **the TS2322 family cracked by DUMPING the relation instead of guessing a third time. Real-lib false positives 18 → 14 (arm C 31 → 27, BOTH SIDES MEASURED; TS2322 ×7 → ×3 and not one other code moved); corpus 12,788 → 12,795 / 0 / 3 (+7 pins); cost gate PASSES with the largest counter −0.68% (an improvement); embedded-lib compiler profile still 46.** THE DEFECT: `getTypeFromTypeReference` instantiated `Iface<A>` into a `Type.Reference` only when EVERY type argument resolved, and otherwise fell through to `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(Iface)` — the RAW OPEN GENERIC, which carries its OWN type parameter and is a different type from any instantiation of it. Nothing relates a `Type.Reference` to that, so every comparison against such an annotation FP'd. On the real libs the unresolved argument is the function's OWN type parameter: a return annotation is resolved with `currentTypeParamScope == null`, so its `T` comes back errorType — which is exactly why the message read `Type 'NodeArray<T>' is not assignable to type 'NodeArray<T>'`, identical text, because the DISPLAY renders the annotation while the COMPARISON used the raw generic. THE FIX mirrors tsc, whose `errorType` is Any-flagged and instantiates regardless: substitute `any` for the unresolved positions and instantiate anyway (the unresolved name is already reported by TS2304 — this only stops the cascade). Fixed sites: parser.ts:3583, watchPublic.ts:371/383, utilities.ts:12378. **WHY TWO ROUNDS OF GUESSING MISSED IT, and the transferable lesson:** the display string was the trap all along, and three separate synthetic probes (overloads, callback-parameter inference, cross-file generics, real libs on) came back CLEAN — the shape only becomes visible when the interface reaches its type parameter through a GENERIC BASE (`extends ReadonlyArray<T>`, real `NodeArray`'s shape); a flat `interface Box<T> { v: T }` relates to its own raw form anyway, so a pin built on one is silent BEFORE and AFTER and proves nothing. **THE INSTRUMENTATION LESSON that cost two compiles:** a debug helper reading a `private val` declared AFTER the class's `init` block reads NULL, because `init` runs the passes — the probe printed nothing and looked like "wrong code path". Hooking `Diagnostic`'s constructor instead named the true emission site in one run. **PINNED by three targets and four negative controls** — a resolvable-but-wrong argument still errors, an unresolvable argument does not mask an unrelated source, does not mask missing members (TS2740 still fires), and the good case stays silent._

_Round 725 (2026-07-27) — **round 724's confirmed defect FIXED, and it cost nothing. THE FIX evaluates only inside the already-failing branch, hence free: take each TypeParam constituent's constraint (`T ⊆ constraint(T)`) and compare it, with a SEPARATELY GATED second step dropping nullish members when an `{}` constituent is present, since `X & {}` is tsc's non-nullish marker. **THE TRAP, pinned by four negative controls:** a blanket `is Type.Intersection -> continue` passes every target case and silently deletes a real diagnostic class — controls cover `A & B` with no type parameter, `NonNullable` of an unconstrained parameter, `NonNullable` of a parameter constrained to an unrelated type, and a nullable-constrained parameter used WITHOUT `NonNullable`. Combining both spawned two concurrent builds racing on one output directory. And a `ps` pattern for a gradle client must match `gradle-wrapper.jar`, not `GradleWrapperMain` — the wrong one reports "nothing running" while a build is live._

The mechanism is not type-level: TS2722 gates on `isOptionalProperty`, and a homomorphic mapped member carries its SOURCE declaration, so the source's `?` is what the scan sees; fixed exactly as M1.10 fixed `-readonly`, with a `mappedRequiredMemberIds` side-channel probed only on the already-optional branch — hence zero cost. Real-lib arm C 48 → 37, TS2722 ×11 → 0, no other code moved. **BUILD.1 settled with a ladder:** 2g GC-thrashes forever, 3g dies mid-compile, 4g fails in ~6 min with an explicit `Not enough memory to run compilation`, 5g succeeds in ~6.5 min; Gradle's own daemon cut 2g → 1g because 5g + 2g oversubscribed the 7.7 GB box and the kernel killed the compile daemon silently. **TWO OF MY OWN DIAGNOSES WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED:** round 719 blamed a local bench loop (those commits are `github-actions[bot]` — remote CI), and rounds 718–719 recorded "40-minute compiles" that were ~6-minute ones **restarted by my own polling**, since each Bash call preempted the previous backgrounded one and killed the build with its shell. Rule: launch long builds DETACHED (`nohup … &`) and wait on ONE timer; if what you have awaited for 25 minutes is 90 seconds old, you are killing it._

_Round 718 (2026-07-26) — **a complete fix for 11 of (LIB.1)'s 35 real-lib false positives — diagnosed, written, and NOT LANDED, because four cold compiles in one session and none would gate it. Parked on `wip/round718-required-minus-optional`; main clean and unchanged at 12,765 / 0 / 3.** THE BUG: `Required<T>` is `{ [P in keyof T]-?: T[P] }`, and the parser records `-?` as a plain `?` — so `Required<T>` behaves as `Partial<T>`, inverted. `-readonly` got its own flag in M1.10; the `?` analogue never did. It costs 11 FPs because tsc declares `tracker: Required<Pick<SymbolTracker, "reportInferenceFallback">>`. **THE MECHANISM IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE:** TS2722 reads like a type question, but the emitter gates on `isOptionalProperty(propSym)` and the codebase deliberately never adds `| undefined` for optional-member access — what bites is the M1.10 trap repeating, a homomorphic mapped member CARRYING ITS SOURCE DECLARATION, so the source's `?` is what the scan sees. Fix is M1.10's mirror: a `mappedRequiredMemberIds` side-channel probed only when the declaration says optional. **TWO DEAD ENDS, BOTH CAUGHT BY CONTROLS:** hand-rolling the mapped types locally reproduces NOTHING (we emit nothing for user-defined mapped types — the controls came back empty and three target assertions were passing vacuously, and would have 'confirmed' no fix at all); asserting assignability through `Partial` measures an axis we do not model. Only `@useRealLibs` + TS2722 reproduces, verified against unmodified HEAD with the target failing and the control passing. **WHY IT DID NOT LAND:** `-Xmx2g` hung, `-Xmx3g` died and restarted after 16 min, only `-Xmx4g` got the main compile through — so (BUILD.1) is escalated from nuisance to the binding constraint on the edit-test loop, its proposal revised 3g → 4g, with a bigger box offered as the alternative. **Also:** the embedded lib declares NO utility types at all, which is why this family is invisible by default — the name degrades to `any`, and `any` is silent._

_Round 717 (2026-07-26) — **the two GATE items landed: logical parity became a MECHANISM with two build-failing controls, and cost became a counter diff. Corpus 12,765 / 0 / 3, unchanged; generated corpus byte-identical.** **(PARITY.1):** `docs/logical-parity.md` carries the form-vs-meaning procedure as two ALLOWLIST tables (7 meaning axes / 6 form axes with their equivalence obligations; anything in neither is MEANING by default), and `logicalParityDivergences` in build.gradle.kts is the single source of truth — the generator emits a switched-off baseline `@Ignore`d so it stays VISIBLE as skipped (a vanished test hides behind an unchanged total), regenerates the ledger into the doc so it cannot drift, and FAILS the build on either rot mode: a baseline matching no generated test, or a `pinnedBy` class that does not exist. That second control is what makes "replace it with a test pinning the logic" mechanical rather than aspirational. Empty list is the healthy state and the generated tree is byte-identical with it. **(COST.1):** `scripts/cost_gate.py` — 20 deterministic `--passTiming` counters diffed against `docs/perf/cost-counters.txt`, failing above ±2%; counters, never wall time. Baseline: 46 errors, 856,962 spine nodes, 696,933 getTypeOfExpression calls over 250,057 distinct nodes, 69,903 narrowing walks, 89,883 bypassed type-node resolutions, 1,377,511 globals lookups at 98.9% miss. It also pins the compiler's ANSWER, because a cost drop that changes the output is not a win. **THE FINDING:** two runs of the same binary agreed bit-for-bit on 19 of 20 counters and disagreed on the AST census (857,350 vs 854,550) — `indexSourceFile` runs on the crawl's concurrent parse threads while `PassTiming.nodeKindHistogram` is a plain HashMap, so it loses increments and always undercounts; harmless for "which kinds dominate", fatal for the exact per-kind population DISPATCH.1 needs. Excluded from the gate, warned at the source, flagged on that item. **Plus a build trap worth half an hour:** the documented `pkill -9 KotlinCompileDaemon` ritual makes the next compile COLD, which does not fit the inherited `-Xmx2g` and HANGS rather than failing (350% CPU, RSS at the ceiling, zero classes written for 14 min); `-Dkotlin.daemon.jvmargs=-Xmx3g` finished it in 2m 33s — queued as (BUILD.1), owner-gated. Warning-clean restored (3 stale Checker.kt warnings)._

_Round 737 (2026-07-27) — **(TYPE.1) DONE: ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK section 0.1 STAGE 3 IS STRUCK — its MECHANISM is exactly right and its SIZE is wrong by 3.2x.** The 701,463 `getTypeOfExpression` calls were attributed BY CALLER for the first time (`--typeOfExprCallers`, opt-in, behaviour-free when off; only the OUTERMOST call walks the stack, so a whole expression subtree is attributed to the handler that ASKED for it and the recursion cannot inflate a caller's own factor). **The claim "several handlers independently type the same node" is CONFIRMED and pervasive: 177 sites initiate typing, 45.2% of the 254,069 typed nodes (114,750) carry MORE THAN ONE origin — modal count three, maximum 17 — and 75.8% of all calls land on them. The x2.76 factor decomposes as 2.05x CROSS-HANDLER times 1.34x recursion, and per-caller factors are 1.00-1.11 everywhere: no handler re-types anything by itself.** **But the money is not there.** A PERFECT per-node cache — the ceiling for this stage in ANY shape — saves **823 ms = 2.9% of a 28.7 s compile**; single-visit discipline **670 ms = 2.3%**; the largest handler-pair merge in the compiler **166 ms = 0.58%** against a re-derived +-2% band of ~590 ms; and the SOUND memo re-measures at **46 ms**. **NOTHING WAS LANDED**, correctly. **Two corrections forced: (1) the often-quoted "getTypeOfExpression = 3,911 ms" is a DOUBLE COUNT** (`typeOfExprNanos` charges a subtree once per nesting level) — the true total cost of all expression typing is **2,439 ms = 8.5% of checker-init**, which was always this stage's ceiling; **(2) 74.4% of the calls are OUTERMOST**, so recursion never was the explanation. **Section 0's law again, in its sharpest instance and with no cache in it: sorted by COUNT the co-occurrence head is 141,388 repeat typings worth 71 ms (0.5 us each) — the property-access receiver trio at 90,948 repeats for 51 ms, `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` re-entering itself at 30,040 for 6 ms — while sorted by TIME the head is 2,603 repeat typings worth 166 ms (64 us each). The two orderings share nothing; what is redundantly recomputed is what was already cheap.** Prediction scored: P1 FALSIFIED (>50% nested; really 25.6%), P2 HELD, P3 HELD (top-3 pairs 41% of the redundant time), P4 FALSIFIED (top-5 origins 52.5%, not >=60%). Method note: the probe costs **16.7 us per attributed outermost call** (`StackWalker`), 190x round 735's 89 ns timestamp read, so the round is deliberately COUNT-heavy — every deterministic counter is byte-identical to the plain run and to `cost-counters.txt`. Corpus 12,910 -> **12,916 / 0 / 3** (+6 `TypeOfExprCallerAttributionTest` pins); profile `--listAll` byte-identical production vs attribution (46 errors); cost gate all 20 counters +0.00%. Full derivation: `docs/perf/type-of-expression-attribution.md`. Follow-on queued as **(TYPE.2)** — `checkVarDeclAssignability` under `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor`, the largest single typing origin (431 ms over 11,933 initializers = 36 us each) inside the third-largest spine handler (2,900 ms) that no round has opened._

Both halves pinned (`EnumShadowedInFunctionScopeTest`), the retirement target landed early._

_Round 788 (2026-08-01) — **(ENGINE.2b) IS CLOSED: BOTH LEVERS LANDED AND THEY ANSWER DIFFERENTLY. Equivalence was MEASURED — an in-binary differential ran both implementations at every query, **15,483 on compiler + 26,119 on harness, 0 mismatches** — then pinned by a test that carries the replaced scan as its OWN reference implementation and compares at every position, so those pins need no ablated binary to have teeth. **(a)** the pre-gate skips **49,003 of 51,967** calls; `typeOfExpr.calls` **-3.27%**, `globals.lookups` **-2.75%**, `globals.misses` **-2.75%**, `narrow.memoServed` **-5.09%**, `output.errors`/`spine.nodes` +0.00%; but the timed row falls **342 -> 233 ms**, not the 265 predicted, and the engine row rises by a comparable amount. The 403 ms estimate is **not confirmed at the wall**; the deterministic counters are the whole measured result. Worth carrying: a warm rebuild is 11.9 s against a ~27 s cold compile, so a saving the JIT has already compiled is a smaller FRACTION of a warm run than of a cold one. _Round 794 (2026-08-01) — **(ENGINE.2f): THE UNION LOOP-ENTRY RETRY IS SUBSTITUTED, NOT SKIPPED — 916 OF ITS 1,859 CALLS SERVED FROM THE PLAIN WALK'S OWN RESULT, `narrow.walks` −916 EXACTLY, ~30 ms (0.11%) — AND THE ROUND'S NEGATIVE RESULT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX.** The prize was re-measured at HEAD before any code (law 1) and had survived rounds 792-793 intact: **1,859 reaching calls, 66 ms, 49% loop-free**. It was then split into its own sub-measure (`N_U_RETRY_LF`, identical boundary count in both arms so no round-793 boundary correction applies) rather than extrapolated as `49% × 66 ms`: **32 ms → 2 ms**. **The item's veto on copying (ENGINE.2d)(a) is correct and load-bearing**: the consumer's first test is the IDENTITY `loopNarrowed !== rawForNarrowing`, and a loop-free repeat crossing a two-antecedent `FlowBranchLabel` mints a FRESH equal union (`getUnionType` does not intern), from which the block suppresses **61** times per compiler-profile compile — so SKIPPING loses them while SUBSTITUTING the plain walk's result preserves both the identity relationship and the member set, which is everything the consumer reads. Equivalence measured with a shipped control: `--verifyUnionRetry` re-walks and HONOURS the re-walk (so the run reproduces the pre-change binary and IS the grid baseline) and compares at `Type`-INSTANCE, MEMBER-ID-SET and suppression-VERDICT granularity — **compiler 916 / services 1,191 / harness 1,313, 0 diffs of any kind across all 3,420**, with instance-diff 0 meaning the mirror returns the very same instance. `--verifyUnionRetryAll` is round 790's FREE positive control over the complement and reports **149 / 101 / 118** diffs, so no deliberately bogus flag was needed. **Law 2 checked to the unit**: `narrow.walks` 27,256 → 26,340 = −916 exactly, and nothing rose (`typeOfExpr.calls` −0.03%, `globals.lookups` −0.26%). **NO A/B was run, deliberately** — 0.11% is an order of magnitude below the warm protocol's ±1.0% band, so a wall verdict would be noise wearing a sign; the counters decide. **WHAT DID NOT WORK: the SKIP the item forbade is indistinguishable from the substitution on ALL EIGHT PROFILES *and* ALL 13,419 CORPUS BASELINES** — ablated in and run in full, 0 added / 0 removed and not one baseline moved, because the retry's fold strictly implies the elaboration's own `memberHasIt` and `missingMembers` is empty a few lines later. Ship the provable form anyway: it costs the same 2 ms, and "nothing we ran noticed" is not equivalence. Second negative: **a bare expression-STATEMENT property access is the only small-file shape whose plain walk is loop-free** — in an argument or a `return` an earlier pass has already walked the reference and the round-664 inter-walk memo serves it, so six candidate fixtures read `loopFree = 0` and would have made two verifier pins vacuous. Suite 13,412 → **13,419 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (+7 pins, **2 discriminating** against fault A; fault B is caught by nothing anywhere, reported rather than papered over). 8-profile grid **46/46/46/46/46/46/46/94, 0 added and 0 removed BOTH directions**; `--partitionCheck 2` **EQUIVALENT — 46**. Full derivation: `docs/perf/property-access-attribution.md` §§ 36-42._

_Round 798 (2026-08-02) — **(IANY.1): THE MAP WAS RE-DERIVED FIRST AND IT NAMED ITS OWN TARGET — `spineIanyEnterNode`, the ONE handler of round 732's big six that no attribution round had ever opened, carries 1,031 ms and 42% of it defines a contextual state NOTHING CAN READ.** Step 1 corrected `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 0 in place (median of 3 probe-free `--passTiming` runs, new 798 column): **flow narrowing 71,414 → 17,851 walks (−75%) for 2,290 → 1,158 ms (−49%)**, `getTypeOfExpression` calls −8.5%, `spineLeaveNode` −16.7%; FLAT: `spineEnterNode`, the ~400 tail passes (10.7% of the compile), the node count and the whole per-kind concentration (CALL_EXPRESSION 17.1% of the spine over 6.1% of nodes; the five statement anchors 38% over 10.0%; IDENTIFIER 9.3% over 44.5%). **The wall did not follow the counters** — 29.3 s vs ~26.5 s recorded at 758 — a statement about the box, which is why COST.1 exists. **Round 797's two leads are DEAD ON ARITHMETIC and are recorded rather than worked**: (A) `getTypeOfExpression` on PropertyAccess+Call arguments is 147 ms = 0.50% and is the cost of typing a composite argument, not waste; (B) the 8,574 arguments typed to `any` and discarded are 99 ms = 0.34% with a recoverable part of ZERO. **THE LEVER**: `spineIanyCtx` has no reader outside its own family and every reader sits INSIDE the subtree the state was defined for, so a state defined for a CHILDLESS child (forEachChild visits nothing for IDENTIFIER/STRING/NUMERIC literals) can never be read — and neither can a CALL's own `kind = 1` state when every argument is childless, its `typed` flag being consulted ONLY by the argument edges. The two gates are COUPLED; the call FRAME is still pushed (it shadows an enclosing state for the CALLEE subtree). **The sibling assignment arm has done exactly this since round 472 and never generalised it** — round 783's debt with a named creditor. **PRIZE, both arms twice on ONE binary with an IDENTICAL boundary count: skippable rows 473/478 → 45/47 ms, but 110 ms REAPPEARS in the surviving population (round 788's law), so the honest figure is the handler total 1,102/1,080 → 789/753 = Δ 320 ms (~1.1%) against a within-arm spread of 22/36 (8.9×).** Deterministic: `typeOfExpr.calls` **−7.63%**, distinct −5.30%, globals lookups/misses −4.4%; what ROSE is `typeNode.bypassed` +0.11% and `narrow.walks` by exactly 2 — the round-754/778 order effect, reported not smoothed; baseline rebased in the landing commit. **No wall A/B, deliberately** (1.1% IS the warm band). Grid vs `--ianyGateOff` — the pre-798 path in the same binary — **46/46/46/46/46/46/46/94, 0 added and 0 removed BOTH directions**; `--partitionCheck 2` **EQUIVALENT — 46**; suite 13,441 → **13,449 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (+8 `IanyGateTest` pins, **4 discriminating**; the second ablation — the scope-push exclusion removed — is caught by NOTHING and leaves the compiler profile byte-identical although its population is reached 11,032 times, so the exclusion is kept BY ARGUMENT rather than by evidence). **WHAT DID NOT WORK**: the IIFE pin's first form asserted a TS7006 that does NOT fire on a working binary (round 694's IIFE-argument contextual typing supplies the parameter type) — round 797's law recurring one round later; restated as an in-process equivalence over both settings of `IanySections.gateOff`, which is strictly stronger. Full derivation: `docs/perf/implicit-any-attribution.md`._

_Round 802 (2026-08-02) — **(SETUP.1) THE LAST UNNAMED REGION IN THE COMPILE IS ONE FUNCTION — AND THE ROUND'S SECOND RESULT IS BIGGER THAN ITS FIRST.** `outside-pass` (975 ms of checker-init inside no `pass()` wrapper, printed for ~300 rounds and never explained) is the ~15 setup statements at the top of `Checker.init`. Each is now `pass("init:<name>")`, so the partition is **exhaustive BY CONSTRUCTION** (round 801's bind shape — the residue is still printed and must stay ~0, so nothing needs calibrating). **The row falls 975 → 144 ms and `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` alone is 636 ms = 65% of the phase and 2.2% of the compile**, against 89 ms for `trackAllImportReferences`, 36 ms for `computeAllEnumValues`, and under 2 ms for eleven of the sixteen rows; it is also the ONLY setup pass doing type-system work (80 `getTypeOfExpression` calls, every other zero — pinned as a shape). **NO LEVER LANDED ON IT, DELIBERATELY**: `getTypeOfSymbol` MEMOISES, so a deferral MOVES the work (round 788's law, which cost round 801 its own lever), and the census is queued as (SETUP.2) with the falsifier written — if `distinct` does not fall faster than `calls`, the item closes. **THE SECOND RESULT: HotSpot's `DontCompileHugeMethods` is a PRODUCT flag defaulting to `true` with `HugeMethodLimit` = 8,000 bytecodes, and a static `javap` census (`scripts/huge_methods.py`, landed) finds 19 of 13,910 methods OVER it — so they are NEVER JIT-compiled and run interpreted for the whole process.** `checkMemberAccessMissingCore` **46,567** (5.8×), `checkArgumentsAgainstSignatureCore` 23,890, `checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore` 19,296, `checkAssignmentExpressionCore` 18,100, `checkSingleCallExpressionTypesCore` 15,567, `checkPropertyAccessInExpr` 9,062 — **and `forEachChild` 9,750**, the traversal primitive all ~400 tail passes run through. **Rounds 787–800 opened five of those one at a time and each concluded "no concentration"; a uniformly interpreted function is exactly that.** `-XX:-DontCompileHugeMethods`: 4 interleaved pairs, **A 25.448 s vs B 24.655 s = −3.1%, B wins 4/4**, all eight runs at 46 errors; arm A sd 0.81%, **arm B sd 1.46% — above the ~1% quietness criterion, so the SIGN is certain and the MAGNITUDE is ±~1.5%**, quoted as (JIT.1)'s upper bound and NOT as a banked win. _Round 814 (2026-08-03) — **(JIT.1)(d): THE `Checker` CONSTRUCTOR, 11,298 BYTECODES, IS AN ENTRY AT 5,538 PLUS TEN HELPERS (`initSetupPasses`, `initDeclarationOnlyPasses`, `initCheckPasses1..8`, 12-804); CENSUS 8 -> 7 AND THE `Checker` LIST IS DOWN TO ONE PLUS ONE ODD ONE.** Census re-measured at HEAD on a rebuilt binary first and reproduced exactly; the after-number measured the same way. **The first target in the arc whose frequency argument is DEGENERATE and is said to be**: a constructor runs exactly once per compile and every input pays all of it, so nothing here is cold and the only cut criterion is SIZE — the eight checking runs are contiguous ~67-code-line slices of an ordered sequence of ~437 `pass("name") { … }` dispatches. **The number that reframes the target: 5,538 of the entry is NOT the pass sequence but the class's 494 PROPERTY INITIALIZERS**, which a JVM constructor cannot delegate to a helper — so the whole dispatch sequence is worth only ~5,760 bytecodes (~13 each; the `pass` lambdas are separate methods) and the remaining 2,462 of headroom is consumed by ADDING FIELDS, not passes. The eleven sum to **11,332 against 11,298** — the split ADDED 34 bytecodes, an ELEVENTH confirmation that a bytecode count is a THRESHOLD predicate and not a cost model. **The prize is bounded to zero wall and no A/B was run**: the constructor runs once and its loops already live inside `pass` lambdas, i.e. in their own JIT-eligible methods; this lands for the threshold and for (f). Shape: 443 body-level statements, **zero** `return`/`break`/`continue` at body level, no body-level loops, exactly TWO body-level locals both kept inside one run — so **cross-boundary values NONE and every helper is parameterless**, and nine of ten regions move at dedent 0. Equivalence by round 805's five checks, all green (`init` reconstruction **IDENTICAL at 43 lines**; accounting 2,094 = 33 kept + 2,061 moved). **All 13 pins validated on the UNSPLIT binary first — 13 ran, 0 failed. DISCRIMINATION 3 OF 3, each mistake alone, pin count confirmed**: run 8 moved to the head fails **exactly 1** (its ORDER seam), run 1 hoisted out of the `declarationOnly` guard fails **2**, the run-5 call deleted fails **exactly 1** (its arm pin). **The transferable find: a wipe-and-repin corpus walker is an ORDER sensor for free** — `checkBuiltinIterator` retracts a TS2339 that `checkSpine` emitted, so it can only work while run 8 runs after run 1. **WHAT DID NOT WORK:** the first run-8 arm pin (`applyDomLibSuggestionRewrite`) is unreachable from a hand-written source — our checker emits NO diagnostic for a property read on a member-less user interface — and the seam pin beside it passed VACUOUSLY on an empty diagnostic list, which is what a positive control exists to catch; plus two Kotlin-daemon memory failures (`Not enough memory`, `GC overhead limit exceeded`), the second with the tell `PINS RAN 0`. Suite 13,671 -> **13,687 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**; 8-profile grid **46x7/94, 0 added and 0 removed BOTH directions** vs a purpose-built pre-split binary (447 `init*Passes` entries vs 0); `--partitionCheck 2` **EQUIVALENT — 46**; cost gate **all 20 counters +0.00%**; build warning-clean. `docs/perf/setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 18._

PLUS (e) FOR `Transformer.transform`, 8,934 -> AN ENTRY AT 2,989 PLUS SEVEN HELPERS; CENSUS 5 -> 4.**  (f): `python3 scripts/huge_methods.py --fail-over <census>` is a round-gate step beside `cost_gate.py`, and `HugeMethodLimitTest` runs the SAME whole-program census inside the suite — failing on a NEW offender AND on a STALE named entry, which is **the tightening rule made mechanical** (Part B triggered it). _Round 818 (2026-08-03) — **(JIT.1)(e): `Transformer.transformClassBody`, 16,233 BYTECODES AND THE SECOND LARGEST METHOD IN THE COMPILER, IS AN ENTRY AT 5,202 PLUS NINE `tcb*` HELPERS (666-1,716); CENSUS 4 -> 3.** First target in the arc whose obstacles are SCOPING rather than control flow, and both are worth knowing: a LOCAL DATA CLASS constructed by a moved region (un-nameable from a member function, so lifted to a private nested class — behaviour-free, it captures nothing and never escapes), and a LOCAL `fun` CALLED FROM BOTH SIDES OF A BOUNDARY (`buildStaticBlockIife`, which closes over the two alias temps the split decides) — it can neither move nor be duplicated, so it is passed as a FUNCTION-TYPED PARAMETER (`::buildStaticBlockIife`), leaving the moved call site textually untouched, and **the ORDER that makes that sound is enforced by nothing in the types**. A sibling local `fun` needed none of it because both its call sites sit inside ONE region: the test is where the call sites are, not what the construct is. **The parts sum to 16,018 against 16,233 — 215 FEWER — and this is the first target where BOTH bytecode-negative mechanisms fire at once**: boxed `var` reads inside the function 31 -> 11 (round 816's) AND local-slot addressing, 2-byte aload/astore 1,947 -> 1,850 / 1-byte 197 -> 254 (round 817's); the 11 boxed reads that remain are the entry's two alias temps, still captured by the local `fun`. Pins validated on the UNSPLIT binary first — 60 ran, exactly 5 failed and they are the 5 size/ratchet pins. Discrimination **4 of 4** with the count predicted before each run (order 1, list-identity 1, return-signal 1, dropped call 1) plus a NEGATIVE CONTROL at its predicted 0, every arm reporting RAN 10. WHAT COST TIME: the added prologue drew two successive warnings on a warning-clean build, and a per-method javap attribution keyed by `line[:70]` reported ZERO boxed reads in a method that has 31 — the method's name sits past character 70 of its signature. Suite 13,739 -> **13,752 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**; 8-profile grid **46x7/94, 0 added and 0 removed BOTH directions** vs a purpose-built pre-split binary; `--partitionCheck 2` **EQUIVALENT — 46**; cost gate **all 20 counters +0.00%**; `--rerun-tasks` build 0 `w:` / 0 `e:`.
