_Round 820 (2026-08-03) — **(JIT.1)(e): `Checker.<clinit>`, 10,339 BYTECODES, IS 3,156 PLUS SEVEN TOP-LEVEL `ckConst*` BUILDERS; CENSUS 2 -> 1.** The last shape in the arc that no contiguity argument settles: a STATIC INITIALIZER. Only **50 of the companion's 276 members cost it anything** — a Kotlin `private const val` of a primitive/`String` carries a `ConstantValue` attribute and executes NO bytecode, so ~200 dispatch tags are free and all 10,339 are `setOf`/`mapOf` literals; regions measured first with the new `scripts/clinit_split_analyze.py` (2,992 / 1,368 / 787 / 671 / 553 / 497 / 371 = 7,239 moved). The builders are **TOP-LEVEL** (`CheckerKt`, a plain `invokestatic`) because a companion `private fun` would have to be reached through the very static field `<clinit>` is installing — and the price of that is the one constraint on the split: **a `private` companion member is invisible to a top-level function in the same file**, so `LIB_MIN_TARGET` moved only its leading `mapOf(…)` and left the `+ TYPED_ARRAY_NAMES.flatMap { … }` tail behind (arm A6). **There is no frequency argument and that is the point** — a static initializer runs once, at class load, so nothing here is priceable by any A/B in this repo (`cost_gate.py`: all 20 counters +0.00%); it lands for the (JIT.1)(f) ratchet. **The parts sum to 84 FEWER and NEITHER known mechanism applies** — round 816's boxing needs a captured `var` and round 817's slot addressing needs LOCALS, of which a `<clinit>` of `putstatic`s has none; fourth round, fourth combination. The risk this shape carries instead is a **WRONG-SET SUBSTITUTION**: five builders return `Set<String>`, so a mixed-up call site type-checks — **5 of the 6 mistake arms compiled with 0 `e:`** — and silently changes which names the checker believes exist. Pins validated on the UNSPLIT binary first (**66 ran, exactly 5 failed and they are the 5 size/ratchet pins**); 66 / 0 on the split; discrimination 4 of 6 arms at the predicted count plus a NEGATIVE CONTROL at its predicted 0, with **both under-predictions sharing one cause** — the TS2749 pin's subject `parseInt` is a member of both substituted sets and `KNOWN_GLOBALS` is upstream of its consumer, so predict from the PIN SUBJECT's membership in the set substituted IN. **WHAT DID NOT WORK:** two arms first returned `RAN 0` with `OutOfMemoryError: GC overhead limit exceeded` — seven successive `Checker.kt` compiles in one script walk into BUILD.1's ceiling because an idle Kotlin daemon keeps its heap; a vacuous arm is not a result, and both were re-run with `--stop` + a bracket-pattern `pkill` inside the loop. Gate: suite **13,778 -> 13,791 / 0 / 3**; 8-profile grid BOTH directions **46/46/46/46/46/46/46/94, 0 added 0 removed** (class dirs confirmed to differ, 14 `ckConst` methods vs 0); `--partitionCheck 2` EQUIVALENT — 46; `--rerun-tasks` 0 `w:` / 0 `e:`; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 1` exits 0. **(JIT.1) is now at ONE**, and round 820 MEASURED it rather than attempting it: `tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` is 1,064 lines with FLAT bytecodes (largest 25-line window **449** of 11,930, 22% inlined stdlib charged to call sites) around one `for (tp in orderedTps)` loop mutating three accumulators across every boundary — a DATA-FLOW answer, not a contiguity one. `docs/perf/setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 25._

_Round 821 (2026-08-03) — **(JIT.1) IS COMPLETE: THE OVER-LIMIT CENSUS IS ZERO.** `Checker.tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs`, **11,930 bytecodes**, is an entry at **1,869** plus `tispGatherAnchorCandidates` 3,054 / `tispGatherCallbackCandidates` 5,388 / `tispCheckConstraint` 1,503; census **1 -> 0**, measured on rebuilt binaries at both ends. It is the only target in the 20-round arc that no contiguity argument settles — its bytecodes are FLAT (largest 25-line window **449** of 11,930, 22.2% of them INLINED stdlib bodies charged to their call sites) and ONE `for (tp in orderedTps)` loop holds 9,368 of them — so the seams came from a scripted DATA-FLOW analysis (`scripts/tisp_split_analyze.py`: per-region read/write sets, liveness, exit classification). Three facts made it exact: an append-only CONTAINER (`candidates`) crosses a call boundary for free as a parameter; the ONE rebind that outlives its region (`tpSawAnyArg`) is RETURNED, never fielded; and a `Boolean?` return leaves all **22** whole-function `return null`s meaning what they meant, so **869 lines move VERBATIM** with no hand-edited control flow. Equivalence by round 805's five checks plus two — the file RECONSTRUCTS from HEAD byte for byte (10,257,330 chars). Discrimination **6 arms for 6**, negative control at its predicted 0; **A4 was rescued rather than written off** — the any-arg fallback is invisible through an ARGUMENT position (a bare `Type.TypeParam` source relates to most targets) and visible through an ARITHMETIC one. Gate: suite **13,791 -> 13,803 / 0 / 3**, 8-profile grid BOTH directions **46x7/94 with 0 added and 0 removed**, `--partitionCheck 2` EQUIVALENT 46, `cost_gate.py` all 20 counters +0.00%, 0 `w:` / 0 `e:`, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` green. **THE ARC, 802-821: 19 methods split, 46,567 bytecodes at the largest; ONE measured wall gain (-3.93%, B wins 5/5, round 803's `forEachChild`) and everything else landed for the THRESHOLD — a method over 8,000 bytecodes is NEVER JIT-compiled, and no other gate in this repo can see that.** The ratchet stays at 0 to catch the next method that crosses._

_Round 822 (2026-08-04) — **THE BOX CHANGED (4 cores / 7.7 GB -> 8 cores / 15.6 GB, swap still ZERO), NATIVE BUILDS WERE RE-AUTHORIZED BY THE OWNER, AND THE KOTLIN/NATIVE GATE IS LIVE AGAIN AFTER 10 DAYS DARK — WITH A FOURTH DRIFT AXIS THAT IS THE ONLY ONE NOT SELF-ANNOUNCING.** Owner decisions first (`9d0694b9`): **(JIT.2) NO launcher flag** — the -3.1% that motivated `-XX:-DontCompileHugeMethods` was measured when 19 methods sat over HotSpot's limit and round 821 took that census to 0, so it now buys nothing and would only make C2 compile a 46,000-byte method; what IS approved is **(JIT.2a), a round MEASURING the JDK 25 AOT cache**. **(JIT.3) WON'T DO** — `HugeMethodLimitTest` already runs the identical whole-program census inside `jvmTest`, so `check` is covered transitively. **THE HEAP FLOOR IS 4g, FOUND BY PROBING DOWN RATHER THAN INHERITING ROUND 772's 6g** — K/N compiles inside the GRADLE daemon, so the binding setting is `org.gradle.jvmargs`, which (BUILD.1) had cut to **1g** for the old box: **that 1g, not the hardware, was starving native, and what dissolved round 772's link blocker is heap going DOWN, not up** (4g is observed-to-succeed, NOT proven minimal — 3g was never probed; and the real margin is outside the heap, the daemon reaching **8.5 GB RSS against a 4g cap** because konan/LLVM allocates there). **DRIFT WAS 17, NOT THE ~169 OF ROUND 682**: axis 1 **17 violations in 7 files, every one the SAME character — a comma** (zero `(`, `)`, `&`, `@`); axes 2 (`kotlin.assert`) and 3 (JVM-only stdlib) **both 0**, structurally closed because agents copy neighbouring idiom, while `,` survives as the one illegal character occurring naturally in English prose and CLAUDE.md's advice named *parentheses*. **THE FIND — A FOURTH AXIS THAT INVERTS THE GATE'S RISK PROFILE:** the three documented axes are COMPILE-TIME and each announces itself as a build error; the fourth compiles clean and **KILLS THE TEST PROCESS**. A `commonTest` driving multi-thousand-frame recursion overflows an uncatchable native stack — `runWithDeepStack` is a pass-through there and `StackOverflowError` is a never-thrown stub, so the `init` boundary guard that converts an overflow into TS2589 (the very invariant those tests pin) is INERT — and **every alphabetically-later class then silently never runs: the first full run lost 4,414 of 13,689 tests to ONE test**. Three pins moved to `src/jvmTest`, nothing weakened. **DEPTH IS NOT THE PREDICATE** — 30,000-term chains, a 3,000-statement flow chain, a 60k-entry `IntKeyMapTest` and the corpus's 6,452-term `binderBinaryExpressionStress` all PASS natively; what crashes is what RECURSES. **This is also a PRODUCTION defect on native, queued as the P0 (NATIVE.1)** — a native `runWithDeepStack` actual (large-stack pthread), which must carry INV.6(6c0)'s thread-local id write-back or round 607's 51 silent corpus failures return. **MEASURED:** `compileKotlinLinuxX64` 2m06s, `compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` 1m39s, **`linuxX64Test` 25m33s then 21m01s at 13,689 / 0 / 3**, peak system 12.5 GB, floor available 3.14 GB. **JVM gate 13,803 / 0 / 3, exactly baseline.** Commits `52c94887` / `fdc0c84c` / `6e448818` / `a2ed6e41`._

_Round 828 (2026-08-04) — **(JIT.2a) THE JDK 25 AOT CACHE IS WORTH 1.638x ON THE COMPILER PROFILE WITH BYTE-IDENTICAL DIAGNOSTICS — AND IT IS *NOT* A START-UP LEVER, WHICH IS WHY THE ROUND NEEDED A THIRD ARM.** 6-rep rotated 3-arm interleave, 18 cold runs, box idle, **every run at 46 errors**: plain jar **22,223 ms** self (sd 287, spread 3.2%) vs AOT cache trained on the compiler profile **13,565 ms** (sd 310, spread 7.3%) = **-8,658 ms, -38.96%, 1.638x, 6/6 wins**, per-pair delta spread **924 ms** against an **8,388 ms** median delta — not noise by any margin this repo uses. On an unrelated 1-file project the SAME cache reads 1,324 -> 560 ms = **2.36x, 5/5, 53 ms spread**, so the cache is a property of the COMPILER, not of the trained project; only the training project's SIZE matters. **THE FRAMING WAS WRONG AND THE CONTROL ARM IS WHAT SAYS SO:** `wall - self` moves 143 -> 115 ms, i.e. the cache buys **~28 ms of start-up and ~8,630 ms INSIDE the compile**; a cache trained on a 1-FILE project — which carries the whole class-loading/linking half — delivers only **-5.92%** (6/6 but magnitude soft: 1,260 ms spread vs a 1,303 ms median delta) against the real-trained arm's -38.96%. **The prize is JEP 515's recorded METHOD PROFILES handed to C2 at start-up, not JEP 483 class loading.** **CORRECTNESS (gate A, run before any timing was quoted): sorted `--listAll` diff EMPTY** — 54 lines each, one md5, both `FAILED — 46 error(s)`, neither carrying round 811's `more error(s)` tell. **WHAT DID NOT WORK, AND IT IS THE HEADLINE: the cache CANNOT BE DUMPED from this repo's normal classpath** (`Cannot have non-empty directory in paths` — CDS needs a JAR-only classpath at dump time), so **`ab-interleaved.sh`, `ab-warm.sh` and `bench-compile-tsc.sh` are all structurally unable to carry an AOT arm**; every arm here ran from the jar. **THE SHIPPING RISK, MEASURED: the JVM NEVER invalidates a stale cache** — with `Checker.class` REMOVED from the jar and a fresh mtime, the plain run gives `NoClassDefFoundError` and the cached run gives **exit 0, `OK — 0 errors`** (921/926 app classes served from cache under every mismatch, `-XX:AOTMode=on` included). Training: **28.06 s / 48.8 MiB** (one ordinary compile + ~2.9 s assembly). **RECOMMENDATION: ship, gated on ONE condition — invalidation is ours to build; queued as (JIT.2b) BLOCKED-PENDING-USER.** Residue stated, not implied: emit mode, the other 7 profiles, the corpus suite under a cache, cross-JDK/cross-machine portability, `--workers 4` training. No `src/` change — `jvmTest`/`cost_gate.py`/`huge_methods.py` not applicable; 13,812 / 0 / 3 untouched. `docs/perf/aot-cache-round828.md`; `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md` § 0 is now a FIVE-point artifact index._

_Round 834 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER DIRECTION: CONFORMANCE BREADTH. THE `object` KEYWORD GETS A REAL RELATION RULE: the one rule the whole compiler held about `object` was tsc's WRITTEN AS ITS NEGATION, which is a different predicate, and correcting it plus the three gates that hid the corrected verdict turns **two more `nonPrimitive` cases GREEN (9 -> 7)**. **THE CATEGORY STILL CANNOT BE ADOPTED, SO IT WAS NOT** — and the honest re-costing below says it is not a one-round category either. Suite **13,847 / 0 / 3** (13,833 + 14 pins, 0 regressions); **all 8 profiles byte-identical against a REBUILT before-arm binary**._

_**THE PER-SUBTEST CENSUS, RE-DERIVED RATHER THAN INHERITED** (round 833's own lesson): the category is 16 files, 1 whole-file-skipped by tsgo, **25 subtests, 9 failing, 0 emit failures** — round 831's counts exactly. But the per-CASE diffs, which no prior round captured, show the 9 are **six unrelated gaps wearing an `object` costume**, and only three of them are about `object` at all. **THE DEFECT, and it reaches further than the keyword:** `isSimpleTypeRelatedTo`'s NonPrimitive leg read `!sf.hasAny(TypeFlags.Primitive or Null or Undefined or Void)` where tsc's rule is the POSITIVE `s & TypeFlags.Object && t & TypeFlags.NonPrimitive`. Those differ because **`TypeFlags.Primitive` omits every LITERAL bit** — so a `Type.StringLiteral`/`NumberLiteral`/boolean-literal source, a bare `Type.TypeParam`, `unknown`, and the instantiable `keyof`/indexed-access types ALL satisfied `object` silently. The furthest consequence was a **false positive one type away from any `object`**: `T[P] extends V | object ? 1 : 0` answered TRUE for a numeric-literal `T[P]`, so a mapped type computed `1 | 1` where tsc computes `1 | 0`. **WHAT LANDED — four changes, every one keyed on the target's `NonPrimitive` flag:** (1) the relation leg inverted to tsc's positive form; (2) a `structuredTypeRelatedTo` arm deciding a `Type.TypeParam` source by its CONSTRAINT (unconstrained `T` rejects, `T extends object` and `T extends {}` accept); (3) `canUseTypeEngine` opened for a `Type.TypeParam` source against a NonPrimitive target (the B60.8 rule below it admits only a CONSTRAINED one — precisely the case that is not an error); (4) a new `isArgCheckableType` = `isSimpleCheckableType` + NonPrimitive, used at **exactly two** argument-position gates. **`nonPrimitiveInFunction` and `nonPrimitiveInGeneric` are GREEN.** **WHAT DID NOT WORK, all three found by measurement and reverted or narrowed:** (a) widening `isSimpleCheckableType` ITSELF — it has 45 consumers, several of them dedicated walkers whose entire FP firewall it is, and B498's `f<object>(123)` walker immediately co-emitted a DUPLICATE TS2345 at one position; hence the separate predicate and its two named call sites, with a pin (`reported exactly once`) that only an explicit type argument can discriminate. (b) opening `canUseTypeEngine` for a UNION source too: it correctly rejects `object | null -> object`, but the same gate then FPs for a union the flow HAS narrowed and we have not (`var e: object | null; e = a; a = e` is legal in tsc) — it bought 3 diagnostics in a case that stays red for 12 other reasons, so it was dropped and the gap is recorded, not hidden. (c) the **one regression the full suite found** was the co-emission CLAUDE.md predicts: `checkSymbolAsWeakTypeArg`'s KDoc claimed "purely ADDITIVE" because the general check skipped `object` params — no longer true, so 8 of `dissallowSymbolAsWeakType`'s 12 diagnostics doubled. Fixed by the prescribed retraction keyed on (code, fileName, start, MESSAGE), with the stale KDoc corrected; the walker keeps its two uniquely-owned TS2769 chains._

_**THE RE-COSTING, which is the round's deliverable as much as the code: `nonPrimitive` needs SIX more sub-steps and two of them are not `object` work at all.** The 7 residual cases: **`nonPrimitiveConstraintOfIndexAccessType`** wants 10x TS2322 `string -> T[P]` — pure INDEXED-ACCESS assignability, we emit nothing, and `object` appears in 1 of its 10 constraints; **`nonPrimitiveAndTypeVariables`** (1 diff) is blocked by the GENERAL `TypeParam -> TypeParam` lenience (`srcConstraint == null || tgtConstraint == null -> return true`) plus a union DISPLAY-ORDER divergence (tsc sorts by type id and prints `object | U`; we preserve source order); **`nonPrimitiveNarrow`** (3) and **`nonPrimitiveStrictNull`** (12) want flow narrowing on `object` — `typeof x === 'number'` to `never`, and 8 TS18047/18048/18049 nullish-access diagnostics; **`nonPrimitiveAccessProperty`** (2) wants TS2339 on an `object` receiver (through the repo's most FP-sensitive walker); **`nonPrimitiveUnionIntersection`** (4) wants `getIntersectionType` to REDUCE (`object & string` -> `never`, `object & {}` -> `object`) plus excess-property checking through such an intersection — bounded but it changes intersection identity and display; **`nonPrimitiveAssignError`** (1) is the only cheap one left, a message shape (TS2741 with the source displayed as its apparent `{}` + TS2728, where we print TS2322). **So, like round 833's `typeSatisfaction`, the 0-emit-failure count overstated tractability — and for the same reason: a category's name describes its FIXTURES, not its gaps.**_

_**GATES.** Suite **13,847 / 0 / 3** from a wiped results dir, `xml.etree` (13,833 + 14 `NonPrimitiveObjectTypeTest` pins, **0 regressions**). `cost_gate.py` **PASS — every counter +0.00%**, `output.errors` 46 unchanged, so no rebaseline was needed or taken. `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, **0 over the limit** (largest 6,353). **THE 8-PROFILE GRID WAS RUN AGAINST A GENUINELY REBUILT BEFORE-ARM**, not against recorded counts: `Checker.kt` backed up, reverted, rebuilt, all 8 captured, restored, rebuilt. Every capture non-empty with no `more error(s)` truncation tell; counts 46x7 + harness 94; **`added=0 removed=0` and the sorted-capture md5 IDENTICAL on all eight**, the compiler profile at `4caacf248ce417899c2972c16a82f1ed` — the digest rounds 828, 832 and 833 recorded. A count match is not a diff (round 811), which is why the before-arm was rebuilt rather than quoted._

_Round 840 (2026-08-06) — **(AOT.4)(a): THE SHIPPED LAUNCHER COULD NOT REACH THE WARM SERVER IT SHIPS.** `scripts/xtsc` and `scripts/xtsc-aot` both ran `com.xemantic.typescript.compiler.MainKt`, while the mode dispatcher is `com.xemantic.typescript.compiler.server.XtscMainKt` — so `--serve` and `--daemon` were **unreachable through the command users are told to run**, and round 839's warm request (~7.3 s against ~13.6 s for a cached one-shot) was measured by invoking `java` by hand. Round 839 recorded the gap in (JIT.2b)'s residue; this round closes it. The dispatcher's `main` is a strict SUPERSET of the plain one (`--serve`, `--daemon`, else `com.xemantic.typescript.compiler.main(args)` verbatim), so a one-shot run gains two class loads and nothing else. **BOTH scripts had to swap together, and that is what the new pins exist to catch:** the main class is the `mainclass` field of the AOT fingerprint block, so a HALF-swap trains a cache under a fingerprint the launcher can never look up and every launch then reads `SKIP manifest-mismatch` — **silently, forever, with the lost 1.638× as the only symptom**. The fingerprint move is a deliberate, designed invalidation and is fail-safe: round 839's shipped cache now reads `SKIP no-cache-file`, **exit 0**, plus the retrain hint; `XTSC_AOT_LAUNCHER_VERSION` was deliberately NOT bumped, because `mainclass` is already its own field and that constant is reserved for command-line changes no other field records. **VERIFIED END TO END THROUGH THE LAUNCHER** (2-file scratch project): `--serve` logged `request 1 served in 1541 ms` / `request 2 served in 218 ms`; two `--daemon` runs produced output **diff-identical** to the one-shot `--noEmit --listAll` at 1,523 ms cold / 217 ms warm and 0.25 s client CPU; and the AOT path still works — a fresh `train` produced a 37,457,920-byte self-verified cache, `status` → `USE …`, a cached run diff-identical to plain at **487 ms vs 1,553 ms**, with `-Xlog:class+load=info` showing **795 of 795** `com.xemantic` classes from `source: shared objects file`, `XtscMainKt` and `CompileServer` among them (served, not merely opened). **PINS: +2 in `AotCacheGuardTest` (now 10), ablated ONE MISTAKE AT A TIME** over a copied `scripts/` dir via `XTSC_TEST_LAUNCHER`, never the tree itself: half-swap → **9 of 10** fail; both back on `MainKt` (round 839's actual state) → **exactly 1**, `the launcher reaches the server dispatcher`; a typo'd class in both → the same 1. **HONEST ABOUT COVERAGE:** `the launcher and the trainer name the same main class` is **not** uniquely discriminating (the fixture builds its manifest with `xtsc-aot` and decides with `xtsc`, so any disagreement already breaks its `USE` precondition) — it is the pin that *names* the two differing classes, said in its KDoc rather than claimed. Side effect recorded: `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh`'s both-arms failure baseline moves **10 → 12** (the new pins read the classpath LAYOUT, like the existing 8). An aside: the first socket attempt failed because a 117-byte scratchpad path exceeds `sockaddr_un`'s ~100 — the dispatcher refusing it BY NAME was itself the first proof the dispatcher was reached. **GATES: suite 13,900 → 13,902 / 0 / 3** from a wiped results dir (`xml.etree`), the +2 exactly the new pins, 0 regressions; `cost_gate.py` and `huge_methods.py` **not run and not required** — shell scripts, `src/jvmTest` and docs only, no `src/commonMain` or `src/jvmMain` change. **NOT VERIFIED, named rather than implied:** `--serve`/`--daemon` UNDER a cache; training with `--serve` in the workload; whether the two extra class loads shift any timing; and the shipped `~/.cache` cache was NOT retrained on the 78-file compiler profile (28 s + ~51 MB). **A NEW OWNER-GATED FINDING:** `build.gradle.kts` sets `nativeImageMainClass = …MainKt` while `XtscMain`'s KDoc says the native image is meant to *be* the thin client — a SECOND instance of exactly this gap, filed as (AOT.4)(b), put to the owner, and **approved and landed the same round** (below). Commit `454bcd12`; `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 8._

_**Round 840(b) (2026-08-06) — (AOT.4)(b), OWNER-APPROVED: THE SAME GAP IN THE GRAALVM NATIVE IMAGE, AND ON THE SHIPPED BINARY IT WAS NOT AN UNREACHABLE MODE BUT A SILENT WRONG SUCCESS THAT WROTE FILES.**  The owner lifted the Guardrails block for this one `build.gradle.kts` property; every other build-system change stays gated, and the approval reached the implementing agent RELAYED through the orchestrating session rather than witnessed directly — recorded because Guardrails turn on exactly that distinction, and the change is one revertible string. **The gap was measured on the artifact, not inferred from the source:** the stale 2026-07-30 `build/native/xtsc` (built from `MainKt`) run as `--serve --socket /tmp/r840b.sock` bound **no socket**, took the socket path as the PROJECT, and **emitted 173 output files at exit 0**; `--daemon` identically. So the prediction ("the mode is unreachable") was too kind — it silently does something else and writes to disk. **The one GraalVM risk settleable without a build was settled:** `javap -c CompileServer` shows all four kotlinx-serialization resolutions are the compiler-plugin intrinsic (`invokevirtual …$Companion.serializer()`), no reflective `serializer(KType)`, and serialization was already reachable from the old entry point via `TsConfigLoader` — so the swap adds no new reflection. **NOTHING NATIVE WAS BUILT OR RUN, and that is the live part of this entry:** GraalVM is not installed on this box, so `./gradlew nativeImage` has never run against the dispatcher (**the image may not even build**), `--serve` has never run on a native image at all, `UnixDomainSocketAddress`/`ServerSocketChannel(UNIX)` under closed-world analysis is unknown and **no metadata was invented for it**, image size/build time move by an unmeasured amount and direction, and the **13,350 ms** native figure quoted throughout the docs is still a `MainKt` measurement. The pin asserts the build script and `scripts/xtsc` name the SAME class — composing with (a)'s executing pin, which is what proves that class actually dispatches — and its KDoc says plainly it cannot see whether the image builds or serves; ablation: reverting the constant fails that pin **and only it** (1 of 11), while editing `compileTsProject`'s unrelated `mainClass` fails none, checked via the XMLs rather than `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` because a build-script edit can leave `jvmTest` UP-TO-DATE and make a green result vacuous. `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh` both-arms baseline 12 → 13. **GATES: suite 13,902 → 13,903 / 0 / 3** (638 XMLs, `xml.etree`), the +1 exactly the pin, 0 regressions; `cost_gate.py`/`huge_methods.py` not run and not required — no `src/commonMain` or `src/jvmMain` change, and test generation is untouched. **Process lesson, now a CLAUDE.md entry:** piping a gradle run through `| tail`/`| grep` buffers until the pipeline EXITS, so a run that dies mid-flight leaves a **zero-byte log** — no `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, no `tests completed`, nothing to tell a killed run from one that never started, which defeats the round-831 rule to read the log before believing an empty results dir. This file's own "How to run tests" incantation carries that hazard. Commit `e7d933d2`._

_Round 839 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER REDIRECT: LEAVE M3.0 CONFORMANCE, RETURN TO M5 / PERFORMANCE. (JIT.2b)'s RESIDUE IS CLOSED — THE SHIPPED AOT CACHE IS NOW VALIDATED IN EVERY MODE WE SHIP, AND THERE IS NO CACHED-vs-UNCACHED DIVERGENCE ANYWHERE.** Rounds 828/832 measured and shipped the JDK 25 AOT cache on ONE mode of ONE profile (`--noEmit`, compiler profile) and recorded four things as explicitly unvalidated. All four ran this round, identical command shape in both arms (`docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 7). **33 whole-project compiles, 8 server requests, 4 emitted output trees and 2 full corpus-suite runs agreed — every `--noEmit --listAll` capture, cached and uncached, sequential and `--workers 2/4/8`, one-shot and server, shares ONE sorted-diagnostics md5 (`59d930db…`, 46 errors).** **(a) EMIT** — the one that mattered, because `--noEmit` never runs the Transformer or Emitter and `xtsc-aot train` trains with `--noEmit`: `dist/` wiped and re-hashed per run, **78 output files sha256-identical file-for-file across all four runs**, diagnostics diff empty, **1.49×** (26,109 → 17,448 ms) against **1.66×** check-only on the same binary — and the emit TAIL costs MORE under the cache (3,134 vs 2,310 ms), because a cached run reaches the emitter after 14 s of warm-up instead of 24 s and the cache carries no emitter profile; that prices "train with emit" at ~800 ms, ~5% of a cached emit run. **(b) THE CORPUS SUITE THROUGH A CACHED JVM** — Gradle's test worker cannot carry an AOT arm (the cache binds to a classpath whose dump-time form must be a PREFIX of the runtime one, and Gradle builds the worker's), so `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh`, landed this round, drives it in one plain `java` process over the trained jar + its 7 deps in that exact order: **638 classes, 13,897 run + 3 ignored = the gate's 13,900, and the per-class result files diff EMPTY between the arms, failure lines included**. The cache was proven IN USE and not merely opened — **922 `com.xemantic` classes loaded with `source: shared objects file`**, round 828's 921/926 population. The 10 failures present identically in BOTH arms are jar-vs-exploded-dir harness artifacts (`AotCacheGuardTest` ×8 "URI is not hierarchical", `HugeMethodLimitTest` ×2 "main classes are not a directory"), both green under Gradle; no corpus baseline failed on either arm. **(c) `--workers`** with the DISTRIBUTION the standing rule demands (rounds 754/824 each shipped a false green from one draw): 20 cached runs (5 each at seq/w2/w4/w8) + 9 uncached, 46 errors and one md5 in all 29. Cached medians seq 14,514 / w2 12,779 / **w4 12,343** / w8 16,368 ms; uncached seq 22,975 / w4 18,966 / w8 20,342 — blocked, not interleaved, so directional only, but **the two levers OVERLAP** (cached-w4 vs plain-seq is **1.86×**, not the 2.26× the headline ratios would multiply to) and **`--workers 8` under a cache is worse than cached SEQUENTIAL**, a reversal of the uncached order. **(d) `--serve`** — the cache halves the FIRST request (23,536 → 13,987 ms) and is **worth nothing warm** (7,268 vs 7,310 ms): both remove interpreter warm-up, so they are the same lever seen twice. Gap found on the way: **`scripts/xtsc` cannot reach `--serve` at all** (its main class is `MainKt`; the dispatcher is `server.XtscMainKt`). **WHAT DID NOT WORK:** driving the suite through Gradle — `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` reaches the worker only via the daemon's environment and the worker's classpath is Gradle's, not the trained one, so the cache would have been silently unused and the run would have looked exactly like a green one (hence the class-load count above); and a standalone suite run without `kotlin-power-assert-runtime-jvm`, which read **4,998 failed in BOTH arms** — a same-in-both-arms result is not automatically a valid one. **NOT REACHED, named rather than implied:** a cache trained under emit or under `--workers 4`; `--watch`/`--incremental` under a cache; the other seven dashboard profiles; and the launcher's `--serve` gap. **GATES: no `src/` change this round** (docs + two new scripts), so the suite / `cost_gate.py` / `huge_methods.py` gates were not required — though the corpus suite was in fact run twice, standalone, with zero non-artifact failures on either arm._

_**Round 843 (2026-08-07) — (WARM.1)(a): THE FIRST WARM-JVM ATTRIBUTION THIS REPO HAS EVER TAKEN, AND IT CORRECTS THE ARC'S HEADLINE ARTIFACT NUMBER BY 40%.** Owner directive: prioritize profiling-based perf on the WARMED-UP JVM, now that a client-server architecture exists. **The premise was verified, not assumed: every per-pass table in `docs/perf` was produced by a COLD one-shot `MainKt` JVM** — four independent strands (each table's own "median of 3 probe-free runs" line, the literal command at `setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 644, `cost_gate.py`'s recipe, and `BenchMain`'s inability to accept a flag) — so the cost map every queue decision rests on describes an artifact we no longer ship. **THE LADDER, re-measured today on one binary and one profile:** probe-free cold **22,971 ms** (n=3); warm `BenchMain` median **6,917 / 7,143 ms**; the real `--serve` ladder **22,753 → 10,898 → 7,754 → 7,606 → 7,447 → 7,410 → 7,447 → 7,100 ms** — two INDEPENDENT warm harnesses agreeing at ~7.0-7.4 s against a record of **11,580 ms** (`CompileServer.kt`'s own KDoc, `aot-native-image.md` § 1/§ 4, § 0.1's endgame framing). **Over the same interval cold improved 12.6% and warm improved 39.5%.** Leading HYPOTHESIS, labelled as one and NOT confirmed this round: the **(JIT.1)** huge-method arc — a method over `HugeMethodLimit` is never JIT-compiled, so removing one pays in STEADY STATE, and rounds 802-821 were measured only with the COLD instrument, where round 803's own falsifier read `NOISE-DOMINATED`. Queued as **(WARM.1)(b)**: a warm A/B of a pre-802 binary vs HEAD would settle it, and if confirmed that arc bought ~3× what it was credited with. **THE INSTRUMENT PRICES ITSELF — the central methodological result:** `--passTiming` costs **~2,840 ms cold (+12.4%)** and **3,450-3,945 ms warm (~+50%)**, so every absolute ms in every `docs/perf` table is probe-inflated — harmlessly cold, dominatingly warm — and **a cold-table share is NOT comparable to a warm-table share; only the RATIOS are.** One deduction needs no assumption: the warm instrumented front end is 907.9 ms in total and cannot hold a 3,945 ms probe, so **>=77% of the probe is inside checker-init**; where inside is unmeasured, and the doc brackets `checkSpine`'s warm share two ways (72% vs 83%) and refuses to pick — queued as (WARM.1)(c), the blocker on sizing any warm lever. **FINDINGS THAT SURVIVE (ratios, both arms inflated):** the front end warms **~4.0×** against the checker's **~2.2×**, so the checker's share RISES warm (86.2% → 91.8%), opposite to what § 0.1's cold budget suggests; the narrowing tail FLATTENS — `>=1ms` walks 134 → 37, their share of narrowing ~47-50% → ~32-34% over IDENTICAL 17,851 walks and 583,779 visits (the buckets are time-keyed), so round 735's "extreme tail" replicates exactly as a **COLD** property and is scope-qualified, not retracted; and per-kind warm-up is **not ordered by cost** (`FUNCTION_DECLARATION` at 64.9 µs warms 2.86×, `BINARY_EXPRESSION` at 30.8 µs warms 2.08×) — what IS sign-consistent is IDENTIFIER's top-12 share falling 9.1% → ~7.1% while the five statement anchors rise 48.4% → ~50.4%. **A REAL DEFECT THE CLIENT-SERVER ARCHITECTURE EXPOSED, found while building the instrument:** `Main.kt` enabled `PassTiming` and never disabled it, and `CompileServer.compileCapturing` calls that same `main()` per request in one long-lived JVM — so **one `--daemon --passTiming` request permanently instrumented every later request**, and worse, `--globalsAmp N` leaked a mode making every globals lookup re-read its key N times forever while `--verifyMappedCache` leaked one recomputing every served cache hit. Fixed by mirroring what `SpineDispatch`/`SpineSections` already do. **The same loop still leaks `ParallelCheckMode.workers`, `FlowScan.legacy`, `ArgNarrowGate.mode` and three more that CHANGE COMPILER BEHAVIOUR — a correctness hazard under `--serve`, deliberately not fixed blind, queued as (SERVE.1).** New instrument: `BenchMain` takes an opt-in 4th argument running ONE instrumented rebuild strictly AFTER the measured loop (so `medianMs` stays probe-free and the probe's cost is the difference), aborting non-zero if its files/errors disagree. **GATES: suite 13,909 → 13,911 / 0 / 3** from a wiped results dir (`xml.etree`), the **+2 exactly the two new pins**, 0 regressions; `cost_gate.py` **every counter +0.00%**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0 — both required and run, `commonMain` changed. **NOT measured, named rather than implied:** no warm A/B was run and nothing was optimized this round; the probe's landing site inside checker-init; one profile only; and **no tsc number** — node is not installed on this box, so every parity statement in the new doc is DERIVED from a CI ratio and labelled as such. `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md`._

_**Round 858 (2026-08-08) — THE THIRD "HARNESS LOADS THE WRONG ARTIFACT" AUDIT IN SIX ROUNDS, AND THE FIRST WHERE THE MEASUREMENTS SURVIVE INTACT: THE WARM ARC STANDS, `build/bench/cp.txt` NOW HAS ZERO READERS, AND BATCH 2 KILLED THIS ROUND'S OWN HEADLINE NUMBER.** Round 857's parting observation — `cp.txt` is the pre-split, pre-bump dependency list — audited per reader. **`ab-interleaved.sh` and `cost_gate.py` SOUND** (both resolve FRESH through the init script; neither ever read it). **`ab-warm.sh` SOUND TODAY, GUARD INCOMPLETE**: its `cp-warm.txt` content is CURRENT (2.4.10 / 0.9.1 / 1.11.0) and its guard currently fails anyway, but it compared the cache only against the **module's** `build.gradle.kts` while the versions live in `gradle/libs.versions.toml` — **blind to exactly the change that produces a stale tail**. **`aot-draw-variance.sh` BROKEN**: `<core jar>:$(cat cp.txt)` plus a main class (`XtscMainKt`) that lives in the DAEMON module and is absent from it — verified `ClassNotFoundException`, dead since the split like `scripts/xtsc` was until round 857, but **failing LOUDLY, so it could never produce a wrong number**. **The documented "pre-split refusal" never guarded `cp.txt`** — it greps the INIT SCRIPT, and in `ab-warm.sh` sits in the resolve branch, so a cache hit skips it. **The one thing that touched published numbers:** `round847`/`round849`'s harnesses ran WARM arms on the current tail and COLD arms on the stale one, so their cold/warm RATIOS compared two tails. **THE DECISIVE EXPERIMENT — behaviour IDENTICAL:** same binary/profile, only the tail swapped, rotated interleave; all 12 timed compiles `46 errors` and **all four `--listAll` digests `59d930db…`** (round-841 lineage), and both tails link. **AND THE TIMING CLAIM DIED IN BATCH 2, which is the round's best result:** batch 1 read stale 23,633 → fresh 24,420 ms, **+3.63%, 3/3, both arm sds <1%** — one batch from being written up as real and used to "correct" two rounds; batch 2 read **+0.33%, 2/3** with deltas +79/−184/+317, and the **fresh arm itself moved −2.7% between batches on a byte-identical configuration**, so batch 1's drift simply landed on one arm. **Round 840(c)'s rule, second confirmation this session and the first where obeying it REVERSED the conclusion.** **So, plainly: the warm arc STANDS and nothing needs re-taking** — 845/846/848/850/851 never touched the stale tail, 847/849's cold arms did but the difference is behaviourally nil and not a demonstrated timing effect, and **round 845's (JIT.1) −33.6%, 4/4, 1.505× is untouched by construction** (both arms built in a throwaway worktree from pre-split commits). **THE GUARD:** `scripts/lib/dep-classpath.sh`, one shared resolver refusing a cache unless non-empty, newer than EVERY build-definition input (`libs.versions.toml` first) and naming only entries that exist; all readers rewired, `aot-draw-variance.sh` additionally given round 857's staged-lib-dir treatment. **ABLATION: three arms, each failing EXACTLY ONE uniquely-its-own pin** — and dropping `libs.versions.toml` reddens only the libs-versions pin **while the module-build-file pin stays GREEN**, which is precisely the state `ab-warm.sh` was in. **GATES: suite 14,036 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (14,030 + 6 new pins). `docs/perf/classpath-readers-audit.md`._

_**Round 870 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.17): THE WARM LEAF PROFILE RE-TAKEN, AND ITS OWN VALIDITY CHECK PASSED — THE TWO FAMILIES ROUNDS 868/869 FIXED ARE GONE FROM ITS TABLE (**barrel search 471 -> 61 ms**, **annotation frames 153 -> 40 ms**), NOTHING NEW ROSE INTO THEIR PLACE, AND THE TOP SURVIVING CANDIDATE IS TAKEN.**_ Same recipe, two processes, and — what makes the tables comparable at all — round 868's own dumps RE-AGGREGATED with the same script, now committed as `scripts/leaf_owner_profile.py` (it REFUSES a `jfr print` dump truncated to 5 frames, filters to the compile thread, charges stdlib leaves to the nearest non-stdlib OWNER). `checkSpine` INCLUSIVE reads **75.28%/74.34%** against this arc's known ~74%. The annotation-frame delta of **113.3 ms** is a genuine cross-instrument agreement with round 869's independent amplifier (**129.7 ms**), 13% apart. **THE DENOMINATOR TRAP, NAMED: the JFR window is a fixed 90 s of steady state, so a share is a share of WALL TIME, not of a rebuild — an unchanged per-rebuild cost READS HIGHER by 1.099 once the compile got faster**, which is why every cross-round row is quoted in ms/rebuild and why the rows that 'moved up' (`ctaFnBodyFrame`, `ctaSpineEnter`, `lookupPerFileForNode`) did not move at all. Shape unchanged: HashMap/HashSet **26.6/27.3%**, `java.util.regex` **0.04/0.13%**. **THE CANDIDATE:** `computeTypeParamInfo` (1.47/1.35%), flagged as unpriced by round 868; `ciaMutualFnDecls$resolve` is bigger in one process and DISCARDED for not replicating (1.40% vs 0.38% on the same binary). **PRICED INDEPENDENTLY** by a counter+span census (`FrontEnd.TPI`), deterministic to the unit over two processes: **82 ms = 1.16% of a warm rebuild** — and **the memo is not the problem, it answers 96.2% of the 28,663 questions**. The cost is one of the miss's three lookups: **2,992,718 symbols iterated to reach 2,184 module-flagged ones — 99.93% of the scan re-decides a question that is a property of the binder tables and not of the name asked**. **THE FIX** (`buildModuleSymbolScanIndex`, commonMain, `internal`, own file) hoists the module-symbol list out of the miss, preserving file order, within-file order and DUPLICATES (one `Symbol` really is in two files' `locals` — `mergeModuleAugmentations` creates that); the `exports` probe stays LIVE because it is a `var` the checker writes to. **Row CONTROLLED (round 793): calls / misses / file probes / module probes / answered / null are IDENTICAL to the unit and only the scan width moves — 2,992,718 -> 2,277 (1,314x), 82 -> 15 ms.** Captured **0.95%**; the 15 ms residue is **0.21%, below the floor, deliberately not taken** — and the candidate's 1.16% is what justified building, said in both directions. No whole-rebuild A/B quoted (rounds 858/869 § 13). **PINS + ABLATION:** `ModuleSymbolScanIndexTest` (11), NINE single-mistake ablations; A1/A2 a deliberate PAIR because `SymbolFlags.Module` is the UNION of `ValueModule` and `NamespaceModule` and either half alone compiles; **A6 initially left every pin GREEN and an 11th pin was CUT to give it a failure of its own** (round 869's rule); one pin recorded as structurally UN-ABLATABLE and A5/A8 as one discriminated position, not two. **GATES: suite 14,139 -> 14,150 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, **8-profile grid added=0 removed=0 on all eight with BOTH arms rebuilt from source and diffed in both directions**. `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` §§ 14-20._

_**Round 914 (2026-08-17) — (API.3c): THE BATCH LANDS AND THE EMBEDDING API IS NOW USABLE BY AN EDITOR — N SPANS COST **ONE** BUILD, MEASURED AT **34x** FOR HOVER AND **62x** WHEN EACH CARET IS ALSO ASKED FOR ITS DEFINITION. THE ROUND'S TECHNICAL PRODUCT IS THAT THE QUEUE ENTRY'S *"it needs no new mechanism"* WAS TRUE OF THE CAPTURE AND **FALSE OF ITS KEY**._ `Project.semanticsAt(fileName, offsets)` is the primitive and `Project.fileSemantics(fileName)` the sweep expressed on it, both answering `SemanticInfo(start, end, kind, quickInfo, definitions)` — one per DISTINCT span, sorted `(start, end)`, so several carets in one identifier collapse to one entry and the result is neither indexed by nor the same length as the input; **the ordering is imposed by the API rather than inherited**, because the compiler's answer order is the order its walk reached the nodes. **THE CORRECTION, and the one place this item could have silently created a defect:** `TypeCaptureRequest`'s packed `(start, end)` key was left un-finalized with a KDoc note saying to fix it *"should a caller ever request spans in bulk"* — `fileSemantics` **is** that caller, and the collapse is round 889's in its purest form (`Long.hashCode` folds the pack onto `start xor end`, and a node's `end` is its `start` plus a token or two, so **>400 distinct spans hash to <40 distinct values**). Now through `packIdPair`; pinned by a MEASURING test with a raw-pack negative control, because no diagnostic, no emitted byte and no `cost_gate.py` counter can see a degenerate bucket distribution. Production pays nothing — the per-node hook returns on a null per-file key set BEFORE it packs. **THE CANDIDATE SET of a sweep is *every `Identifier`* and the argument is that the rule must fit in a sentence**; member names are IN (identifiers, and typed) while their DEFINITION stays refused, which is one span pinning both halves. **WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: re-expressing `quickInfoAt`/`definitionsAt` on the batch** — it would delete ~10 duplicated lines and make the EQUIVALENCE pin a tautology; they stay independent code so "the batch says span for span what the single-caret members say" compares two paths and two builds. **THE MEASUREMENT** (34-identifier in-memory fixture, warm, two agreeing draws): sweep **1 compile / 100-103 ms**; the same 34 carets via `quickInfoAt` **34 compiles / 3,373 ms**; each asked both ways **68 compiles / 6,209 ms**. **THE BUILD COUNTER IS A PER-PATH READ, AND THE FIRST VERSION WAS FLAKY**: counting ALL Vfs touches read 29 where six builds should be 30, once — some compiler cache warms across builds within one JVM and takes a source read with it — so the unit is reads of `tsconfig.json`, exactly 1 per build, established by a control pin. **GATES: suite 14,567 -> 14,593 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the 26 new pins** (22 `-project`, 4 core), XML-summed over all six modules; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (738 classes) and on the module explicitly (**12 classes against round 913's 11 — the gate SAW the new code**); `spine_closure_audit.py` 46 handlers all supersets; warning-clean. Next: member go-to-definition and `(API.4)` completions._

_**Round 918 (2026-08-18) — (API.4b): FREE-NAME COMPLETIONS LAND, AND THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS THAT THE TWO RULES THE ITEM TOLD ME TO COPY FROM `lexLevelHasName` ARE **BOTH WRONG FOR THIS CHAIN** — they belong to an ascent that has a SECOND, THREADED population to fall back on, and transplanted onto `spineScopeLookup`'s chain one of them deletes every FILE-LEVEL name and the other deletes every NAMESPACE member; both divergences are ABLATED rather than argued._ **IT LANDED BY DELETING ONE REFUSAL, exactly as (API.4a) designed**: `FREE_NAMES_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` is gone and no signature moved. The mechanism is a THIRD span list (`TypeCaptureRequest.scopeSpans` -> `CapturedScope` / `CapturedName(name, kind)`) unioned into `keysByFile` the way `memberSpans` is, so the per-node hot-path guard is byte-identical. **THE ONE STRUCTURAL NOVELTY: this is the first capture that admits a NON-`Expression` node** — a completion caret is BETWEEN nodes by construction, so the anchor is the innermost node ENCLOSING it, which for a caret on a blank line in a function body is the BODY BLOCK, and a function-like's immediate body SHARES its function's scope in the binder, so that is exactly the scope holding the parameters and locals. No EOF branch and no special case. **THE ENUMERATION IS `spineScopeLookup`'s OWN WALK RUN TO EXHAUSTION** — each level's `symbols` then its `existing`, innermost first, first sighting wins, then the merged/lib GLOBALS filtered through `globalsForFile` (INV.3(c)) — and that identity IS the correctness argument: **a name the list offers is a name `definitionsAt` resolves, and a name it hides is hidden because something nearer binds the spelling**, pinned by completing at a caret and then navigating from a use. **DIVERGENCE 1: `LexicalScope.existing` IS read**, against round 748's rule as quoted — that rule is about a RESOLVER whose soundness is that `symbols` excludes everything the main binder bound, and an enumeration reading `symbols` only offers **no file-level declaration and no import at all** (arm A5, 8 red). **DIVERGENCE 2: the UNTRUSTED-level skip is NOT applied** — it is sound in `lexLevelHasName` because a threaded population sits underneath it; here it answers NOTHING inside every namespace body (arm A3, 1 red uniquely its own). Cost stated rather than hidden: a namespace merged across files can offer a sibling declaration's non-exported member. **A FREE-NAME ITEM CARRIES NO `typeText`, and the deciding argument is CORRECTNESS not cost.** Measured at a caret in a real function body of the compiler profile: **1,628 items**, enumeration **0.39-0.64 ms**, typing every item **+2.6-14.3 ms** (noise against the query's own **5.3-8.9 s** warm rebuild) — but **618 of 1,629 = 37.9% would render `any`/`error`**, because a free name may name a TYPE, and decorating 38% of a list with a lie is worse than decorating none. (On a two-file toy project the COST argument bites the other way: 2,232 items, enumeration 0.55 ms, typing them all 26-170 ms = **20-75% of the whole 125-360 ms query**.) The field stays a non-null `String` at `""`; a host wanting one item's type asks `quickInfoAt`, which is `completionItem/resolve`'s shape. **KEYWORDS ARE REFUSED with a reason and a pin**: a useful list is context-sensitive and the anchor is token-level, so an unconditional one offers items that do not compile — the one thing the member half already refuses to do. **TWO IMPRECISIONS PINNED AS DECISIONS**: a `let` declared LATER in the same block IS offered (tsc does too — the binding exists, it is in its temporal dead zone), and a function's body locals are visible inside its own PARAMETER DEFAULTS (the binder's fn scope is flat). **PINS +22** (`-project` 191 -> 209, core 14,337 -> 14,341); the DISCRIMINATOR is (API.4a)'s inverted — a body local SHADOWING a name imported from ANOTHER FILE must appear ONCE and be the LOCAL, separated by `kind` — and the SHARP NEGATIVE is a SIBLING scope, which an enumeration built over the file's NODES passes every positive pin and fails alone. The core `ScopeCaptureMeasurementTest` is sharper than either measurement before it: **post-hoc there is nothing to fall through TO** — the ascent walks no levels and answers with the globals leg alone, so a parameter and a type parameter are simply GONE while the shadowed OUTER binding stands where the local should be, under the same name and the same `kind`. **SEVEN-ARM ABLATION, one mistake at a time, each dry-run for a real diff and restored from a sha256-verified snapshot rather than `git checkout`; all seven compiled and SIX reddened a DISTINCT set**: A1 scope leg never records -> **18 red**; A2 no shadowing dedup -> **1 red uniquely its own**; A3 untrusted-level rule applied -> **1 red uniquely its own**; A4 enumerate POST-HOC -> **14 red**, distinguished from A1 by the four pins it leaves GREEN (the globals leg and the rows whose subject survives on it) — the measurement made a second time; A5 `symbols` only -> **8 red**; A6 no globals leg -> **6 red**, uniquely reddening the post-hoc control; **A7 drop the writable-name filter -> 0 red, UNDISCRIMINATED and recorded in-file as such** rather than claimed (no non-identifier spelling reaches this fixture, so the filter guards a shape the test does not carry, and its sort and dedup assertions are pinned by no arm at all). **GATES: suite 14,662 -> 14,684 / 0 failures / 0 errors / 3 skipped = EXACTLY the +22**, XML-summed over all six modules and re-run on the byte-restored post-ablation tree; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** (a real gate — `Checker.kt` grew ~190 lines reachable from the hook on the hot walk — proven live by its own 46-error / 78-file compile); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` clean on core (**745 classes, 15,890 methods, 0 over**) and on `-project` explicitly (**21 classes, 216 methods**); `spine_closure_audit.py` 46 handlers all supersets; warning-clean. No wall A/B: production executes not one new instruction. **(API.4) is now complete in both halves**; what is left is keywords, contextual object-literal keys, and the fact that a completion is still a FULL REBUILD — 5.3-8.9 s warm, of which the enumeration is under a millisecond, so a host must debounce and the lever is the architecture inversion, not this API._

[K]: string }` now emits a spurious TS2322, because our member map is last-wins for EVERY duplicate spelling (measured at HEAD for a plain `p; p` and for `["p"]`) — the program is an ERROR program in tsc and we moved 0 -> 1 diagnostics of the wrong code; _**Round 939 (2026-08-19) — (CHK.6): THE COMPUTED-KEY FAMILY RE-JUDGED AGAINST *PRISTINE*. NO CODE, AND THE ROUND'S PRODUCT IS AN INSTRUMENT PLUS A VERDICT: **`tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc` IS THE ONLY REFERENCE THAT RUNS ON THIS BOX AND IT IS NOT THE ONE WE DIFF AGAINST — BUT THE PRISTINE ORACLE WAS ON DISK ALL ALONG, IN THE CORPUS'S OWN `tests/baselines/reference`, AND IT SAYS ROUNDS 933-938 LANDED NOTHING PRISTINE CONTRADICTS.**_ Round 938 found the two references parting on this family's own territory; rounds 933-937 had tsgo-only evidence for rows no corpus baseline covers, which is what this round was commissioned to settle. **THE INSTRUMENT IS `scripts/pristine_oracle.py`** — `--code` / `--pattern` / `--fixture`, every hit labelled **ACTIVE vs not-generated** (i.e. whether the suite already gates it), plus **`--extract DIR`**, which writes pristine's own input back out so our binary can be run over exactly what pristine tsc saw. Four non-obvious facts make it work: an ABSENT `.errors.txt` beside a present case means pristine was SILENT and is evidence; `tests/cases` in this clone is INCOMPLETE (6,537 files against 9,055 error baselines, whole conformance directories missing), so the search must go through the `.js`/`.errors.txt` baselines, which echo every input verbatim; the search shells out to `grep` (0.2 s over 53,049 files, where the pure-Python form timed out at 120 s); and the `.types`/`.symbols` baselines answer a NAMING question with no diagnostic at all — `computedPropertyNames10_ES6.types` records ``[`hello bye`]()`` as the member `"hello bye"` and a SUBSTITUTING template as `[x: string]`, which is round 933's landed rule and its negative control read straight off pristine. **THE CLASSIFICATION: 34 landed decisions — 22 PRISTINE-CONFIRMED, 10 CORPUS-SILENT, 1 tsgo-ONLY, 1 PRISTINE-DIVERGENT**, and the one divergence is a message FORM the round that landed it had already recorded: our TS2741 for a missing late-bound member says `'p'` where pristine says `'[K]'` — pristine names the key AS WRITTEN in every baseline that names one (`'[E.A]'`, `'["a"]'`, `'[c1]'`, `'[Symbol.toPrimitive]'`), so pristine and tsgo agree and we are the outlier; queued as (CHK.5)(f). **THE CORPUS PROTECTS FAR MORE OF THIS FAMILY THAN THE NOTES CLAIMED**: `dynamicNames`, `dynamicNamesErrors`, `duplicateIdentifierComputedName`, `destructuredLateBoundNameHasCorrectTypes`, `checkDestructuringShorthandAssigment2`, the three `duplicateObjectLiteralProperty_computedName*` and **7 of the 10 TS2717 baselines in the whole corpus** are ACTIVE byte-exact gates sitting on these exact decisions — `dynamicNames` alone covers the declaration side, the qualified `[N.c2]` route, the `implements` rows and the object-literal supply row, and it is the baseline that MOVED mid-round-937. **THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE IS A NEGATIVE**: our binary was run over **300 ungated pristine fixtures** (326 stems, 26 with no recoverable source) carrying a computed member key, differencing (line, code) against pristine's baseline — **277 of 300 emit nothing pristine does not, and of the 23 that do, four are the pre-existing divergences below and NOT ONE of the other nineteen is attributable to rounds 933-938**; where we differ we emit FEWER diagnostics (TS2464 / TS2564 / TS2699 / TS2804 / TS2411, checks this compiler does not implement) and where we do emit, the positions match exactly. **FOUR PRISTINE DIVERGENCES *WERE* FOUND AND ALL FOUR ARE OLDER THAN THE FAMILY, PROVED BY THE DIFF RATHER THAN ARGUED** (`git diff 0d38189f..HEAD` mentions `getPropertyName` / `getPropertyKeyName` / `evaluateComputedPropertyName` / `checkObjectLiteralDuplicates` / `PrivateIdentifier` **zero times**), queued as (CHK.7): TS1117 is SPELLING-keyed so a repeated DYNAMIC key is a false positive (`symbolProperty1`) — **and the corpus cannot see it because pristine's own negative-control fixture uses two DIFFERENT identifiers**; `computedSymbolKey`'s invented name meeting an unchecked key expression (`symbolProperty52`); a private-name duplicate reported at BOTH declarations where pristine reports the second (`privateNameDuplicateField`); and string/numeric member-name equivalence (`"1"` vs `1.0`) missing from both duplicate scans (`numericStringNamedPropertyEquivalence`). **METHOD NOTE PAID FOR TWICE: a FIXED scratch `tsconfig` manufactures false positives** — `uniqueSymbols` read an ours-only row at `target: es2015` that vanished at `esnext`, so every ours-only row was re-read at a second target before being believed. **GATES: named as NOT APPLICABLE rather than reported green** — no Kotlin changed, so nothing to compile, nothing for `cost_gate.py` to count, no method to grow past 8,000 bytecodes and no binary to grid; the suite stands at round 938's **15,168 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** with every compiled module byte-identical to `022cdd42`. What WAS exercised is the HEAD binary, **300 times**, against 300 pristine fixtures._

**AN ERROR-REPORTING QUERY IS 104-108 ms -> 25 ms — THE RE-ENTRANT REPLAY IS **2.25-2.30x**, NOT
THE "DECAYING 1.68x" FIVE ROUNDS RECORDED, AND IT IS NOW WIRED FOR DIAGNOSTICS BEHIND A TYPE-LEVEL
VALVE (2026-08-24, (INC.40)).** The lineage was not wrong about the floor shrinking; it was
measuring the wrong thing. **Every figure in it carried a whole-file `TypeCaptureRequest` in BOTH
arms** — the request `replay-differential.sh` needs in order to GRADE the mechanism — and that is
+9-17 ms per query of cost common to both arms, which **dilutes a ratio and leaves no trace in
it**. Measured that way at HEAD the same run still reads 1.34x; the diagnostics channel asks for no
capture at all, and it is exactly the channel the differential grades as EQUIVALENT. Re-priced in
**two independent JVMs**, warm, six warm-ups, leading draw discarded, ABBA-rotated, tsc's own 78
sources: at `k = 1` **10,656 / 10,783 ms fresh against 4,728 / 4,685 replay = 2.25 / 2.30x**, per
query **104 / 108 ms -> 25 / 25**; at `k = 2` 1.72 / 1.81x; at `k = 8` 1.26 / 1.25x. **The ratio
falls with the working set exactly as it must** — the thing the replay deletes is the floor, paid
once per QUERY and not per file — and **the replay arm's TOTAL lands on the whole-program CHECK
cost** (4,728 against ~4,935 ms), i.e. (INC.37)'s **1.39x re-derivation tax being COLLECTED rather
than re-paid**: 77 fresh checkers each re-derive the shared lib and foreign-declaration
resolutions, one live checker does not. Floor **54 ms**, cross-checked against
`partition-equivalence.sh`'s 61 ms at the same commit.
**IT SERVES DIAGNOSTICS AND NOTHING ELSE, AND THE REFUSAL IS A *TYPE* RATHER THAN A COMMENT.**
`replay-differential` at HEAD: **0 `DIVERGE-DIAG`, 0 `DIVERGE-DEF`** on both arms (46 rows over
tsc's sources, 178 over the 71 `partition-gate` files carrying them, 352,713 definition spans),
against **43 `DIVERGE-TYPE` of 75 files** — the pre-existing HEAD state, overwhelmingly the
union-alias display family (INC.26)/(INC.27) in which the FRESH arm is not automatically the right
one. So `Project.diagnosticsOf` holds the live program through `DiagnosticsOnlyRecheck`, a private
one-way valve whose single member takes a `Set<String>` and returns a `List<Diagnostic>`: **no
`TypeCaptureRequest` is expressible at that boundary and no `CapturedType` can leave it**, so the
caret channels cannot reach it even by mistake. The handle is dropped by `updateFile`,
`deleteFile` and `close`. Queued as **(INC.41)**: closing those 43 is what would let captures
through the same valve, and the prize for it is NOT measured.
**THE ABLATION'S ONE TRANSFERABLE ARM.** Four arms, one mistake each, each verified as a real diff
against the committed file — and in a3 (**the handle serves without widening, so it answers an
empty list**) **the build-COUNT pin stays GREEN**. A count-only suite would have shipped a
language service reporting no errors at all, at full speed, with every cost pin green; the value
pins are what redden. One pin is recorded as UNDISCRIMINATED rather than claimed as coverage.
**TWO INSTRUMENT TRAPS, BOTH FAILING TOWARD A PLAUSIBLE TABLE**: a **floor build is its own code
path**, so floor draws taken after whole-program warm-ups read 129/89/96 ms against a true 52-56 —
a 1.7x over-read that would have understated every derived ratio, now drawn at the END and

78/78, `partition-gate` sensitivity 75/75, `cost_gate.py` all counters in band (`mapped.hits`
